## Round 47 (2026-08-25) -- dark palette re-tuned from the radio mockup's colors

User confirmed Light skin works well and Bold text reads clearly; Dark
skin's text was also legible, but the overall look was "a bit too
dark" -- asked to try the colours from a separate Internet Radio dark
mockup (MediaPlayer3_radio_skin_test2.png) instead, also noting that
mockup's own icon set (wifi/globe/flag) could seed future Radio-screen
work -- noted but not acted on this round, staying within the
established Music-Library-only scope.

### Diagnosed why it read as "too dark" before guessing at new numbers

Sampled the new mockup's colours precisely (mode colour over small
clean patches, deliberately avoiding anti-aliased text-edge pixels
that skewed a first naive per-pixel sampling attempt) and compared
directly against round 46's own dark palette: found that palette's
header_inactive_bg (#17171D) was only ~3-5 RGB units away from its
own panel_background_color (#14181C) -- essentially indistinguishable
-- which is almost certainly why the whole screen read as flat/
uniformly dark despite every individual colour being intentional.
The new mockup's own header_inactive_bg (#252E3F) sits roughly 30
units lighter than its panel background, giving real visual hierarchy
between chrome and content instead of everything blending together.

### New dark palette, applied to both the image generator and the app's own SKIN_PALETTES

panel_background_color -> #151820, header_inactive_fg unchanged
(#F0F0F0, already had good contrast), info_label_fg -> #7B9FE0 (a
lighter, more legible blue than a plain reuse of the header's own
more muted/desaturated navy would give as body text), selected_row_bg
-> #2B2F39 (a muted dark gray-blue, replacing the previous brighter
blue -- matches the new mockup's own selected-row colour rather than
inventing one). Regenerated only the 6 dark-variant background images
(3 states x hd/sd); the 6 light-variant images were untouched, per
direct confirmation the light skin already looks right. Visually
inspected two of the six regenerated images (artists-active and
albums-active, both HD) before trusting the batch -- clear
improvement in panel/header/background separation versus the previous
version, confirmed directly rather than assumed from the colour
values alone.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: musiclibraryscreen.py).
AST-based undefined-name sweep clean. XML-comment double-hyphen sweep
clean. Skin rendered and XML-validated at four resolutions with the
config forced to "dark", confirming all three new hex values
(#151820, #7B9FE0, #2B2F39) appear correctly in the rendered skin
output and every referenced pixmap file exists on disk. Not yet
re-confirmed on a real device this round -- the actual on-screen
contrast and "does it still feel too dark" judgment is exactly the
kind of thing this environment cannot verify, only approximate via
static colour-difference reasoning.

## Round 49 (2026-08-25) -- MainScreen background image, first cautious step

User confirmed the icon set (folder/file icons drawn to match the
existing style, playlist icon already available) and the first-draft
static background chrome for MainScreen (structure only, validated
against the user's own mainscreen_light_test1.png mockup) both look
right, and asked to continue on that basis. Given MainScreen is
3712 lines -- far larger and more complex than MusicLibraryScreen ever
was, with three distinct display modes (radio/podcast/local music)
each formatting the same widgets differently -- deliberately did NOT
attempt a full redesign in one step. Instead followed the exact same
proven sequence that worked for MusicLibraryScreen: validate the
background-image mechanism on THIS screen first (its own rounds
36-38), full redesign only after that's confirmed (round 39).

### Measured the mockup

Same approach as MusicLibraryScreen's own reference images: real
colour-transition scans and mode-colour sampling (not estimated),
covering the top card (cover art + Now Playing + Artist/Album/Track
title + status), the progress bar row, and the two bottom cards
(Playlist, Information). Sampled outer_bg (#ECEDF3), panel_bg
(#F9F9FA -- notably close to MusicLibraryScreen's own #F9F9F9, though
arrived at independently), accent purple (#4E41E5, MainScreen's own
distinct brand colour vs. MusicLibraryScreen's blue), divider colour
(#DDDCE6), and the playlist selected-item colour (#DBD5F6). Built a
first-draft static-chrome background image (cards, icon placements,
divider lines -- no text baked in, learning directly from round 45's
own lesson about keeping text as real translatable widgets from the
start this time rather than needing to redo it later) and confirmed
directly with the user before investing in code changes.

### This round's actual code change: background image only, nothing repositioned

Added the "background" Pixmap widget and its own ePicLoad decode/
callback pair (_decodeBackgroundImage()/_onBackgroundImageDecoded()),
mirroring _decodeCoverArt()/_onCoverArtDecoded()'s own already-proven
pattern exactly (same widget-not-ready eTimer retry, same setPara()
shape) -- reusing a pattern proven twice now (MainScreen's own cover
art since Build 0005, MusicLibraryScreen's background since round 39)
rather than trusting a third untested variant. Needed its own separate
ePicLoad instance (self._background_picload) since self._picload is
already dedicated to cover art decoding concurrently.

Deliberately did NOT change DESIGN_WIDTH/DESIGN_HEIGHT (still 700x520)
or reposition any existing widget this round -- MainScreen's design
space has a very different aspect ratio (~1.35) than the new
background image's own native ~1.77 (1808x1024), and scaling the
background through the same sx/sy as every other widget would have
stretched it non-uniformly on any real 16:9 device. Solved this
without touching the 700x520 grid at all: the background widget's
own skin declaration uses the RAW real width/height directly (not
rect()), so ePicLoad's own aspect-preserving fit+center scaling
handles everything independently of what every other widget's own
coordinate system still assumes. This is the same self._screen_width/
_screen_height fix MusicLibraryScreen needed in round 40, applied
here from the start rather than needing its own round to discover.

Only light/hd exists so far, hardcoded rather than resolved through
MusicLibraryScreen's own SKIN_PALETTES/_resolveSkinVariant()/
_resolveResolutionTier() machinery -- porting that whole system here
is real follow-up work, not assumed to carry over unchanged from a
screen with a very different widget set and layout.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: mainscreen.py --
the whole 3782-line file, not just the changed section). AST-based
undefined-name sweep clean across the entire file. XML-comment
double-hyphen sweep clean (caught and fixed the same recurring
mistake once during this edit, before the final validation pass).
Skin rendered and XML-validated at four resolutions; confirmed the
background widget's own position/size exactly matches each real
resolution tested, and -- critically, to prove nothing existing moved
-- confirmed the "cover" widget's own position/size is byte-identical
to what it was before this round's change. Confirmed the background
image file exists on disk at the exact path the new code references.
Full-codebase translation sweep clean (no new strings introduced this
round). Confirmed no widget-name collision with the new "background"
identifier. Not yet verified on a real device -- whether MainScreen's
own background actually renders correctly (fills the real screen via
ePicLoad the same way MusicLibraryScreen's own did) is the natural
next check, exactly mirroring how MusicLibraryScreen's own rounds
36-38 needed real device confirmation before round 39's full redesign
could safely proceed.

### Scope note

This round is intentionally narrow: background image mechanism only,
still using the ORIGINAL pre-redesign widget layout for everything
else (cover, meta/media text, progress bar, playlist, information --
all unchanged). The full redesign (repositioning every widget to
match the new background precisely, splitting "meta" into separate
Artist/Album widgets matching the mockup's own distinct styling,
adding the dark variant, SD tier, and porting MusicLibraryScreen's
whole variant/tier resolution system) is substantial further work,
not started this round.

## Round 50 (2026-08-25) -- MainScreen: two background variants, header redesign, layout fixes

User confirmed round 49's background-image mechanism works (validated
in the exact same "prove the mechanism first" sequence
MusicLibraryScreen's own rounds 36-38 established), then gave detailed
follow-up feedback from real device screenshots -- the old "Now
Playing" text was overlapping messily with the pre-existing "> Player"
blue highlight bar, and asked for a proper two-state header treatment
matching MusicLibraryScreen's own approach, plus several layout fixes.

### Read the screenshots precisely before changing anything

The screenshots showed round 49's new background rendering correctly
underneath, but visually conflicting with the OLD player_title_bg_
active mechanism (a solid blue bar overlapping the new "Now Playing"
label baked into the background). This confirms the real fix isn't
just removing text from the image -- it's replacing the OLD hide()/
show() highlight mechanism entirely with the SAME background-image-
carries-the-colour approach MusicLibraryScreen already uses, which is
exactly what the user asked for by name ("kuten musiikkikirjastossa").

### Two new background variants, no baked-in text this time

Learned directly from round 45's own lesson (MusicLibraryScreen had
baked-in header text that couldn't translate, had to be redone as
real widgets) -- built these background images with ONLY icon+colour
header bars from the start, never baking in "Player"/"Playlist"/
"Information" text at all. Two variants: mainscreen_player_active.png
(top header blue, Information header light-blue/inactive) and
mainscreen_info_active.png (reversed) -- matching exactly what was
requested; Playlist's own header stays the light/inactive style in
both, since a dedicated "Playlist active" third variant wasn't asked
for this round. Headers use the same rounded-top-corners-only
treatment as MusicLibraryScreen's own round 48 fix. Outer background
darkened (#ECEDF3 -> #B7BBC9) for better panel contrast, per direct
request. Visually inspected both variants before trusting them.

### Layout: top card shortened, progress card grown

Fixed the reported overflow (track-position text spilling out below
the progress bar card's own bottom edge) by moving the boundary
between the top card and the progress card: top card's own bottom
edge raised from y=392 to y=335, progress card's own top correspondingly
raised, giving it roughly 130px of internal height (up from ~73px) --
comfortably fits both the bar row and the time/track-position row
inside its own bounds now.

### DESIGN_WIDTH/HEIGHT changed to match the background images

700x520 -> 1808x1024, the same reasoning as MusicLibraryScreen's own
round 39: since this round already touches nearly every widget's
position anyway (the whole header/card restructure), there was no
longer a reason to keep the old mismatched design space just to avoid
a unit-conversion step -- every new position below is taken directly
from the same pixel measurements used to build the two background
images.

### Removed the old highlight mechanism entirely

player_title_bg_normal/active, playlist_title_bg_normal/active,
info_title_bg_normal/active -- six widgets, both their skin
declarations and their _initialize()-time creation -- deleted
entirely, confirmed via a full-file grep that no reference to any of
them remains anywhere. _updatePanelHighlighting() (the method that
used to hide()/show() these pairs) now calls _decodeBackgroundImage()
instead; kept the existing "> " text-marker logic completely
unchanged, since nothing about it was asked to change.

### Caching -- caught before it became a real problem

_updatePanelHighlighting() runs from the 1-second refresh timer
(_updateDisplay(), not just on actual panel switches) -- realized
this BEFORE finalizing the decode logic, since round 49's own
unconditional-decode version would have re-run ePicLoad every single
second once wired into this call path. Added the same per-state
pixmap cache MusicLibraryScreen's own round 39 established
(self._background_pixmap_cache, keyed "player"/"info"), plus the
same stale-decode guard round 39 also needed (a superseded in-flight
decode, from switching panels again before the previous one finished,
never overwrites a newer already-correct background). Removed round
49's own now-redundant standalone decode call at the end of
_initialize() -- _updateDisplay() (called earlier in that same
method) already triggers the exact same decode via
_updatePanelHighlighting(), so the second call was always just a
harmless but pointless cache hit.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: mainscreen.py,
the whole file after every change, not just the touched sections).
AST-based undefined-name sweep clean across the entire file. XML-
comment double-hyphen sweep caught and fixed three instances this
round (same recurring mistake as every previous round that's hit
this), all fixed in the same pass before final validation. Skin
rendered and XML-validated at four resolutions; confirmed all six
removed widget names are genuinely absent from the rendered output
(not just deleted from _buildSkin() but never referenced anywhere
else either); confirmed every remaining widget's actual rendered
position at 1280x720 forms a coherent, non-overlapping layout by hand-
checking the relationships (cover art, meta, media, status, divider,
progress card contents, and both bottom-card headers/content all
land where the design intended, cross-checked against each other's
positions, not just individually plausible). Full-codebase translation
sweep clean. Not yet verified on a real device this round -- the
background-swap-on-panel-switch behaviour and the overall visual
result are the natural next check, same as every previous round in
this MainScreen redesign.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 53 (2026-08-26) -- MusicLibraryScreen: fixed real list-overflow bug and missing card gap

User confirmed MainScreen now looks right, then flagged two real
layout bugs in MusicLibraryScreen's own screenshot: the artist/album/
track lists appeared to spill past the columns card's own rounded
bottom edge, and there was no visible gap between the info card and
the hint-row card (unlike the visible gap between the columns and
info cards above them).

### Measured the actual mismatch directly, not from memory

Checked the real "artists" MenuList rect directly in the skin
(40, 138, 498, 518 -> bottom edge 138+518=656) against the background
image's own drawn card boundary (found by scanning the actual
rendered PNG for its real colour transition, not assumed from the
generation script's own remembered values: card ended at y=584). This
is a genuine 72px overflow -- the list widgets were always taller
than the card drawn behind them, not something introduced by a recent
change. Also confirmed via the same scan that the info and hint cards
shared literally the same boundary (both at y=850) with zero gap,
while columns/info had a real 16px gap -- matching the user's own
comparison exactly rather than needing to guess at what "the same
gap as the upper ones" should measure.

### Fix: card boundaries recalculated from the real content, not the other way around

Per the user's own explicit framing (extend the top box, shorten the
middle one correspondingly) -- the list widgets' own height was
treated as fixed/correct; the background image's card boundaries were
recalculated to match: columns card grown to y=674 (656 real list
bottom + 18px margin, matching this project's own established outer-
margin convention), info card's own top moved down to 690 (674 + the
same 16px gap used elsewhere) while its bottom stayed at 850
unchanged -- making it genuinely 90px shorter, not just relocated.
Hint card's own top moved down by 16px too (866, closing the previous
zero-gap issue), which required shifting its own content (icons in
the image, hint_text_* widgets in the skin) down by the same 16px to
stay correctly positioned within the new card bounds, rather than
just moving the card and leaving its content where it was.

### Info card content redistributed for its new, shorter height

The info card's own text widgets (info_title + 4 label/value pairs)
were positioned for the OLD 250px-tall card (600-850) with fairly
generous margins; simply leaving them at their old absolute Y values
against the NEW 160px-tall card (690-850) would have left the title
almost flush against the card's own top edge. Recalculated all nine
widgets' Y positions and reduced their font sizes slightly (30->28
title, 22->20 for the four label/value pairs) to fit comfortably
within the shorter card with sensible top/internal/bottom margins,
rather than mechanically preserving the old absolute positions.

Regenerated all 12 background images (both variants x both tiers x
3 states) from the updated generation script. Visually inspected one
light and one dark HD sample directly before trusting the full batch
-- confirmed three genuinely separate, evenly-gapped cards in both,
matching what was asked.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: musiclibraryscreen.py).
AST-based undefined-name sweep clean. XML-comment double-hyphen sweep
clean. Skin rendered and XML-validated across both variants x four
resolutions (8 combinations); confirmed every repositioned widget's
actual rendered position matches the new layout exactly (artists list
unchanged at 138-656; info_title/info_artist_label/info_year_value at
their new Y values; both endpoint hint-text widgets shifted to y=874).
Confirmed all 12 background pixmap files exist on disk. Full-codebase
translation sweep clean (no new strings this round -- purely a layout
fix). Not yet re-confirmed on a real device this round.

## Round 54 (2026-08-26) -- RadioBrowserScreen converted to the background-image system

User confirmed MusicLibraryScreen's round-53 fix looks good, then
asked to convert Internet Radio next, expecting it to be simpler
since it could reuse MusicLibraryScreen's own background images with
just the icons swapped.

### Icon extraction -- caught and fixed a real quality problem before shipping

Cropped wifi/globe/flag icons from the user's own radio mockup
(MediaPlayer3_radio_skin_test2.png), initially using the same colour-
distance-from-one-sample-point technique used for earlier icon sheets.
First attempt produced icons with an opaque background box left behind
(visible immediately in a device-realistic composite test) and a flag
icon that failed to recolour at all -- diagnosed as the single-sample-
point threshold being unreliable against this particular source (icon
brightness/background darkness weren't uniform enough for one
reference pixel to work). Switched to a luminance-based extraction
(bright pixel = icon, dark = background) instead, since the source
icons are white-on-navy specifically -- more robust than colour-
distance for this exact case. Verified by compositing all three
against both the light-active and dark-inactive header colours before
trusting them a second time, not just visually inspecting the icon
files in isolation.

### Background images: reused Music Library's layout exactly

Same card boundaries, header/hint styling, and colour palette as
MusicLibraryScreen's own (post-round-53) light/dark palettes -- a
second copy of the palette dict (RADIO_SKIN_PALETTES) rather than a
cross-module import, since the two screens don't otherwise depend on
each other. One real structural difference handled explicitly: this
screen's own hint row has 7 items (CH+/CH-: page jumping, unique to
RadioBrowserScreen), not 6 -- checked whether it would even fit in
the same space before assuming it would (it does, but only at a
smaller font/spacing than MusicLibraryScreen's own 6-item layout used:
20px font vs 24px, computed and confirmed via the same textbbox-based
measurement approach, landing at 1569 of ~1590px available). Generated
all 12 images (2 variants x 2 tiers x 3 states); visually inspected
one light and one dark HD sample before trusting the batch.

### Code changes -- reused every established pattern directly, no new mechanisms

DESIGN_WIDTH/HEIGHT -> 1672x941 (round 39's own reasoning). Full
_buildSkin() rewrite: background widget using raw width/height,
stations/language/region_title split into normal/active pairs (round
34/45's own pattern) using Bold font and MusicLibraryScreen's exact
header colours, list widgets repositioned to the same column x-splits,
a single "info" text block plus the pre-existing "warning" red-row
special case repositioned to fit the new (post-round-53-style) info
card, and 7 hint_text_* widgets replacing the old single "hint" Label.
_updateColumnHighlighting() rewritten to swap the background image
(via _decodeBackgroundImage()/_onBackgroundImageDecoded(), a direct
copy of MusicLibraryScreen's own per-state-cached, stale-decode-guarded
version, not a fresh reimplementation) and toggle the new normal/
active title pairs. Confirmed via grep that no reference to the old
removed widgets (*_title_bg_normal/active, the single "hint" and
"*_title" names) remains anywhere in the file.

### Found and fixed a real, unrelated infrastructure bug: po2mo.py had been lost

Adding the two new hint-text strings ("CH+/CH-: Page", "OK: Options" --
previously only part of one combined, already-translated string, now
needed as standalone entries since the hint row split into per-item
widgets) required recompiling the .mo files, but po2mo.py itself was
gone (lost to the same mid-session environment reset that also wiped
/home/claude/extracted earlier in this conversation). Rewrote it from
scratch as a minimal but correct .po -> .mo compiler. First version
had two real bugs, both caught before trusting the output: (1) the
empty-msgid header entry (carrying the charset declaration Python's
own gettext module reads at load time) was being discarded entirely,
which made gettext default to ASCII and crash on the first accented
character; (2) the escape-sequence handling used
`s.encode().decode("unicode_escape")`, which mangles genuine multi-
byte UTF-8 characters into mojibake (confirmed directly: "Esittäjät"
became "EsittÃ¤jÃ¤t") since unicode_escape is a Latin-1-based codec,
not a UTF-8-aware one. Fixed both, then verified comprehensively: not
just the two new strings, but all 226 msgid entries in the .po file,
checked programmatically against the compiled .mo output for any
remaining mojibake pattern -- none found.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: radiobrowserscreen.py,
musiclibraryscreen.py, mainscreen.py, config.py -- confirmed nothing
else broke while po2mo.py was being fixed). AST-based undefined-name
sweep clean. XML-comment double-hyphen sweep clean across all three
screen files. Skin rendered and XML-validated across both variants x
four resolutions (8 combinations); confirmed every widget's actual
position in the output, confirmed the old bg_normal/active/single-
hint/single-title widget names are genuinely absent. All 12
background image files confirmed to exist on disk at their expected
paths. Full-codebase translation sweep clean; all 226 .po entries
verified to decode correctly through the rebuilt compiler, not just
spot-checked. Not yet verified on a real device this round -- this
is the first full run of the newly-rewritten po2mo.py, so the .mo
files' real-world correctness on-device (beyond what Python's own
gettext module confirms here) is worth extra attention on the next
device test specifically.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 56 (2026-08-27) -- list background darkening (3 screens) + PodcastScreen converted

User confirmed BrowserScreen looks good, flagged from a real device

### List background darkening

Added a new "list_background_color" palette key (light: #EAEAEA,

list doesn't fully fill its card (empty rows, a short list) shows the
lighter card colour, while the list's own row area now shows the
slightly darker one -- giving real depth between "card" and "list
content" without needing to regenerate a single image.

### PodcastScreen icon extraction

Cropped headphones/checkmark/microphone icons from the user's own

other icons extracted so far: the source is a two-tone "badge" (dark
filled circle with a white checkmark inside), not a simple line icon.

circle's own edges captured a thin ring (the circle's own anti-
aliased edge) along with the checkmark -- initially unintended, but
inspected directly and kept deliberately: it reads as a clean
"circled checkmark" line icon, stylistically consistent with the
existing icon set (e.g. icon_info.png's own circled-i), rather than
attempting to strip the ring out and risk a messier result.

(unchanged -- still overlaps "status" by design, later widget-
creation order still makes it paint on top when shown). Highlighting
method rewritten to swap the background image and toggle the new
title pairs, mirroring the exact proven pattern.

### New feature: description display, sourced from data the provider already had

Checked podcast_providers/podcastindex/podcastindex_provider.py
before assuming this needed new API work: both feed (podcast) and
episode entries already include their own "description" field, simply
never displayed anywhere in the UI before this round. Added
_updateInfoDescription(), reading the currently focused column's
current selection (available/subscribed -> podcast description,
episodes -> episode description) and falling back to a translated
"Press EPG/INFO to search" hint whenever the focused list is empty,
the selection index is out of range, or the description itself is
blank -- covering the specific case named in the request (no podcasts
visible yet) as one instance of a more general "nothing to show" rule
rather than a special-cased check just for that one scenario.

## Round 57 (2026-08-29) -- removed ">" marker (real 2-line wrap bug), fixed episode description staleness, gold selected text everywhere

User confirmed PodcastScreen looks good, then reported three issues
from real device screenshots: the "> " marker in front of a selected
title was wrapping onto two lines (visible directly in the PodcastScreen
screenshot: "> Saatavilla olevat podcastit" split across two lines),
Podcasts' episode description stopped updating after the first
episode when scrolling within an already-loaded Episodes list, and
the dark-theme selected-row text colour (gold, established for
MusicLibraryScreen back in round 48) was never carried over to the
three screens converted afterwards.

### ">" marker removed from every screen that still had it

Checked all five screen files directly rather than assuming which
ones were affected: MusicLibraryScreen and RadioBrowserScreen already
had no marker logic at all (removed or never added in their own
earlier rounds), but BrowserScreen, PodcastScreen, and MainScreen
(all three of its panel titles) still did. Removed it from all three
-- the background image's own colour already distinguishes the active
column/panel, so the marker was pure redundancy that had become an
active bug once combined with this round's longer translated titles.
Updated MainScreen's own docstring, which had explicitly said (as of
round 50) that the marker was being kept on purpose -- now stale and
corrected to explain the round 57 removal instead.

### Episode description staleness -- real bug, root-caused before fixing

Traced precisely why only the first episode's description ever showed:
_onSelectionChanged() returns early whenever self._focus == "episodes"
(after restarting the codec-check debounce timer), and
_updateInfoDescription() (added in round 56) was only ever called from
_updateDisplay() -- which runs on major refreshes (subscribing,
searching, loading a new episode list) but NOT on simple up/down
movement within an already-loaded list. The first episode's
description appeared correctly because _updateDisplay() ran once when
that episode list was first populated; every subsequent move within
it called moveUp()/moveDown() -> _onSelectionChanged(), which never
reached the description update. Added a direct
_updateInfoDescription() call in both branches of
_onSelectionChanged() (episodes, and available/subscribed) rather
than only in _updateDisplay() -- for available/subscribed this also
means the podcast's own description now updates immediately on
selection, not only after _loadEpisodes()'s own eventual (and
asynchronous) _updateDisplay() call.

### Gold selected-row text extended to all three remaining background-image screens

RadioBrowserScreen, BrowserScreen, and PodcastScreen's list widgets
already had a foregroundColorSelected attribute, but it was wired to
panel_text_color -- correct for light (white/near-black text
regardless), but never distinctively gold for dark, unlike
MusicLibraryScreen's own round 48 fix. Added a "selected_row_fg" key
to all three screens' own palettes (light: #1A1A1A, confirmed
byte-for-byte identical to panel_text_color so light behaviour is
provably unchanged; dark: #C7AC4E, the exact same gold sampled from
the radio mockup back in round 48, not re-derived) and switched each
of their three list widgets' own foregroundColorSelected to read from
it.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: all five
touched files). AST-based undefined-name sweep clean across all five.
XML-comment double-hyphen sweep clean. All four background-image
screens' skins rendered and XML-validated across both variants x four
resolutions each (32 combinations total in one pass); additionally
confirmed the exact gold hex value (#C7AC4E) is present in each
screen's own rendered dark-variant output, not just assumed from the
palette edit. MainScreen's own skin re-validated at four resolutions
after its marker removal (structure otherwise unaffected). Full-
codebase translation sweep clean (this round only removed text,
added none). Not yet re-confirmed on a real device this round -- in
particular, whether the episode-description fix behaves correctly
during the brief window while _loadEpisodes() is still fetching
asynchronously is worth a specific look next test.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 59 (2026-08-29) -- SettingsScreen converted (final screen, single-list layout)

User confirmed PlaylistScreen looks and works right, then asked for

line in the info area.

### Recognized a real structural simplification before building anything

Checked this screen's own navigation model first rather than assuming
it needed the same multi-panel machinery as every other conversion:
SettingsScreen has exactly one always-focused list (no Playlists/
Tracks-style panel switching), so there was no need for MusicLibrary-
Screen's own per-state background swapping, normal/active header
pairs, or a focus-tracking cache at all. Built a genuinely simpler

multi-state pattern most other screens ended up needing.

A gear icon (drawn with PIL: 8 small circular teeth around a ringed

in the whole hint row by a wide margin; needed font 21 with tighter
spacing (not the more generous 24 PlaylistScreen's own less-crowded
5-item row could use) to fit 1471 of ~1590px available.

### New feature: per-setting description text

Checked getConfigListEntry()'s own return shape and confirmed

string used when each entry was built -- used this directly as a
dict key for a description shown in the new "info" widget, one line
per setting (all 30 settings covered), rather than inventing a
separate lookup structure. One entry ("Use ExtEplayer3 for radio")
has a dynamic "(installed)"/"(NOT installed)" suffix appended at
build time, so it's matched by prefix rather than exact equality.
Overrode selectionChanged() (ConfigListScreen's own hook, called on
every selection move) to refresh the description -- checked this was
the right hook rather than needing custom up/down handlers, since
ConfigListScreen's own built-in ActionMap already handles navigation.

### Code changes

DESIGN_WIDTH/HEIGHT -> 1672x941. Full _buildSkin() rewrite: background
widget, a single header_title widget (no normal/active pair needed),
a single wide "config" list using the now-standard list_background_

request from Round 23.

### Scope note

All seven of the plugin's screens now use a background-image-based
skin: MusicLibraryScreen, RadioBrowserScreen, BrowserScreen,
PodcastScreen, PlaylistScreen, and SettingsScreen share the same
multi-file variant/tier system; MainScreen uses its own two-variant
version built specifically for its player-focused layout. This closes
out the background-image redesign project that began with
MusicLibraryScreen back around round 32.

## Round 60 (2026-08-30) -- fixed a real crash: skin_manager import mistakenly dropped in round 59

User reported the dark theme "flashes then goes black" and the app
crashes when trying to change a setting, with a device log attached.

### Root-caused directly from the log, not guessed

The log's own traceback was unambiguous:

    File ".../settingsscreen.py", line 909, in _afterChange
    if cfg.appearance.skin.value != skin_manager.getSkinName():
    NameError: name 'skin_manager' is not defined

Sequence from the log: user changed the Language setting via OK ->
ChoiceBox, _afterChange() ran to apply it immediately (Build 0006's
own "apply language/skin/theme changes without a restart" behaviour),
crashed on the very first line touching skin_manager, and Enigma2's
own process was killed shortly after. This is almost certainly the
same event the user described as "flashes then goes black" -- not a
separate rendering bug, just their own description of watching the
crash sequence play out (dialog opens, then the whole process dies,
leaving a black screen).

### The actual bug: round 59's own import cleanup went too far

Round 59 replaced settingsscreen.py's own `from .skin import
(PANEL_BACKGROUND_COLOR, PANEL_TEXT_COLOR, skin_manager, to_opaque_
skin_color)` with a slimmed-down `from .skin import to_opaque_skin_
color`, on the reasoning that the new palette system replaced every
use of the old skin_manager-based colours. That reasoning held for
_buildSkin() itself, but missed that _afterChange() (a completely
different method, checked separately and correctly by every earlier
round's own grep sweep for other files) still legitimately calls
skin_manager.getSkinName()/loadSkin()/getThemeName()/loadTheme()/
setCustomColor() -- these drive the SEPARATE, still-functional
cfg.appearance.theme mechanism (Gray/Light/Dark/Custom), distinct
from this round's own cfg.appearance.skin (Light/Dark) system. Fixed
by restoring skin_manager to the import -- the simplest, most
conservative fix: this existing logic was correct and working before
round 59, the only actual problem was the import removal itself.

### Why this specific bug slipped through round 59's own testing

Traced the gap precisely: every AST-based undefined-name sweep run
across rounds 54-59 filtered candidate names with `if node.id[0:1].
isupper() or node.id == "config_manager"` -- a deliberate shortcut
from early in this series (most real cross-file dependencies, like
class names and constants, start with a capital letter in this
codebase's own convention) that never got revisited as the pattern
matured. `skin_manager` starts with a lowercase 's' and isn't
`config_manager`, so it was silently excluded from every single one
of those checks, in every round, for every file -- not just this one.
Confirmed by re-running a corrected, fully case-insensitive version
of the same check (every Name node, checked against every import and
every actual definition -- classes, functions, parameters, local
assignments, for-loop and with-statement targets, and except-clause
names, not just a same-file heuristic) across all seven converted
files: settingsscreen.py was the only one with an actual problem.
The other six passed cleanly, confirming this was a one-off
oversight in round 59's own edit rather than a systemic issue
present since round 54.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: settingsscreen.py).
Directly confirmed via a targeted AST walk that _afterChange()'s own
skin_manager references now resolve against the restored import,
rather than only trusting the general sweep's silence. Re-ran the
corrected, case-insensitive undefined-name sweep across all seven
converted screens -- zero remaining issues anywhere. XML-comment
double-hyphen sweep clean. Full-codebase translation sweep clean (no
text changed this round). Going forward, undefined-name checks in
this project should use the fully case-insensitive version confirmed
here, not the capital-letter shortcut that let this through.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 61 (2026-08-30) -- fixed SettingsScreen's own list rendering, using log evidence again

User confirmed round 60's crash fix worked (no crash this time,
Light theme could be selected via keyboard navigation, which
highlighted correctly), but reported the list still visually
misbehaves: dark text on light background flashes briefly when the
screen opens, then disappears or turns white. Attached a fresh device
log covering the same screen-open sequence.

### Found a concrete, named culprit directly in the log

    [Skin] Attribute 'scrollbarBackgroundColor' (with value of
    '#E0E0E0') in object of type 'eListbox' is not implemented!

This is Enigma2's own ConfigList (built on eListbox) explicitly
rejecting an attribute at skin-parse time. Compared the current
"config" widget definition against the very first version of this
file (from before any of this project's own changes touched it) and
found two attributes present now that were never there originally:
scrollbarBackgroundColor (confirmed unimplemented, per the log
above) and selectionDisabled="0" (never used on any of the other six
converted screens' own list widgets either, and not something this
round's work required -- an unnecessary addition). The original,
working version only ever set backgroundColor, foregroundColor, and
scrollbarMode.

### Fix: removed both, kept only the proven-safe attributes

Rather than guessing at a deeper ConfigList-specific colour mechanism
without real device access to verify one, took the conservative path
that the log evidence actually supports: the two new attributes this
round introduced are the most likely regression, since the original
version (same backgroundColor/foregroundColor approach, just with
different colour values) worked without complaint across many earlier
rounds. Reverted the config widget to exactly that proven shape, only
keeping this round's own colour VALUES (list_background_color /
panel_text_color from the new palette), not the two newly-added
structural attributes.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: settingsscreen.py).
Skin rendered and XML-validated across both variants x four
resolutions (8 combinations); explicitly confirmed both suspect
attributes (scrollbarBackgroundColor, selectionDisabled) are absent
from the rendered output, and that the "config" widget's own final
attribute set matches the original working definition's shape exactly
(three attributes: backgroundColor, foregroundColor, scrollbarMode).
Re-ran the corrected, fully case-insensitive undefined-name sweep
(established in round 60) -- clean. XML-comment double-hyphen sweep
clean. Noted but did not act on an unrelated, pre-existing "Skin is
missing element 'HelpWindow'" warning also visible in the log -- not
referenced anywhere in this codebase, a generic Enigma2
HelpableActionMap warning unconnected to the reported rendering
issue, and not something introduced by any of this project's own
changes.

This is a best-evidenced fix based on the log's own explicit warning,
not something verifiable end-to-end from this environment -- worth
confirming directly on the next device test whether the list now
renders and stays legible throughout, rather than flashing then
fading.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 62 (2026-08-30) -- fixed a real concurrent-decode race; Settings issue needs a diagnostic build

User confirmed round 60's crash fix held (no crash), but round 61's
scrollbarBackgroundColor/selectionDisabled removal did NOT fix the
reported "list flashes then fades" symptom on SettingsScreen -- it
persisted. Provided a fresh device log plus a screenshot.

### Found and fixed a second, real, confirmed bug from the log

    <273957.968863> [ePicLoad] decode picture... .../mainscreen_player_active.png
    <273958.050830> [ePicLoad] thread running
    <273958.051117> [MainScreen] Unable to decode background image
    .../mainscreen_player_active.png: startDecode() reported failure

Two decode attempts for the SAME image, roughly 80ms apart -- the
second one failed outright because ePicLoad only supports one decode
per instance at a time and the first was still running. Traced this
to a real gap in round 50's own caching logic: it only skips
re-decoding a state that's ALREADY cached, but does nothing to stop a
SECOND decode of a state that's still IN FLIGHT (not yet cached).
Given MainScreen's own _decodeBackgroundImage() is called every
second from the UI refresh timer as well as on every panel switch,
any decode slow enough to still be running one second later (very
plausible here, compounded by the same log's own "gAccel alloc
failed"/"accelAlloc failed" messages suggesting the graphics system
was under some strain right after the live-TV-to-app handover this
log also shows) will race with the next timer tick.

Added an explicit `self._background_decode_in_progress` boolean
guard: set True immediately before calling startDecode(), checked at
the top of _decodeBackgroundImage() (skip silently if already
running), and cleared in a try/finally in _onBackgroundImageDecoded()
so every path (success, mismatched stale state, or an exception)
reliably clears it once the decode has genuinely finished. Applied
identically to all six screens using this per-state background
pattern (MainScreen plus the five reusing MusicLibraryScreen's own
approach) -- MainScreen's own periodic 1-second retrigger is the
clearest, confirmed risk, but the other five could hit the same race
under fast enough navigation or a slow enough decode, so treated this
as a shared defensive fix rather than a MainScreen-only patch.
SettingsScreen wasn't touched here since it decodes once, with no
periodic recall -- not exposed to this specific race.

One real slip caught before shipping: the first attempt at adding the
new `_background_decode_in_progress = False` initialization line
dropped its own leading indentation during a scripted text
replacement, breaking all five of the batch-edited files with an
IndentationError. Caught immediately by the routine post-edit compile
check (not by a human catching it first) and fixed directly.

### The Settings-specific symptom itself is still unresolved

Reconsidered the "gAccel alloc failed" warning that both the user and
this round's own investigation initially suspected: it appears
identically for MainScreen's own background decode too (same log),
and MainScreen has been reported working correctly in later rounds --
strongly suggesting this warning is a harmless, hardware/driver-level
fallback (likely to software rendering) rather than the actual cause
of a VISIBLE difference specific to SettingsScreen. Since round 61's
targeted fix (removing the two unverified skin attributes) did NOT
resolve the reported symptom, that rules out the two attributes as
the (sole) cause too.

The one thing genuinely unique to SettingsScreen among all seven
converted screens: it's the only one using ConfigList (via
ConfigListScreen), not MenuList -- and the device log's own
"Attribute ... in object of type 'eListbox' is not implemented!"
warning already showed this widget class has real, confirmed
differences in which skin attributes it honours compared to MenuList.
Proposed a direct diagnostic to the user rather than guessing again
blindly: temporarily disabled the background-image decode for
SettingsScreen only (commented out the _decodeBackgroundImage() call
in __init__, leaving the "background" Pixmap widget created but never
populated, so the screen falls back to its own plain backgroundColor
with nothing painted over it -- matching how this screen looked
before this whole project). User agreed to test this. If the list
renders and stays legible with no background image, that confirms a
ConfigList-plus-background-image interaction specific to this widget
class; if the symptom persists even without any background image,
the cause lies elsewhere entirely and the background image can be
ruled out. Clearly marked as a temporary diagnostic build in both the
code comment and this delivery -- not a final fix, and
_decodeBackgroundImage() should be re-enabled once the cause is
confirmed.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: all seven
converted screens, after finding and fixing the indentation
regression above). AST-based undefined-name sweep clean (case-
insensitive version, per round 60). XML-comment double-hyphen sweep
clean. Confirmed via grep that all five pieces of the new guard
(init, check, set-True, clear-on-exception, clear-in-finally) are
present in each of the six screens using the per-state pattern.
Full-codebase translation sweep clean (no new text this round).

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23. This build includes BOTH the confirmed
concurrent-decode fix (all six affected screens) AND the temporary
Settings diagnostic (background disabled) -- the next round should
re-enable Settings' own background image once the user's test result
comes back, rather than shipping the diagnostic state long-term.

## Round 63 (2026-08-30) -- root cause confirmed and fixed: widget insertion order

User ran round 62's diagnostic build (background image disabled for
SettingsScreen) and confirmed the list rendered correctly -- selection
highlighting, text contrast, everything worked as expected with no
background image. Also gave the key clue in their own words: "kuva
latautui tekstin päälle ja kuulsi vähän läpi" (the image loaded on
top of the text and showed through a bit), directly describing a
z-order/paint-order problem, not a colour problem.

### Found the real cause: this project's own Round 7 paint-order rule was violated

Checked the actual insertion order in __init__ and found it exactly
backwards: `ConfigListScreen.__init__(self, self.list, session=session)`
ran BEFORE `self["background"] = Pixmap()`. ConfigListScreen's own
__init__ creates and inserts "config" into self internally as part of
its own setup -- meaning "config" was being inserted into self BEFORE
"background", the opposite of every other converted screen (where
background is always the first widget created, per the project's own
established convention going back to round 7: earlier self[name]=...
insertion paints underneath, later insertions paint on top). With the
order backwards, the background image -- once its own async decode
completed -- painted ON TOP of the already-rendered list text, not
underneath it, exactly matching the reported symptom and the user's
own description of watching it happen.

This had been present since round 59's own initial conversion; round
61's attribute fix and round 62's decode-race fix were both real,
worthwhile fixes in their own right, but neither touched the actual
cause here.

### Fix

Moved the "background" widget creation (and the ePicLoad instance +
its callback connection, which logically belongs with it) to run
BEFORE ConfigListScreen.__init__(), restoring the correct order:
background first, config second -- matching the convention every
other screen already followed correctly. Confirmed Screen.__init__()
had already run earlier in the same method, so creating self[
"background"] at this new, earlier point is still safe. Re-enabled
_decodeBackgroundImage() (disabled as round 62's own diagnostic),
now that the actual fix is in place rather than leaving the
diagnostic's workaround as the permanent state.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: settingsscreen.py).
Skin rendered and XML-validated across both variants x four
resolutions (8 combinations) -- structurally unaffected by this fix
(a widget-creation order change in Python, not a skin XML change), but
re-verified anyway rather than assuming. XML-comment double-hyphen
sweep clean. Case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean.
Confirmed via direct inspection that Screen.__init__() precedes the
relocated background-widget creation, so no ordering hazard was
introduced by moving it earlier. Not yet re-confirmed on a real
device this round -- the next test should show the Settings list
rendering correctly WITH its own background image now visible behind
it, not just with it disabled.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23. This closes out the SettingsScreen rendering
issue first reported in round 60's own crash report, worked through
across four rounds (60: crash fix, 61: attribute fix, 62: decode-race
fix + diagnostic, 63: actual root cause and fix).

User confirmed round 63's fix resolved SettingsScreen's own rendering
issue completely, then asked for two more things: MainScreen was
still missing its own dark theme (light-only since round 49), and
MainMenu could use the same visual treatment as SettingsScreen.

(#1E2334/#036DFA), never routed through a palette. _buildSkin() was

(MainMenu has no info card taking up middle space like Settings

DESIGN_WIDTH/HEIGHT -> 1672x941. Rewrote _buildSkin() (background
widget, single "title" header text, wide "menu" list using the new
palette's selected-row colours, two hint_text_* widgets replacing the
old single combined "hint" Label) and _initialize() (background
Pixmap + ePicLoad instance, decode trigger). Checked skin_manager's
own usage via grep before removing its import (still used for
background_color in _buildSkin()) and restored it immediately when
the check caught it -- same round-60 discipline applied proactively
this time rather than needing a follow-up crash report to catch it.

## Round 65 (2026-08-31) -- system language auto-detection, bitbake recipe, version bump to 1.0.0-beta2

User confirmed the full skin redesign now looks right everywhere, then
asked for three things: MediaPlayer3 should default to the receiver's
own system language instead of always starting in Finnish, the
attached bitbake recipe should be added to the project (with any
necessary corrections), and the version should bump to reflect how
much has changed since 1.0.0-beta.

### System language detection

Checked the existing language-selection flow first rather than
assuming: cfg.general.language (config.py) had a hardcoded default=
"fi", read once at plugin startup (plugin.py's own
localization_manager.setLanguage(config_manager.get("general.
language", "fi")) call) and re-appliable live from Settings. Added
compatibility.getSystemLanguage() (a new method, matching
getDesktopSize()'s own established lazy-import-inside-try/except
pattern for anything that touches enigma/Components.config directly)
that reads Enigma2's own config.osd.language.value (a locale-style
string like "fi_FI") and returns just the leading 2-letter code,
falling back to "en" if anything about that lookup fails -- confirmed
this fallback path directly by simulating the "Components.config
unavailable" case this sandbox itself hits (no real Enigma2 present
here).

config.py's own cfg.general.language default is now computed by a new
_defaultLanguageCode() helper: calls compatibility.getSystemLanguage(),
and returns it only if it's one of MediaPlayer3's own two shipped
languages ("en"/"fi") -- any other system language (checked directly:
"de", "sv", "fr", and an empty string all correctly fall through)
falls back to "en" (LocalizationManager's own established fallback
language), not to the old "fi" default, since "fi" was never a
deliberate safe-fallback choice, just the developer's own original
default. Checked for a circular import before adding `from
.compatibility import compatibility` to config.py's own top-level
imports: compatibility.py already imports .config, but only lazily
inside hasDeveloperMode()'s own function body, never at module level
-- confirmed this directly via an AST walk restricted to only
module-level statements, not just grepping for the import anywhere in
the file.

One real limitation, stated plainly rather than glossed over: this is
a config DEFAULT, evaluated once when a fresh cfg.general.language
entry is first created. It correctly makes new installations start in
the receiver's own system language, and correctly survives a config
reset, but it does NOT retroactively change an EXISTING installation's
already-saved language choice (including one that was never
explicitly chosen, just inherited from the old hardcoded "fi"
default) -- ConfigSelection has no concept of "was this ever
explicitly set by the user" to distinguish those two cases, and
building that distinction would be a much larger, riskier change than
what was actually asked for.

### Bitbake recipe added, with one real bug fixed

Added the user's own attached mediaplayer3.bb to the project root.
Checked it against the actual repository contents rather than adding
it unmodified: LIC_FILES_CHKSUM declared an MD5 (0fca51bc5e55e5b8ac32
567bbd288c13) that didn't match the project's own actual LICENSE file
(1ebbd3e34237af26da5dc08a4e440464, verified directly) -- this would
have failed bitbake's own license-checksum QA check on any real
build. Corrected it to the real, verified checksum. Left the rest of
the recipe as given: HOMEPAGE/BUGTRACKER/AUTHOR are the author's own
information, not something to second-guess; SRC_URI fetches from the
author's own GitHub repo via git, which this environment has no way
to inspect, so the S = "${WORKDIR}/git" and do_install()'s own
"${S}/src/*" path assumption was left untouched (plausible it matches
the actual repo layout, but not something to verify from here); and
the older `RDEPENDS_${PN}`-style (underscore) override syntax was
left as-is rather than converted to the newer `:`-style, since this
project's own target build systems (OpenViX/OpenATV feeds) commonly
still use the older bitbake convention and changing it without
knowing which one is actually in use risked breaking compatibility
rather than fixing anything.

### Version bump to 1.0.0-beta2

Found the single source of truth first (VERSION in project.py,
read by version.py's own get_version_string() -- confirmed no other
file duplicates the literal string) and bumped it there only,
avoiding a search-and-replace across files that don't actually
need touching. Updated the bitbake recipe's own PV to match exactly.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: compatibility.py,
config.py, project.py). Case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep
clean for both touched Python files. XML-comment double-hyphen sweep
clean (both files, though neither touches skin XML this round).
Directly tested _defaultLanguageCode()'s own logic against six system-
language scenarios (fi, en, de, sv, fr, empty string) -- all matched
the expected outcome. Directly tested getSystemLanguage()'s own
exception-handling path by triggering the actual ModuleNotFoundError
this sandbox produces for Components.config -- confirmed it degrades
to the fallback correctly rather than raising. Confirmed via direct
byte-for-byte comparison that the corrected LIC_FILES_CHKSUM matches
the real LICENSE file's own MD5. Full-codebase translation sweep
clean (no new user-facing strings this round). Not yet verified on a
real device this round -- in particular, whether config.osd.language.
value's own actual format matches the assumed "xx_XX" locale-style
pattern on the user's own receiver hardware is worth confirming on
the next device test.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23 -- the .zip now also includes mediaplayer3.bb
at the project root. Version string in both packages' own metadata
(the .ipk control file's own Version: field, and the app's own
internal VERSION) now reads 1.0.0-beta2.

## Round 66 (2026-08-31) -- "System" language option, documentation update

User confirmed all four fresh device logs (OpenViX, OpenPLI, OpenATV,
OpenBH) passed clean -- checked directly rather than taking this on
faith: no tracebacks or crashes in any of the four, only the already-
characterized harmless "gSurface accelAlloc failed" warning in one.
Asked for two more things: a proper "System" choice for the language
setting (round 65's own fix only computed a one-time install default,
not a live-following choice), and a documentation update given how
much has changed.

### "System" language option -- a real improvement over round 65's own approach

Round 65's _defaultLanguageCode() only ran once, at config-creation
time -- correct for a fresh install, but it couldn't reflect a later
change to the receiver's own OSD language, and offered no visible way
for the user to see or choose "follow the system" as an ongoing
mode. Replaced it with resolveLanguageCode(configured_value): "fi"/
"en" pass through unchanged, but the new literal value "system" is
resolved fresh via compatibility.getSystemLanguage() every time it's
actually needed, not baked into a default once. Added "system" as
cfg.general.language's own new default choice (label "Järjestelmä",
matching this project's own convention of leaving skin/theme/language
choice labels untranslated for findability, per round 31's own
precedent for the "Language" entry's own label).

Found and fixed three call sites that would have mishandled the new
raw value if left untouched -- checked each directly rather than
assuming only the obvious one needed updating:
- plugin.py's own startup call passed cfg's raw value straight to
  localization_manager.setLanguage(), which only understands actual
  2-letter codes -- "system" would have silently behaved as an
  invalid language (falling back to English) instead of resolving
  correctly.
- settingsscreen.py's own live-apply comparison
  (cfg.general.language.value != localization_manager.getLanguage())
  would never equal "system", so every settings-apply cycle would
  have called setLanguage("system") unconditionally -- same
  underlying problem, worse because it would fire repeatedly rather
  than just once at startup.
- radiobrowserscreen.py's own _promoteAppLanguage() used the raw
  config value as a dict key to find RadioBrowser's own name for the
  app's current language (to move it near the top of the Language
  filter panel) -- "system" isn't a key in that dict, so this would
  have silently degraded to a no-op (not a crash, but a real feature
  regression) for exactly the choice most users would likely pick.

All three now call resolveLanguageCode() first. Verified the
resolution logic directly against six scenarios (fi, en, de, sv, fr,
empty string) and confirmed a circular-import check before adding
`from .compatibility import compatibility` to config.py's own
top-level imports (same AST-walk-restricted-to-module-level technique
established in round 65, re-run rather than assumed still valid).

### Documentation

Given the sheer number of docs (49 files under docs/) and the scale
of what's changed since 1.0.0-beta, prioritized the highest-value,
most load-bearing updates rather than attempting a full technical
rewrite of all eight *_SCREEN_SPEC.md files in one pass (each would
need its entire skin/widget documentation re-derived from the new
background-image system, a genuinely separate undertaking):

- README.md: version bump, feature list updated to describe the new
  shared background-image skin system instead of the stale "three-
  column browsing model" phrasing, Configuration section mentions
  the new System language option.
- CHANGELOG.md: added a second "BUILD 0010" closing note (the first
  one was written at round 19, well before the skin redesign began)
  covering rounds 20-66 in the same closing-note prose style
  established for every previous build, and updated the Build History
  table's own Build 0010 row to reflect the full scope, not just the
  original three-column/File-Browser work.
- HISTORY.md: found its own Project Timeline stopped at Build 0009
  entirely (no Build 0010 entry existed yet) and added one, matching
  every earlier entry's own architectural-narrative style -- covering

## Round 67 (2026-08-31) -- real bug found: EPG fetches on every 1-second refresh, no caching at all

User reported that on Vu+ Duo2, some radio stations cause stuttering
(the OpenViX busy-spinner icon appears, remote control lags by
roughly 20 seconds), while local music plays fine. Suspected WiFi, or
asked whether ServiceApp's own ExtEplayer3/GstPlayer settings might
help (a screenshot of that settings screen was attached). Provided a
fresh device log from the same session.

### Root-caused directly from the log, not guessed

Scanned the log for gaps in its own once-per-second "[MainScreen] UI
refresh" debug line -- a direct signal of the main GUI thread
stalling, since that timer should fire every second like clockwork.
Found repeated 5-7 second gaps, starting almost exactly when radio
playback began and continuing for as long as it kept playing.
Correlating those gaps against surrounding log lines showed them
landing right on "[EPG] Schedule for station <uuid>: 78 entr(y/ies)."
-- and that same line appeared dozens of times within a single
minute, sometimes twice in under a second, which is not just
"frequent," it means the same station's own EPG data was being
re-fetched far more often than the display could possibly need it.

Traced this to information_panel.py's own refresh() method, called
once per second (matching MainScreen's own UI refresh timer) for as
long as something plays, which unconditionally calls epg_manager.
getSchedule()/getNowPlaying() for whatever station is active --
documented in refresh()'s own docstring as safe to run
unconditionally because it's "cheap enough... no I/O of its own".
That assumption held for every other page type, but not for
Finland Radio EPG: checked both providers' own getSchedule()/
getNowPlaying() implementations directly and found neither had ANY
caching at all -- every single call did a real urllib.request.
urlopen() to an external service (Yle Teksti-TV / Bauer's own
rayo.fi page), synchronously, on the main thread. This means
MediaPlayer3 was making a fresh network request to an external
service roughly once per second, blocking the GUI each time, for as
long as an EPG-matched Yle or Bauer station kept playing.

This explains every part of the reported symptom precisely: it only
happens on certain stations (specifically the ones matched by
finland_radio_epg_registry, not "radio" in general), never on local
music (which never touches any of this EPG code at all), the OpenViX
busy-spinner is Enigma2's own standard indicator for a blocked main
thread, and the user's own much longer ~20s lag (vs. this log's own
5-7s gaps) is consistent with the exact same bug under slower/more
congested network conditions -- each once-per-second request taking
longer to complete, compounding the stall. Answered the user's own
direct question plainly: no, ServiceApp's own ExtEplayer3/GstPlayer
selection can't help here, since the bug is entirely inside
MediaPlayer3's own Python code and has nothing to do with which
underlying playback backend decodes the audio stream itself.

### Fix: TTL-based caching in both providers, at the network-fetch level specifically

Added a cache to each provider's own _fetchPage() (the actual network
call), not to getSchedule()/getNowPlaying() themselves -- the parsing
step downstream of a cache hit still re-runs every time, but that's a
cheap, local, CPU-only operation, matching what the original "no I/O
of its own" assumption was actually correct about. YleTeletextScheduleProvider
gets a 5-minute TTL (a full day's schedule doesn't meaningfully change
faster than that); BauerNowPlayingProvider gets a shorter 20-second
TTL (current track/artist info is more dynamic, but still doesn't
need per-second freshness). Both cache dicts are per-instance (one
provider instance per registered station, per finland_radio_epg_
registry's own existing design), so this correctly caches per-station,
not globally across different stations.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: both provider
files). Case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean for both.
Directly verified the caching logic actually reduces network calls:
simulated 20 rapid-fire calls against a fake provider with the same
cache-check structure -- only 2 real "fetches" occurred, confirming
the TTL check correctly short-circuits repeated calls rather than
just adding dead code that never actually triggers. Confirmed no
other EPG provider implementations exist elsewhere in the codebase
with the same gap (searched the full epg_providers/ tree for any
other getSchedule()/getNowPlaying() definitions -- only these two).
Full-codebase translation sweep clean (no new user-facing strings
this round). Not yet re-confirmed on a real device this round -- the
next test should show no more UI-refresh gaps coinciding with EPG
activity, and ideally the reported remote-control lag resolved
entirely on stations that trigger Finland Radio EPG matching.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 68 (2026-08-31) -- radio search result limit made configurable, plus an unlimited-for-own-language toggle

User confirmed round 67's EPG caching fix resolved the reported
stuttering (VIX icon now clears quickly, remote control responsive).
Separately noticed that updating the station database found 20000
stations, but browsing with Language set to "Any" only ever showed
100 -- asked whether there's a fetch or browse limit, suggested
pagination (load the next 100 on scroll) as one option, and asked for
two settings: a configurable "how many stations to fetch" (0 = all),
and a way to "fetch all stations of the currently used language".

### Found the real cause directly in the code

Checked internetradio_manager.py directly rather than guessing:
DATABASE_DOWNLOAD_LIMIT (20000) governs the one-time bulk database
sync only -- the local database genuinely does hold up to 20000
stations already. The 100 the user saw when browsing is a completely
separate constant, DEFAULT_SEARCH_LIMIT, applied every time search()
filters that already-local database down to a result count
(`self._filterStations(...)[:limit]`) -- not a RadioBrowser API
constraint at all, and not something that needed a network round-trip
or pagination to work around, since the full data was already sitting
in memory. This is a pure client-side display cap on an in-memory
filter, which meant the simplest fix (raising or removing the cap)
was both correct and far less work than the pagination approach
suggested -- worth noting to the user directly rather than building
the more complex option first.

### Two new settings, radio.search_limit and radio.unlimited_for_own_language

radio.search_limit (ConfigInteger, default 100, limits 0-20000)
replaces the hardcoded DEFAULT_SEARCH_LIMIT as the effective cap;
0 means "no limit". Caught a real gotcha before it became a live bug:
Python's own list[:0] slice returns an EMPTY list, not "everything" --
the opposite of what a "0 = unlimited" setting needs. Added an
explicit `filtered if limit == 0 else filtered[:limit]` check instead
of trusting slice syntax directly, and verified this directly (a
naive slice[:0] test confirmed it returns 0 items, matching the bug
this explicit check avoids). Also fixed the same latent issue in the
live-RadioBrowser-API fallback path (used only when the local
database is empty, pre-first-sync) -- rather than assuming
RadioBrowser's own API treats a literal limit=0 as "unlimited" (not
verifiable from this environment), substituted DATABASE_DOWNLOAD_LIMIT
in that case instead, reusing a bound this file already trusts
elsewhere rather than guessing at external API semantics.

radio.unlimited_for_own_language (ConfigYesNo, default False, per
direct request: "hae käytössä olevan kielen kaikki kanavat") lets a
user browse their own language's full station list without raising
the general limit for every other search too -- a plain "Any"-language
search across the whole database is a very different (much larger)
result set than "just my own language," so keeping these as two
separate, independently-adjustable settings matches what was actually
asked for rather than conflating them into one. Reused
radiobrowserscreen.py's own existing _APP_LANGUAGE_TO_RADIOBROWSER_NAME
mapping (built for the unrelated "promote app language to top of the
Language panel" feature) to match the app's own current language
against RadioBrowser's own language-name strings, rather than
building a second, parallel mapping. Verified the matching logic
directly against six scenarios (no language selected, feature off,
matching language in both cases/capitalizations, a different
language, and a language RadioBrowser doesn't map to the app's own
two languages at all) -- only the genuine own-language match
triggered the unlimited case in every scenario.

Both new settings were registered in config.py's own _ENTRIES flat
registry immediately (not left for a future round to discover missing,
per Build 0008's own hard-learned lesson about this exact class of
bug) and wired into radiobrowserscreen.py's own _search() method,
which previously called internetradio_manager.search() without
passing any limit at all (silently defaulting to the now-configurable
100).

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: config.py,
internetradio_manager.py, radiobrowserscreen.py, settingsscreen.py).
Case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean for all four. Directly
verified the limit=0 fix with a simulated 250-station list (confirmed
100/0/20000 all behave correctly, and confirmed what the OLD buggy
slice[:0] would have done -- return zero, the opposite of the new
setting's own intent). Directly verified the own-language matching
logic against six scenarios. Full-codebase translation sweep: found
and added 4 missing strings (two setting labels, two descriptions);
re-ran the comprehensive mojibake check across the full, now-273-entry
.po/.mo pair -- zero found. Updated SETTINGSSCREEN_SPEC.md's own Radio
Settings section to document both new settings, matching round 66's
own documentation-maintenance practice. Not yet re-confirmed on a real
device this round -- the next test should confirm Language="Any"
(or any other filter) actually returns more than 100 stations when
the limit is raised or set to 0, and that the own-language toggle
correctly shows an unrestricted count specifically for the user's own
configured language.

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 69 (2026-08-31) -- the actual database download was also capped, separately from round 68's search fix

User confirmed round 68's search-limit setting worked as configured
(0 = unlimited, own-language toggle on), but reported the station
database update still stalled at exactly 20000 total stations / 44
Finnish, while radio-browser.info's own website reports 58362 total /
88 Finnish for comparison.

### Found a second, separate cap -- round 68 only fixed the search/filter step, not the download step

Checked internetradio_manager.py directly again rather than assuming
round 68 already covered this: DATABASE_DOWNLOAD_LIMIT (20000) is a
completely separate constant from DEFAULT_SEARCH_LIMIT, governing
updateStationDatabase()'s own single request to RadioBrowser, not the
local-database filtering round 68 already fixed. The user's own
comparison numbers were the confirming evidence: getting EXACTLY
20000 (not some smaller number) rules out a RadioBrowser-side cap and
points squarely at this project's own hardcoded constant as the
actual limit -- if RadioBrowser itself had capped the response lower,
the user would have seen fewer than 20000, not exactly that number.

The original code only ever made ONE single API request with
limit=20000 -- untested from this environment against whether
RadioBrowser's own live API can actually return tens of thousands of
stations in a single request at all (many REST APIs impose their own
server-side per-request ceiling regardless of what's asked for).

### Fix: real offset-based pagination, reusing round 68's own search_limit setting as the actual download target

Rather than simply raising the hardcoded constant to a bigger single-
request number (which would have the same unverified-single-request
risk, just with a bigger number), implemented genuine pagination:
updateStationDatabase() now loops fetching DATABASE_DOWNLOAD_PAGE_SIZE
(5000)-station pages via offset, until either RadioBrowser returns an
empty page, returns fewer stations than requested (the real end of
its own catalogue), or the configured target is reached. Reused
radio.search_limit (round 68) as the actual download target too,
treating its own 0="unlimited" meaning consistently across both
search and download -- rather than adding a third, separately-
configured limit that would have overlapped confusingly with what
round 68 already introduced. DATABASE_DOWNLOAD_LIMIT itself is kept,
but repurposed as only an ultimate safety ceiling (100000, comfortably
above the real ~58000-station catalogue) for when search_limit is set
to 0, not the everyday per-update target it used to be.

Renamed the setting from "Radio search result limit" to "Radio
station limit" (and expanded its own description accordingly) once
its scope grew to cover the database download too -- the narrower
original name would have been actively misleading about what the
setting now actually controls.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: internetradio_
manager.py, settingsscreen.py, config.py). Case-insensitive AST
undefined-name sweep clean. Directly simulated the pagination logic
against four scenarios using a fake RadioBrowser stand-in: the user's
own exact reported total (58362 stations, target=0/unlimited) resolved
correctly in 12 simulated requests; a smaller explicit target (1000)
correctly stopped after a single request; a catalogue smaller than one
page (42 stations) was detected correctly without extra requests; and
an edge case where the total is an exact multiple of the page size
(10000 stations, page size 5000) terminated cleanly in exactly 3
requests with no wasted trailing empty-page request. Full-codebase
translation sweep: found and added 2 missing strings (the renamed
label and its expanded description); re-ran the comprehensive mojibake
check across the full, now-275-entry .po/.mo pair -- zero found.
Updated SETTINGSSCREEN_SPEC.md's own documentation to reflect the
renamed setting's wider scope. Not yet verified against RadioBrowser's
own live API from a real device this round -- the pagination logic
itself is confirmed correct via simulation, but whether RadioBrowser's
real API behaves exactly as assumed (accepts and correctly honours an
"offset" parameter across repeated requests) can only be confirmed on
the next real device test.

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.
