## Round 71 (2026-08-31) -- fixed a real premature-track-end bug, caught a critical error in the fix itself before shipping it

User reported local music sometimes cuts off before the track actually
finishes, and attached both a device log showing the failure and a
proposed code change (basing the "track finished" decision on
estimated_position rather than GStreamer's own reported elapsed).

### Root-caused directly from the log, then verified the exact mechanism with arithmetic

The log showed GStreamer's own reported "elapsed" stuck at a single
value (272) for many consecutive ticks while estimated_position (wall-
clock derived) kept climbing normally and correctly (256, 257, 258,
259...). Each tick correctly logged "Discarding implausible elapsed
reading" -- the existing tolerance check (elapsed > estimated_position
+ tolerance) was doing its job. But the tolerance itself grows with
estimated_position (POSITION_DRIFT_TOLERANCE_RATIO = 0.03/second), so
it was only ever a matter of time before it widened enough to swallow
the stuck value. Recomputed the exact boundary by hand: at
estimated_position=259.5, tolerance = 5 + 259.5*0.03 = 12.785, and
259.5 + 12.785 = 272.285 -- just barely wider than GStreamer's own
stuck 272, so on that exact tick the stale reading was accepted as
"plausible" for the first time, and because it happened to exactly
equal duration, immediately triggered the old elapsed>=duration check,
ending the track roughly 13 seconds before the audio had actually
finished playing.

### Applied the user's own proposed fix, but found and fixed a critical bug in it before trusting it

The submitted patch's own structure (primary check on
estimated_position, elapsed/duration as a fallback "if estimated_
position isn't available yet") was sound in intent, but as literally
written the fallback's own condition (`elif elapsed is not None and
duration and elapsed >= duration`) would fire whenever the PRIMARY
condition was false for ANY reason -- not just when estimated_position
was genuinely unavailable (None). Caught this by testing the exact
logged scenario directly rather than trusting the patch's structure:
a direct simulation showed the fallback branch still firing "FINISHED
(via elapsed fallback)" for the precise bug scenario the fix was
supposed to prevent, since estimated_position being merely "too low to
finish yet" (259.5, not None) still fell through to the same broken
elapsed>=duration check. Fixed by adding an explicit `estimated_
position is None` guard to the fallback branch, then re-ran the same
seven-scenario test suite -- the bug scenario now correctly stays
"still playing," while a genuinely-unavailable-estimate case (radio,
or the brief window right after playback starts) still correctly
falls back and fires when appropriate.

Also added a `duration` truthiness guard to the primary check that
the submitted patch didn't have -- required because live radio
streams typically report no fixed duration (None), and `duration -
0.5` would otherwise raise a TypeError on every single radio playback
tick. Verified this doesn't affect radio at all: a "duration=None"
test case correctly falls through to "still playing" without error in
either branch.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: playback_
controller.py). Case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean.
Directly simulated the corrected decision logic against seven
scenarios: the exact bug case from the log (now correctly does NOT
finish), a genuine normal finish (estimated close to duration, still
finishes correctly), radio with no duration (no crash, correctly
never finishes via either branch), the early-playback fallback case
(finishes correctly via the elapsed fallback when estimated_position
is genuinely None), a mid-track false-positive check (correctly still
playing), a duration=0 edge case (no crash), and the fallback's own
positive case (estimated_position=None, elapsed>=duration -- correctly
fires). Full-codebase translation sweep clean (no user-facing strings
changed this round). Not yet re-confirmed on a real device this round
-- the next test should confirm the specific track from the log (or
another with the same failure pattern) now plays to its actual end
rather than cutting off early.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 72 (2026-08-31) -- fixed a related display bug: elapsed/remaining time could jump to duration/0 for the rest of a track

User confirmed round 71's fix worked (the track now ends correctly),
but reported a new symptom: roughly 12 seconds before a track actually
ends, the displayed elapsed time jumps to show the full duration and
remaining jumps to 0 -- and asked directly whether the time display
and lyrics use GStreamer's own time.

### Answered the direct question by checking the code, not assuming

Lyrics: no. information_panel.py's own _buildLyricsPage() already
calls playback.getEstimatedElapsedTime() directly (round 10's own
fix, confirmed by reading its own code comment: "GStreamer's own raw
reading can [jump]... accepted for the elapsed/remaining [display]" --
this single sentence, written back in round 10, essentially predicted
today's exact bug report). Time display: yes -- MainScreen's own
elapsed/remaining/progress-bar update was still calling
self._playback.getElapsedTime(), returning the raw, sometimes-stuck
GStreamer value directly.

### Root-caused the exact mechanism from the attached log

Confirmed directly: GStreamer's own reported elapsed stayed fixed at
272 across many consecutive ticks while estimated_position climbed
268, 269, 270, 271 -- and every one of those ticks was ACCEPTED (no
"Discarding implausible" log line), meaning self._position (and
therefore the display) was set to 272 for the ENTIRE remainder of the
track's real playback, not just the one tick round 71 already
prevented from ending the track. This follows directly from how the
tolerance check works: it only rejects a reading that's too far AHEAD
of the estimate; once a stuck reading first gets accepted (exactly
the round-71 boundary case), a value that has stopped advancing while
the estimate keeps climbing past it will keep looking "close enough"
to the tolerance check for the rest of the track, since the estimate
moving further away only makes the tolerance window (which grows with
estimated_position) even wider, never narrower.

### Fix: the display now uses the same estimate lyrics already trusted

Changed MainScreen's own progress-bar-update method to compute a new
display_elapsed via getEstimatedElapsedTime() (the same method round
10 already built specifically for this class of problem, just never
extended to this display) and use it for the "elapsed"/"remaining"
text widgets and the progress bar's own percentage (computed directly
from display_elapsed and duration, rather than calling
getProgress(), which still reads the raw, potentially-stuck position
internally). Checked every other caller of getElapsedTime()/
getProgress() before touching anything broader: only three exist in
the whole codebase -- developer_screen.py (a diagnostics screen,
left alone; showing the true raw value there is arguably the correct
behaviour for a debugging tool) and MainScreen's own two calls (now
fixed). The raw elapsed/duration values are still computed and still
passed to _updateInformationPanel() unchanged, since InformationPanel
already correctly resolves its own estimate internally when it
actually needs one for lyrics, and only uses the passed elapsed value
as an "is playback happening" gate that works identically either way.
Also updated the method's own closing diagnostic log line to report
display_elapsed instead of the raw value, so a future device log
shows numbers that actually match what was on screen at the time,
rather than the mismatch that would otherwise make a future report
like this one harder to read.

### Testing

Full plugin.main() regression passes (syntax-checked: mainscreen.py).
Case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean. Directly simulated
the exact reported scenario (estimated 268-271, GStreamer stuck at
272, duration 272) both before and after the fix: before, every tick
showed elapsed=272/remaining=0 (matching the report exactly); after,
elapsed/remaining advance smoothly and correctly (268/4, 269/3, 270/2,
271/1). Full-codebase translation sweep clean (no user-facing strings
changed this round). Not yet re-confirmed on a real device this round
-- the next test should show the elapsed/remaining time and progress
bar continuing to advance smoothly and accurately all the way to the
track's real end, rather than freezing at the full duration early.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

translation sweep was needed. Not yet confirmed on a real device --

## Round 75 (2026-08-31) -- investigating a reported CH+/CH- focus bug on RadioBrowserScreen (diagnostic only, not yet fixed)

User confirmed round 74's footer-hint nudge looks good (all seven
hint pairs now clear of their icons). User also reported a new,
unrelated issue: on RadioBrowserScreen, UP/DOWN correctly moves the
selection in whichever panel (Stations/Language/Region) currently has
focus, but CH+/CH- (PAGE_STEP jump) always moves the Stations panel's
selection even when focus is on the Language or Region panel.

### Code review -- the reported behaviour contradicts what the source does

moveUp()/moveDown() (bound to UP/DOWN) and pageUp()/pageDown() (bound
to CH+/CH-) both read the same self._focus attribute and both call
self[self._focus].up()/.down() -- pageUp()/pageDown() simply loop that
call PAGE_STEP times. self._focus is written in exactly three places
(__init__ default "stations", focusPrevious(), focusNext()) and none
of them are conditioned on which key triggered the call. By direct
code reading, CH+/CH- should already respect the same focus as
UP/DOWN -- there is no code path here that would special-case the
Stations panel.

### Decision: gather a device log rather than guess a fix

Since the code already looks correct on inspection, changing it
without understanding the real mechanism would be exactly the kind of
blind fix this project avoids (see "simulate the exact failure
scenario directly" in HANDOFF's own established practices). Instead,
added the current self._focus value to pageUp()'s/pageDown()'s own
existing verbose log lines ("CH+ pressed." / "CH- pressed." ->
"CH+ pressed. focus=%s" / "CH- pressed. focus=%s"), so the next
device test's log will show directly whether self._focus was actually
"language"/"region" at the moment CH+/CH- was pressed (confirming the
bug is somewhere else entirely, e.g. a competing keymap context) or
whether it was somehow still "stations" (which would point back at
focusNext()/focusPrevious() not being called/registering when
expected).

### Testing

Confirmed the file's own syntax with ast.parse() after the change; no
skin/XML touched this round, so the mandatory skin-validation sweep
doesn't apply. No user-facing strings changed, so no translation
sweep was needed.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23. No version bump this round -- diagnostic
logging only, not a shipped fix.

---

## Round 76 (2026-08-31) -- root-caused the CH+/CH- focus bug from a device log: missing ActionMap context, not a self._focus bug

User attached a device log capturing several CH+/CH- presses while
focus was on RadioBrowserScreen's Language/Region panels.

### Root-caused directly from the log

Every genuinely-dispatched key in the log (RIGHT, DOWN, OK, MENU) logs
an "[ActionMap] Keymap '<context>' -> Action = '<name>'" line
immediately before MediaPlayer3's own "[RadioBrowser] X pressed." log
line. Across roughly 20 separate KEY_CHANNELUP/KEY_CHANNELDOWN
presses in the log (each correctly reported by the low-level
"[InfoBarGenerics] Key: ... Binding='(BOUQUET+,)'"/"(BOUQUET-,)" trace
that fires for every physical key regardless of Python-side handling),
not one is followed by an "[ActionMap] Keymap ..." line, and
round 75's own new "focus=%s" logging never appears either -- proving
pageUp()/pageDown() were never invoked at all. This isn't a
self._focus bug (round 75's code-reading suspicion): the key never
reaches ANY Python ActionMap, ours or anyone else's.

"BOUQUET+"/"BOUQUET-" (the action names this receiver's keymap.xml
binds KEY_CHANNELUP/KEY_CHANNELDOWN to, and already the first
candidate in compatibility.py's own CHANNEL_UP_KEY_ACTIONS/
CHANNEL_DOWN_KEY_ACTIONS -- so the action-name side was never the
problem) live in Enigma2's standard "InfobarChannelSelection" context,
which RadioBrowserScreen's own ActionMap never declared (it declares
"InfobarBouquetActions" instead -- a different, unrelated context).
With the correct context missing, the key was never dispatched to
anyone, Python-side, at all -- not a focus bug, a plain missing-
context bug.

### Fix

Added "InfobarChannelSelection" to RadioBrowserScreen's own ActionMap
context list (alongside the existing "InfobarBouquetActions").
pageUp()/pageDown()'s own action-name registration and self._focus
logic (rounds 23 and 75) were already correct and needed no change.

### Testing

Confirmed the file's own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile. No
skin/XML touched this round, so the mandatory skin-validation sweep
doesn't apply. No user-facing strings changed, so no translation
sweep was needed. Not yet confirmed on a real device -- the next test
should show CH+/CH- correctly jumping PAGE_STEP entries in whichever
panel (Stations/Language/Region) currently has focus, and the round
75 diagnostic log line ("CH+ pressed. focus=...") should now finally
appear in the device log.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 77 (2026-08-31) -- round 76's context guess was wrong; found the real one from a second device log, matching BrowserScreen's own known-working pattern

User reported CH+/CH- still didn't work on RadioBrowserScreen after
round 76's fix, and pointed out it already works correctly in
BrowserScreen (jumping there, by the user's own count, 5 lines) --
suggesting comparing the two screens' own setup.

### Root-caused from a second device log: round 76's context was never even reached

The new log confirmed round 76's "InfobarChannelSelection" addition
never fires (no "[ActionMap] Keymap 'InfobarChannelSelection' ..."
line anywhere). What actually dispatches on every single CH+/CH- press
is "[ActionMap] Keymap 'MediaPlayerActions' -> Action = 'prevBouquet'"
/ "'nextBouquet'" -- and RadioBrowserScreen's own ActionMap never
declared the "MediaPlayerActions" context at all, so that dispatch was
always going to whoever ELSE currently has "MediaPlayerActions"
registered: MainScreen, which binds "prevBouquet"/"nextBouquet" to
its own previous/next-track skip (visible earlier in SystemInfo's own
"previous_track_key_actions: previous, skip_back, prevBouquet" /
"next_track_key_actions: next, skip_forward, nextBouquet") and stays
registered underneath RadioBrowserScreen the whole time it's open.

Checked BrowserScreen's own ActionMap context list for comparison
(user's own suggestion) -- it already includes "MediaPlayerActions",
which is exactly why CH+/CH- already works correctly there: its own
pageUp()/pageDown() (bound to the same "prevBouquet"/"nextBouquet"
candidate names via compatibility.getChannelUpKeyActionNames()/
getChannelDownKeyActionNames(), used identically in both screens)
wins the dispatch over MainScreen's competing handler for the same
(context, action) pair whenever BrowserScreen is the topmost screen.

### Fix

Removed round 76's "InfobarChannelSelection" (confirmed via log to
never actually fire -- a wrong guess) and added "MediaPlayerActions"
to RadioBrowserScreen's own ActionMap context list instead, matching
BrowserScreen's own already-confirmed-working pattern exactly. No
change needed to the action-name registration or PAGE_STEP (10,
same as BrowserScreen's own) -- both were already correct.

### Testing

Confirmed the file's own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile.
Removed the __pycache__ directory py_compile leaves behind before
packaging (round 76's own delivered .ipk accidentally included one --
noticed via an Installed-Size jump during packaging, not shipped).
No skin/XML touched this round. No user-facing strings changed, so no
translation sweep was needed. Not yet confirmed on a real device --
the next test should show CH+/CH- correctly jumping PAGE_STEP entries
in whichever RadioBrowserScreen panel currently has focus, matching
BrowserScreen's own already-working behaviour.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 78 (2026-08-31) -- CH+/CH- direction was inverted; PAGE_STEP raised to 15 (RadioBrowserScreen only)

User confirmed round 77's fix made CH+/CH- actually move the focused
panel, but in the wrong direction (CH+ moved down, CH- moved up), and
asked for a larger jump (~15 entries).

### Root-caused directly from the attached log

The log's own "[RadioBrowser] CH+/CH- pressed. focus=..." lines
(round 75's diagnostic) confirmed it precisely: KEY_CHANNELDOWN
resolves to action "prevBouquet" and logs "CH+ pressed" (registered
under compatibility.py's own CHANNEL_UP_KEY_ACTIONS), while
KEY_CHANNELUP resolves to "nextBouquet" and logs "CH- pressed"
(registered under CHANNEL_DOWN_KEY_ACTIONS) -- the exact reverse of
what those two tuples assumed. This is a leftover from rounds 2-3,
when neither "prevBouquet" nor "nextBouquet" had ever actually been
confirmed to fire at all (round 76-77's own context bugs meant they
never got the chance until now) -- the UP/DOWN assignment for those
two names specifically was always an unconfirmed guess, unlike
"BOUQUET+"/"BOUQUET-" which round 4 had already confirmed correctly.

### Fix

Swapped "prevBouquet" and "nextBouquet" between CHANNEL_UP_KEY_ACTIONS
and CHANNEL_DOWN_KEY_ACTIONS in compatibility.py (shared by every
screen that pages with CH+/CH-, not just RadioBrowserScreen -- see
note below). Also raised RadioBrowserScreen's own PAGE_STEP from 10 to
15 per direct request. BrowserScreen's own PAGE_STEP (also 10) was
left unchanged since only RadioBrowserScreen's jump size was asked
about.

### A note for the next round: this may also affect BrowserScreen

BrowserScreen's own pageUp()/pageDown() are wired through the exact
same compatibility.py CHANNEL_UP_KEY_ACTIONS/CHANNEL_DOWN_KEY_ACTIONS
candidates, and the device log confirms this receiver's real keymap
never actually fires "BOUQUET+"/"BOUQUET-" for either screen -- always
"prevBouquet"/"nextBouquet" via MediaPlayerActions. That means
BrowserScreen's own CH+/CH- almost certainly had the same reversed
direction as RadioBrowserScreen did, just unreported (the user's own
round-77 comment was about it "working", not about which way it
jumped). Flagged for the user to double check next test; not changed
defensively without their own confirmation either way.

### Testing

Confirmed both files' own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile.
Removed the __pycache__ directory before packaging (round 76's own
lesson, repeated here as a reminder to check every round). No skin/XML
touched. No user-facing strings changed, so no translation sweep was
needed. Not yet confirmed on a real device -- the next test should
show CH+ moving up and CH- moving down in RadioBrowserScreen's focused
panel, jumping 15 entries per press, and should also confirm whether
BrowserScreen's own CH+/CH- direction changed (for better or worse).

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 79 (2026-08-31) -- CH+/CH- confirmed correct on RadioBrowserScreen; rolled the same fixes out to BrowserScreen, MusicLibraryScreen, PodcastScreen, and added the feature to PlaylistScreen from scratch

User confirmed RadioBrowserScreen's CH+/CH- now moves the right way,
and confirmed BrowserScreen's own CH+/CH- direction is correct too

and PodcastScreen's own ActionMap context lists included

Neither did -- both matched RadioBrowserScreen's own pre-round-77
state exactly, missing only "MediaPlayerActions" from an otherwise
identical context list. BrowserScreen was the only one of the four
that already had it, which is exactly why it was the only one

CH+/CH- action registration at all -- only moveUp()/moveDown() for
UP/DOWN. Added, mirroring the other four screens' own established
pattern exactly:
- PAGE_STEP = 15 (module-level constant, same convention as the other
  four screens).
- pageUp()/pageDown() methods, structured identically to the existing
  moveUp()/moveDown() (same self._focus == "playlists" branch calling

- Registered via the same compatibility.getChannelUpKeyActionNames()/

- Added "MediaPlayerActions" to PlaylistScreen's own ActionMap context

  before, since it never handled CH+/CH- at all).

No footer hint text added for CH+/CH- on any of the four screens that
don't already show one (BrowserScreen/MusicLibraryScreen/PodcastScreen/

RadioBrowserScreen has ever displayed one, and this wasn't asked for.

### Testing

## Round 80 (2026-08-31) -- CH+/CH- confirmed working on Radio, Podcasts, Playlists and Browser; clamp page-jump at list boundaries instead of wrapping

User confirmed CH+/CH- now works correctly (right direction, 15-entry
jump) on RadioBrowserScreen, PodcastScreen, PlaylistScreen and
BrowserScreen (MusicLibraryScreen not mentioned this round). Asked for
one more refinement: if fewer than PAGE_STEP (15) entries remain
between the current selection and the top/bottom of the list, CH+/
CH- should stop at that end of the list rather than wrapping past it.

### Fix

In all five screens with CH+/CH- paging (BrowserScreen,
MusicLibraryScreen, PodcastScreen, PlaylistScreen,
RadioBrowserScreen), changed pageUp()/pageDown() from blindly calling
.up()/.down() PAGE_STEP times to first computing how many steps are
actually safe:
- pageUp(): steps = min(PAGE_STEP, widget.getSelectedIndex()) -- can't
  go higher than the current index.
- pageDown(): steps = min(PAGE_STEP, max(0, len(widget.list or []) - 1
  - widget.getSelectedIndex())) -- can't go past the last entry.

Then loops exactly that many times instead of the full PAGE_STEP.
This guarantees the selection lands exactly on the first/last entry
when fewer than PAGE_STEP entries remain, regardless of whether the
underlying MenuList's own .up()/.down() would otherwise wrap around --
reuses the same self[widget].getSelectedIndex()/.list access already
used elsewhere in these files (e.g. RadioBrowserScreen's own
_selectListEntry()), so no new widget API assumptions were introduced.

PlaylistScreen's pageUp()/pageDown() (added fresh in round 79) also
picked up a small simplification alongside the clamp: now reads
self[self._focus] directly (self._focus is always "playlists" or
"tracks", both valid widget keys) instead of the separate hardcoded
self["playlists"]/self["tracks"] branches round 79 had copied from
moveUp()/moveDown() -- the if/else on self._focus is kept only where
it decides whether to also call _onPlaylistSelectionChanged()
afterward.

### Testing

Confirmed all five files' own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile.
Removed __pycache__ before packaging. No skin/XML touched, no
user-facing strings changed, so no translation sweep was needed. Not
yet confirmed on a real device -- the next test should show CH+/CH-
stopping cleanly at the first/last entry of a short list (or when the
selection starts within 15 entries of an end) instead of jumping past
it.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 81 (2026-08-31) -- HelpScreen's last line was getting clipped; help documents had fallen out of date with the CH+/CH- work

User reported the last line of help text gets partially cut off while
scrolling, suggested adding a trailing blank line to each help
document and tightening the display area's bottom edge, and asked for
a full review of every help document's own content since the program
has changed a lot since they were written.

### Display fix

Found that simply adding a blank line to each help document's own
Markdown source would have had no effect: HelpManager's own
_collapseBlankLines() ends with .strip("\n"), which strips any
trailing blank line before HelpScreen ever sees the content. Instead,
added the trailing blank line at the HelpScreen level itself (self.
_lines.append("") right after loading content in __init__) --
guarantees a blank buffer line at the very end of every help text
regardless of what's in the source document, immune to any future
change in HelpManager's own rendering.

Also lowered VISIBLE_LINES from 20 to 18: 20 lines' rendered height
was landing right at the edge of the content box for this font/
line-height combination, so the last line only partially fit; 18
leaves enough headroom that every line is always shown in full,
matching the same "conservative, not-dynamically-measured estimate"
approach this constant already used (per its own existing comment,
matching DeveloperScreen's own convention).

### Reviewed every help document's content -- found it stale in exactly the way the user expected

Read all twelve documents in resources/help/. Five referenced the
CH+/CH- work directly and needed updating:
- browserscreen.md, musiclibraryscreen.md, podcastscreen.md,
  radiobrowserscreen.md: all said "jump 10 entries/rows", now say 15
  (rounds 78-80); the three that had a "(may not work on every
  remote/image)" caveat (musiclibraryscreen.md, podcastscreen.md,
  radiobrowserscreen.md) had it removed, since rounds 76-79 confirmed
  and fixed the underlying cause rather than leaving it as a
  per-device gamble. Also added a short "stopping at the top/bottom
  of the list" note reflecting round 80's clamp fix.
- playlistscreen.md: didn't mention CH+/CH- at all, since the feature
  didn't exist before round 79. Added a line for it, matching the
  other four screens' own new wording.

Deliberately left player.md's own "CH+ / CH-: previous/next track
(may not work on every remote/image)" line untouched -- that's
MainScreen's own, separate CH+/CH- binding (track skip, not list
paging), which this session's device logs never actually exercised or
confirmed either way, unlike the five list-paging screens above.
Removing an unconfirmed caveat without device evidence would be
exactly the kind of guess this project avoids; flagging it instead
for the user to confirm on a future test if they want that line
updated too.

The other seven documents (mainscreen.md, mainmenu.md,
settingsscreen.md, developerscreen.md, information.md, player.md
[aside from the CH+/CH- line just discussed], playlist.md) were read
in full and found to still match current behaviour -- no changes
made to them.

### Testing

Confirmed help_screen.py's own syntax with ast.parse() and
py_compile. No skin/XML rect or font values changed (only
VISIBLE_LINES and the trailing-blank-line logic), so the mandatory
skin-validation sweep doesn't strictly apply, but the screen's own
syntax was still confirmed clean. Markdown help documents aren't
code, so no syntax check applies to them; each edited line was
re-read after editing to confirm correct wording. Not yet confirmed
on a real device -- the next test should show every line of help text
fully visible while scrolling, including the last one, and the five
updated documents' CH+/CH- descriptions should now match actual
behaviour.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 83 (2026-09-01) -- 1.0.0-beta3 marked as released to public testing; started the LRCLIB lyrics-download feature

User confirmed 1.0.0-beta3 is ready and asked to mark it as released
to public testing, with all changes from this point on belonging to
the next version. Also started a new feature: downloading lyrics from
LRCLIB, attaching the `lrcget` reference application (a Rust/Tauri
desktop app that already implements this against the real LRCLIB API)
and a link to lrclib.net's own API docs. First concrete step
requested: in BrowserScreen, OK on a directory or a file gets a new
"Download lyrics" menu entry, opening a confirmation dialog ("Download
lyrics?", OK continues) -- deliberately its own small step so future
rounds can add further questions (e.g. synced vs. plain-text lyrics)
without restructuring a combined dialog.

### Marked 1.0.0-beta3 as released

Added a "1.0.0-beta3 released to public testing (2026-09-01)" marker
to CHANGELOG.md, between the existing "POST-BETA2 FIXES (rounds
67-81)" section and the older "SKIN REDESIGN & LANGUAGE DETECTION"
one. Wrote a new HANDOFF_2026-09-01.md replacing the previous
(round-72-dated) handoff, summarizing rounds 73-82 and this round's
new feature for continuity in a future session.

### Researched the LRCLIB API from the attached reference app and lrclib.net's own docs

lrclib.net/docs itself is a JS-rendered page that didn't return usable
content via a plain fetch; cross-checked via web search results
(cached copies of the same page) and, more concretely, read the
bundled `lrcget` app's own Rust client code directly
(src-tauri/src/lrclib/*.rs) -- an actual working implementation against
the real API, not documentation prose. Confirmed:
- GET {base}/api/get?artist_name=...&track_name=...&album_name=...&duration=<int seconds>
  -- exact-match lookup. Returns plainLyrics/syncedLyrics (LRC-format
  string)/instrumental/etc; 404 if no match.
- GET {base}/api/search -- fuzzy fallback (same params minus exact
  duration, plus a free-text `q`), used by lrcget when the exact match
  fails; returns an array, no pagination.
- GET {base}/api/get/{id} -- fetch a specific known record.
- Publishing needs a proof-of-work challenge (SHA-256 nonce search,
  src-tauri/src/lrclib/challenge_solver.rs) -- not needed for a
  download-only feature, noted for later in case contributing lyrics
  back is ever wanted.
- lrcget's own User-Agent convention ("LRCGET vX.Y.Z (url)") matches
  this project's own existing RadioBrowser User-Agent pattern (round
  70) closely enough to reuse the same approach for LRCLIB requests
  in a future round.
Recorded all of this in the new HANDOFF file so a future round doesn't
have to re-derive it from the reference app again.

### Implemented round 83's own requested first step: the confirmation dialog only

Added "Download lyrics" to BrowserScreen's `_directoryMenu()` (both
branches -- with and without a previewed-files shortcut) and
`_fileMenu()` choice lists, each routing to a new `_offerLyricsDownload
(target)` method (shared by both directory and file callers -- `target`
is a path either way, only the eventual download logic will need to
tell them apart). This opens a `MessageBox.TYPE_YESNO` asking
_("Download lyrics?"), matching this project's own existing
confirmation pattern (e.g. RadioBrowserScreen's `_offerDatabaseDownload()`)
rather than introducing a new dialog style. Confirming currently logs
verbosely and shows a brief "Lyrics download is not implemented yet."
info message (MessageBox.TYPE_INFO, 3s timeout) rather than doing
nothing silently -- the actual LRCLIB request/save logic is real,
separate work for a future round, not built this round, and a
live-test button that visibly does nothing would look broken rather
than obviously unfinished.

### Testing

## Round 84 (2026-09-01) -- LrclibManager: the actual LRCLIB download, wired into round 83's confirmation dialog

User confirmed round 83's "Download lyrics" confirmation works for
both directories and files, suggested a dedicated lrc_manager module
for the real logic, and attached the `lrclibapi` Python package
(reference implementation + docs) as a second source, noting it's
three years old and hoping the API hasn't changed much.

### Cross-checked lrclibapi against round 83's own findings from lrcget -- consistent, plus one new detail

Read lrclibapi's own api.py/models.py directly. Confirmed everything
round 83 found from lrcget's Rust source: GET /api/get with artist_
name/track_name/album_name/duration(int seconds), JSON response with
plainLyrics/syncedLyrics/instrumental, 404 for no match. One new
detail: a GET /api/get-cached endpoint exists alongside /api/get (not
used this round -- exact-match /get is enough for a first working
version).

### New module: lrclib_manager.py

Matches this project's own established *_manager.py shape (matching
internetradio_manager.py's own urllib-based request pattern -- no
third-party HTTP library, same User-Agent convention as round 70's
RadioBrowser one: "MediaPlayer3/{VERSION} (Enigma2; Python/
{python_version})"):
- downloadForFile(filepath): reads tags via the existing metadata_
  reader (metadata.py, already used by Music Library -- reused rather
  than writing a second tag reader), skips if a sibling .lrc/.txt
  already exists, queries LRCLIB's /api/get, and saves the result as
  a sibling .lrc (if syncedLyrics present) or .txt (if only
  plainLyrics present) -- the exact same sibling-file naming
  LyricsManager already reads from (lyrics_manager.py's own
  _siblingPath()), confirmed by an actual round-trip test: saved a
  realistic LRCLIB-shaped synced_lyrics string through
  LrclibManager._saveSibling(), then read it back through the real,
  unmodified LyricsManager._getLRCLyrics()/_parseLRCLines() and
  checked the parsed timestamps matched exactly.
- downloadForDirectory(directory): non-recursive (matches Browser's
  own directory scope elsewhere), tallies per-file results.
- Returns plain result codes (saved_synced/saved_plain/instrumental/
  not_found/already_has_lyrics/missing_tags/error) rather than
  building user-facing text itself -- kept UI wording in
  browserscreen.py, matching this project's own manager/screen
  separation (managers never touch UI text).
Fuzzy /api/search fallback and the publish-lyrics flow (needs a
proof-of-work challenge, not needed for a download-only feature) are
both deliberately not built yet -- noted as open follow-up work, not
silently skipped.

### Wired into BrowserScreen, replacing round 83's placeholder

_lyricsDownloadConfirmed() now uses the same deferred please-wait
pattern as RadioBrowserScreen's own _updateStationDatabase() (round 9):
sets the existing "status" widget to "Downloading lyrics, please
wait...", defers the actual (blocking) call by one GUI tick via a
10ms one-shot eTimer so the message actually paints first, then runs
the real download and shows a result MessageBox. Single-file downloads
get a specific message per result code; directory downloads get a
tally summary (e.g. "Lyrics download finished: 3 synced, 2 not
found."). This is the same class of "acceptable, user-initiated,
one-off blocking call" this project already uses for the station
database update -- not the same problem as round 67's bug, which was
an unintentional per-second background poll, a different class of
issue entirely.

### Testing

Confirmed both lrclib_manager.py's and browserscreen.py's own syntax
with ast.parse() and py_compile; case-insensitive AST undefined-name
sweep clean on both (one false positive each, both pre-existing
`except ... as error:` bindings, not from this round's own code).
Directly simulated LrclibManager's own logic against six scenarios
(synced, already-has-lyrics skip, plain-only, instrumental, not-found,
missing-tags) plus a directory-tally case, all via a monkeypatched
_fetchLyrics() rather than trusting the branching structure alone --
confirmed via the actual imported module (package-relative imports
needed importlib.import_module("src.lrclib_manager") from the project
root, not a bare script run). The .lrc round-trip test above is the
one that actually matters for correctness (an LRCLIB response that
LyricsManager can't parse back would be a real, silent-until-playback
bug) -- confirmed clean. 17 new user-facing strings added to both the

round).

## Round 88 (2026-09-01) -- new feature: download cover art from MusicBrainz/Cover Art Archive, mirroring the LRCLIB lyrics feature exactly

User confirmed round 87's fullscreen lyrics/cover art views both work
correctly (device log showed both screens created, decoded/loaded
content, and closed cleanly via different keys). Requested the next
feature: downloading cover art for local albums, using the same UX as
the LRCLIB lyrics download, attaching `coverlovin2` (a Python
reference app) as a starting point.

### coverlovin2 uses a third-party library and a more complex flow than needed

Read coverlovin2's own ImageSearcher_MusicBrainz class directly: it
depends on the third-party `musicbrainzngs` library (this project is
stdlib-only) and does artist-search -> browse-all-releases ->
fuzzy-match-by-title -> get_image_list, a multi-step flow built around
that library's own API shape. Rather than reimplementing that
specific flow, confirmed the underlying REST APIs directly from
MusicBrainz's and the Cover Art Archive's own official documentation
and used those directly via urllib -- a single combined Lucene query
does the same job as coverlovin2's two-step artist+release lookup:
GET https://musicbrainz.org/ws/2/release/?query=artist:"..." AND
release:"..."&fmt=json, taking the top-scored (0-100 relevance) result
(rejecting anything below a MINIMUM_MATCH_SCORE of 80 as too
uncertain), then GET https://coverartarchive.org/release/{mbid} for
the actual image (this is the officially documented, no-API-key Cover
Art Archive REST API -- confirmed its JSON shape directly from
MusicBrainz's own wiki page, not from a third-party wrapper).

### New module: coverart_manager.py

Mirrors lrclib_manager.py's own shape and public interface closely
(downloadForFile/downloadForDirectory(recursive)/hasSubdirectories),
but reflects a real difference: cover art is one image per album/
directory, not one file per track like lyrics. downloadForFile()
resolves to its own containing directory but queries using THAT
SPECIFIC file's own artist/album tags (not "whichever file in the
directory happens to be found first") -- matters if a user picks a
particular file to identify the album by. downloadForDirectory()
recursive mode treats every subdirectory with audio files as its own
separate album (e.g. "Artist/Album1", "Artist/Album2"), matching
LrclibManager's own round-85 recursive convention. Skips a directory
that already has cover.jpg/cover.png/folder.jpg/folder.png (the exact
same COVER_ART_FILENAMES convention MainScreen's own artwork
resolution already looks for -- duplicated as a plain constant rather
than imported, since a Core manager importing a UI screen's class
attribute would be the wrong dependency direction for this project).
A small per-instance rate limiter (MUSICBRAINZ_MIN_REQUEST_INTERVAL_
SECONDS = 1.1) keeps a recursive multi-album batch polite to
MusicBrainz's own documented ~1 req/sec guidance for unauthenticated
clients -- the Cover Art Archive itself documents no rate limit, so
only MusicBrainz searches are throttled. Same User-Agent convention
as RadioBrowser (round 70) and LRCLIB (round 84).

### Wired into BrowserScreen, exactly mirroring the lyrics chain

Added "Download cover art" to both the Directories and Files action
menus. _offerCoverArtDownload()/_coverArtDownloadConfirmed()/
_startCoverArtDownload() are a direct parallel of rounds 83/85's own
_offerLyricsDownload()/_lyricsDownloadConfirmed()/_startLyricsDownload()
chain, question-order included: "Download cover art?" first, then
(only if the target is a directory with subdirectories) "Also
download cover art for subdirectories?", then the same deferred
please-wait pattern before the real (blocking) work runs. Caught and
fixed one real ordering mistake before it shipped: an early draft
asked the subdirectory question BEFORE the main confirmation
(backwards from the actual, working lyrics flow) -- re-read rounds
83/85's own code directly rather than trusting memory of it, found
the mismatch, and corrected the order to match.

### Testing

Confirmed both coverart_manager.py's and browserscreen.py's own
syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile; case-insensitive AST
undefined-name sweep clean on both (coverart_manager.py's own two
flagged names, "error" and "release", are both false positives --
a pre-existing except-clause binding and a lambda parameter my sweep
script's simple ast.FunctionDef-only argument check doesn't cover,
not anything wrong). Directly simulated downloadForDirectory() against
five scenarios (happy path, already-has-cover skip, no MusicBrainz
match, missing tags, empty directory) via monkeypatched network
methods. Separately simulated the two real JSON-parsing paths with
realistic response shapes rather than trusting the mocked-out version
above: MusicBrainz's own score-based result selection (confirmed it
picks the 100-scored release over a 60- and an 85-scored one, and
confirmed a below-threshold single result is correctly rejected as
"not found") and the Cover Art Archive's own front-image preference
(confirmed it picks the image marked "front": true over a "back"
image, and falls back to the first image when none is marked front).
14 new user-facing strings added to both .po files (three -- "not
found"/"missing tags"/"failed" -- already existed from the lyrics
feature and were reused rather than duplicated); recompiled with
msgfmt with no duplicate-msgid errors; counts matching between fi/en
(312 msgid lines each); mojibake sweep clean. Not yet confirmed on a
real device -- the next test should show a real cover.jpg/png saved
into an album folder and picked up by MainScreen's existing artwork
resolution the next time that album plays.

Updated HANDOFF_2026-09-01.md with this feature and its own API
findings, for continuity.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 89 (2026-09-01) -- automatic retry for MusicBrainz's own 503 rate-limit response

User confirmed the cover art download works and finds subdirectories
correctly. A device log showed MusicBrainz returning HTTP 503
("Service Temporarily Unavailable" -- MusicBrainz's own standard
rate-limit response) three times in a row for the same album across
two separate manual download attempts, only succeeding on a fourth
try -- flagged this and asked whether to add an automatic retry.
User confirmed: add the retry, and a distinct "temporarily busy, try
again shortly" message if it's still rate-limited afterward.

### Fix

Added `_requestMusicBrainzWithRetry()`: on HTTP 503 specifically,
waits MUSICBRAINZ_RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS (5s) and retries once; a second
503 raises a new `MusicBrainzRateLimited` exception rather than being
treated the same as "no matching release" (a real bug this would
otherwise have been: silently reporting "not found" for an album that
MusicBrainz almost certainly does have, just not reachable at that
exact moment). `_searchRelease()` now delegates its own HTTP request
through this retry wrapper. `_downloadForOneDirectory()` catches
`MusicBrainzRateLimited` and returns a new "rate_limited" result code,
kept distinct from "not_found"/"error" throughout -- BrowserScreen's
own `_describeCoverArtDownloadResult()`/`_describeCoverArtDownloadTally()`
both gained a specific message for it ("MusicBrainz is temporarily
busy. Try again in a moment." / "temporarily busy, try again shortly"
in a tally), rather than folding it into the generic failure message.

### Testing

Confirmed coverart_manager.py's own syntax with ast.parse() and
py_compile; case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean (widened
the sweep script itself this round to also cover lambda parameters,
not just def parameters -- this file's own `lambda release: release.
get("score", 0)` inside `_searchRelease()` was a false positive in
round 88's sweep for exactly this reason, now correctly recognized).
Directly simulated the retry logic against three scenarios via a
mocked urlopen: a 503 followed by a successful second attempt
(confirmed it retries exactly once and returns the correct MBID, and
that time.sleep() was actually called); two consecutive 503s
(confirmed MusicBrainzRateLimited is raised after exactly two
attempts, not more); and downloadForDirectory() correctly mapping that
exception to a {"rate_limited": 1} tally rather than propagating an
uncaught exception or silently reporting "not_found". Two new strings
added to both .po files, recompiled with msgfmt, counts matching
between fi/en (314 msgid lines each), mojibake sweep clean. Not yet
re-confirmed on a real device against an actual live 503 -- the
simulation is thorough, but the next test should confirm the real
retry behaves the same way against MusicBrainz's own live rate
limiting.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 90 (2026-09-02) -- same automatic retry for LRCLIB's own 503 rate-limit response

User tested both downloads on a second device (Vu+ Uno4K SE) and

### Fix

Mirrors round 89's own CoverArtManager fix exactly. Added a new
LrclibRateLimited exception and LRCLIB_RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS (5s)
constant. `_fetchLyrics()`'s single request became a two-attempt loop:
on HTTP 503, waits and retries once; a second 503 raises
LrclibRateLimited rather than being treated as a generic "error" (the
real gap the device log exposed -- previously indistinguishable from
a genuine request failure, and with no retry at all). The existing
404 "no lyrics for this track" handling is untouched -- still returns
its own synthetic not-found dict immediately, no retry (a 404 isn't
rate limiting, retrying it would be pointless). `downloadForFile()`
catches LrclibRateLimited and returns a new "rate_limited" result
code. BrowserScreen's own `_describeLyricsDownloadResult()`/
`_describeLyricsDownloadTally()` gained the same distinct message
pattern round 89 added for cover art ("LRCLIB is temporarily busy.
Try again in a moment." / reused round 89's own "temporarily busy,

clean on both (one pre-existing except-clause false positive in
lrclib_manager.py). Directly simulated the retry logic against four
scenarios via a mocked urlopen: a 503 followed by a successful second
attempt (confirmed the correct lyrics dict comes back and time.sleep()
was called); two consecutive 503s (confirmed LrclibRateLimited is
raised after exactly two attempts); a 404 (confirmed it still returns
the synthetic not-found dict immediately with no retry, unchanged from
before this round); and downloadForFile() correctly mapping the
exception to "rate_limited" rather than propagating it uncaught. One
new string added to both .po files (the tally label was already added
in round 89 and reused as-is), recompiled with msgfmt, counts matching

_performCoverArtDownload/_describeCoverArtDownloadResult) defined

## Round 92 (2026-09-02) -- lyrics download: choose all / synced-only / plain-only

User confirmed the LRCLIB retry logging works well (shared a real,
large batch result: 991 already had lyrics, 1069 not found, 31
rate-limited, 11 instrumental, 30 missing tags, 106 plain, 252 synced)
and asked for the previously-deferred follow-up question (flagged as
open work since round 83/84): replace the plain "Download lyrics?"
yes/no with a choice of what to download -- all, synced only (LRC),
or plain text only (TXT).

### Fix

`lrclib_manager.py`: `downloadForFile()`/`downloadForDirectory()` both
gained a `mode` parameter ("all" default, "synced_only", "plain_only").
"all" is the unchanged round-84 behaviour (synced preferred, plain
fallback). "synced_only" saves only if synced lyrics exist -- a
track with plain-only lyrics on LRCLIB is now "not_found" rather than
silently falling back to the .txt (this is the actual behaviour
change "synced only" needs: no fallback at all, matching what
"only" means). "plain_only" always saves the plain text version (even
for a track that also has synced lyrics available, ignoring the
synced version entirely) and reports "not_found" for a track with no
plain text at all (rare, since LRCLIB entries with synced lyrics
almost always carry the derived plain text too, but not guaranteed).

`browserscreen.py`: `_offerLyricsDownload()` now opens a ChoiceBox
("Download lyrics?" title, three mode choices + Cancel) instead of a
plain MessageBox.TYPE_YESNO -- renamed its own callback from
`_lyricsDownloadConfirmed` to `_lyricsDownloadModeChosen` to match.
The chosen mode threads through the rest of the existing chain
unchanged (`_startLyricsDownload`/`_performLyricsDownload`, the
subdirectory question from round 85 still asks the same way in
between) down to the actual `lrclib_manager` calls.

### Testing

Confirmed both files' own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on both. Directly
simulated all three modes against five scenarios: "all" with both
lyrics types available (saved_synced, unchanged baseline); "synced_
only" with both available (saved_synced); "synced_only" with only
plain available (confirmed "not_found", no fallback -- the actual
behaviour change under test); "plain_only" with both available
(confirmed it saves .txt specifically and does NOT also create a
.lrc); "plain_only" with only synced available (confirmed
"not_found"). Three new strings added to both .po files, recompiled
with msgfmt, counts matching between fi/en (318 msgid lines each),
mojibake sweep clean. Checked browserscreen.md's own help document
for any lyrics-download mention needing an update -- it was never
documented there in the first place (added after round 81's help
review), so nothing to update. Not yet confirmed on a real device.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 93 (2026-09-02/03) -- lyrics display: fixed-position current line, font-size tiers, live fullscreen view

User asked for a substantial lyrics-display change, in both MainScreen's
own windowed Information panel and the round-87 fullscreen view:
- LRC (synced) lyrics: previous/next line in a slightly bigger font,
  current line bigger still or bold, and the current line always
  landing at the same screen position (so the bigger font doesn't
  visually jump around).
- TXT (unsynchronized) lyrics: same centered-window behaviour, but no
  font-size increase, since timing isn't actually known.
- Fullscreen view: replace the round-87 static full-text display with
  the same live, centered window as the small panel, scaled up.
- Fullscreen view: UP/DOWN should adjust the lyrics timing offset
  instead of closing the screen, matching the windowed panel's own
  existing UP/DOWN behaviour -- only OK/EXIT/LEFT/RIGHT still close it.

### lyrics_manager.py -- padded windowing + structured data

The existing getScrollWindow()/_windowAround() shifted the window near
the very start/end of a song to keep it full of real lines -- which
meant the "current" line's position WITHIN the window moved around
exactly there, undermining "always the same spot". Replaced with
_windowAroundPadded(): pads with empty strings on whichever side runs
out instead of shifting, so the current line's index within a
fixed-size window is now always exactly window_size // 2, unconditionally.
getScrollWindow() itself is now a thin wrapper joining this same data
with "\n" (fully backward compatible, confirmed by simulation). Added
getScrollWindowData() returning {"lines": [...], "current_index": int,
"synchronized": bool} -- structured, so a caller can style each line
individually, which a joined string can't support.

### information_panel.py -- expose structured window data alongside the existing plain text

Added self._current_lyrics_raw/_elapsed/_duration, stashed inside
_buildLyricsPage() right where self._synchronized_lyrics_title already
gets set (round 4 of Build 0009) -- reuses that exact same "is this
page the live-windowed lyrics page" check rather than adding a second,
parallel tracking mechanism. New public getCurrentLyricsWindowData
(window_size): returns None when the current page isn't Lyrics or live
position isn't available (same title-matching scroll() already uses),
otherwise calls lyrics_manager.getScrollWindowData() fresh at whatever
window_size the caller needs -- independent of self._visible_lines,
which stays the window size the plain getCurrentContent() still uses
for every other page (Metadata/Codec/Radio EPG/...), unaffected by any
of this.

### MainScreen -- multi-widget lyrics display replacing info_content for the Lyrics page only

Added MainScreen.LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS: 13 (height, font_size, bold)
tuples -- 10 normal rows (28/20), 2 bigger prev/next rows (34/24), 1
biggest/bold current row (44/30, always row index 6, the middle of 13)
-- sized to fit info_content's own 428-design-unit box with real
headroom (learned from HelpScreen's own round-81 lesson: don't fill a
text box exactly, leave margin). _buildSkin() now also generates one
lyrics_line_N Label widget per row, stacked over the exact same
position info_content occupies, and stashes self._lyrics_font_family/
_lyrics_font_scale for later use. _updateInformationPanel() now checks
getCurrentLyricsWindowData() each tick: if it returns data, hides
info_content and calls the new _showLyricsWindow() to populate/show
the 13 rows; otherwise hides the lyrics rows and shows info_content
exactly as before (every other page, and the "no live position"
fallback, both completely unaffected).

_showLyricsWindow() sets each row's text, then -- this is the one case
a purely static skin can't express -- forces every row back to the
SAME (normal-tier) font/size for unsynchronized lyrics via a real
runtime gFont() call (widget.instance.setFont()), not just a text
change, since the skin can only bake ONE fixed tier per row and
synced/unsynced tracks need different tiers on the very same widgets.
This is a genuinely new technique for this codebase (no prior runtime
font-changing anywhere else) -- guarded with an `if widget.instance is
not None` check before calling setFont(), matching this project's own
established caution around widget-not-ready-yet races.

### LyricsFullscreenScreen -- rebuilt around the same live mechanism, fullscreen-sized

Completely rewritten (SPECIFICATION_VERSION 0.1 -> 0.2). Its own
LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS (13 rows, larger: 55/70/90-height, 34/42/54-font,
sized for the full 1920x1080 canvas rather than squeezed into
MainScreen's smaller box) and its own _showLyricsWindow()/
_hideLyricsWindow() -- deliberately duplicated rather than shared with
MainScreen's own version: the two screens' widget names, scaling and
skin are otherwise unrelated, and the algorithm itself is short enough
that a shared abstraction would add more indirection than it saves.
Now takes the shared InformationPanel instance directly (passed by
MainScreen when opening it) instead of a pre-resolved text block, and
runs its own 1-second eTimer (matching MainScreen's own refresh
cadence) calling getCurrentLyricsWindowData() each tick -- the single
source of truth, never recomputed here. Falls back to the original
round-87 static "content" Label display when that returns None (no
live position available at all). The refresh timer is explicitly
stopped in closePressed() (same try/except pattern MainScreen's own
closePlayer() already uses for its own refresh timer) so it can't fire
callbacks against a closed screen.

Key handling changed, per direct request ("Muutetaan kokoruudun
tilassa että nuoli ylos/alas ei palauta takaisin vaan muuttaa
sanoitusten ajoitusta, kuten ikkunoidussa tilassa"): UP/DOWN now call
information_panel.scroll(-1)/scroll(1) -- the exact same method the
windowed panel's own UP/DOWN already calls, which already knows to
route to offset-adjustment instead of ordinary scrolling while a live
lyrics page is active (round 4, Build 0009) -- reused directly rather
than duplicating that routing logic. OK/EXIT/LEFT/RIGHT still close
the screen. MainScreen's own _showLyricsFullscreen() updated to pass
self._information_panel through to the new constructor signature.

### Testing

Confirmed all four touched/rewritten files' own syntax with
ast.parse() and py_compile; case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep
clean on all (each file's own single flagged name is the same
pre-existing except-clause false positive seen throughout this
project). Extracted and validated both MainScreen's and
LyricsFullscreenScreen's own _buildSkin() methods with mocked
dependencies, parsing the output at HD and SD (both screens don't
branch on Light/Dark for the lyrics rows themselves, so this covers
the combinations that matter) -- confirmed all 13 lyrics_line_N
widgets are generated and that row 6 specifically uses the Bold font
family in both. Swept both for "--" inside "<!-- -->" comments (only
the same pre-existing Python-comment false positive already known from
other files).

Ran five separate simulations rather than trusting the structure
alone: (1) lyrics_manager.py's own padded windowing against song
middle/start/end/unsynchronized/backward-compat-string scenarios --
confirmed current_index is always exactly window_size // 2 regardless
of song position, confirmed correct blank-padding at the edges,
confirmed getScrollWindow()'s string output still matches the new
structured data joined with "\n". (2) The runtime font-tiering
algorithm (shared logic between MainScreen and LyricsFullscreenScreen)
against synchronized/unsynchronized/padded-lines scenarios with mock
widget objects -- confirmed row 6 gets Bold+30 only when synchronized,
confirmed every row correctly forces down to Regular+20 when not, and
confirmed missing/padded lines render as blank text without breaking
the font logic. (3) InformationPanel's own new title-matching glue
logic, via a minimal stand-in replicating that exact code (the full
module couldn't be imported standalone in this sandbox -- it pulls in
real Enigma2 Components.config, unlike lyrics_manager.py) -- confirmed
it returns structured window data when the Lyrics page is active and
correctly returns None the moment the active page changes to Metadata,
without needing to reset any stashed state explicitly (title-matching
alone prevents stale-data leakage into the wrong page).

Not yet confirmed on a real device -- this is the single largest UI
change of this whole feature arc, and the next test should specifically
check: the current line's row never moves as a synced track plays;
prev/next/current are visibly different sizes; a .txt-lyrics track
shows uniform font sizing (no accidental bigger/bold rows); the
fullscreen view live-updates in step with the windowed panel; and
UP/DOWN in fullscreen changes the lyrics offset (check the windowed
panel's own title suffix, e.g. "+1s", to confirm) rather than closing
the screen.

One new user-facing string ("OK / EXIT / LEFT / RIGHT: Close -- UP /
DOWN: Adjust timing") replaced the old fullscreen hint text (no longer
referenced anywhere) in both .po files; recompiled with msgfmt, counts
still matching between fi/en (318 msgid lines each, net unchanged --
one string swapped for another); mojibake sweep clean.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 95 (2026-09-03) -- lyrics fullscreen background fix, MainMenu title z-order bug, MediaPlayer3 branding + clock on MainScreen

Four screenshots: two of LyricsFullscreenScreen showing the lyrics
area overlapping the title area at the top and the content running a
bit too far down at the bottom, and two of MainMenu. User diagnosed
the fullscreen issue as a background-image mismatch and suggested a
dedicated image; separately asked for "MediaPlayer3" branding at the
top of both MainScreen and MainMenu, a clock in MainScreen's top-right,
and reported MainMenu's own title flashing on open then disappearing
behind its background.

### Round 94's reused mainmenu_background.png didn't fit LyricsFullscreenScreen

Rendered and inspected mainmenu_background.png directly (PIL) rather
than guessing: it's a specific image with a hamburger icon baked into
its own header bar and play/exit icons baked into its own footer bar
-- both meaningful for MainMenu's own hints, meaningless (and visually
colliding with this screen's own title/content positions) for Lyrics
fullscreen. Pixel-scanned the reference image's own card boundaries
and sampled its exact colours (page background, card background,
header-bar tint, for both light and dark) rather than guessing hex
values, then used PIL to generate a dedicated lyrics_fullscreen_
background.png (all four variants: light/dark x hd/sd) with card
boundaries computed to match LyricsFullscreenScreen's OWN actual
widget positions (title at y=30-90, lyrics rows at y=110-890, hint at
y=1025-1065) instead of MainMenu's, and no icons at all. Swapped the
image path in _decodeBackgroundImage().

### MainMenu: title flashing then disappearing behind its own background

Root cause: MainMenu's "title" widget already existed and already said
"MediaPlayer3" (not missing, as first suspected) -- it's created before
the background image's own ePicLoad decode completes, so it's briefly
visible, but Enigma2's own asynchronous setPixmap() on an already-
shown "background" widget can restack it above widgets added earlier
in Python, regardless of creation order. Fixed by adding zPosition="-1"
to the "background" widget -- an explicit, permanent z-order pin,
reusing the exact same technique this project already used for
MainScreen's own cover-art-as-background widget (Build 0005, "zPosition
-1, behind all text") rather than inventing a new approach for what
is fundamentally the same class of race.

### MainScreen: MediaPlayer3 branding + clock

Added "app_branding" (static "MediaPlayer3" text) and "clock" (HH:MM,
updated every tick in _onRefreshTimer(), matching everything else this
screen already refreshes on the same 1-second cadence) to the Player
panel's own top row -- the header bar already spans the full screen
width in the background image with nothing to its right
(mainscreen_player_active.png), so no other widget needed to move.

### A self-caught mistake: -- inside XML comments, twice

While writing the explanatory comments for both the MainMenu z-order
fix and the MainScreen branding/clock addition, used " -- " as an
em-dash inside two separate <!-- --> blocks -- exactly the documented
"crashes Enigma2 at startup" landmine this project has flagged
throughout its own history. Caught both with a precise regex sweep
(re.finditer(r'<!--(.*?)-->', ..., re.DOTALL) checking for "--" inside
the captured group) rather than the plainer grep pattern used in
earlier rounds, which is more reliable for catching real instances
without false-positiving on Python comments that merely mention the
rule. Fixed both (rewordered to avoid the double-hyphen) and re-ran
the same precise sweep across all three touched files to confirm.

### Testing

Confirmed all three files' own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on all three (each
file's own single pre-existing except-clause false positive). Ran the
mandatory skin-XML validation (mocked dependencies) at Light/Dark x
HD/SD for MainScreen and MainMenu, and at HD/SD for LyricsFullscreenScreen
(no Light/Dark branching in its own background path logic) -- all
combinations passed; additionally confirmed app_branding/clock widgets
are present in MainScreen's generated XML and zPosition="-1" is present
on MainMenu's background widget. Verified all four new PNG assets
open correctly via PIL at their expected sizes (1920x1080 hd,
1000x562 sd). Sanity-checked time.strftime("%H:%M")'s own output
format. Not yet confirmed on a real device.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 97 (2026-09-03) -- found the real source of MainMenu's redundant title text; lyrics fullscreen title styling

## Round 99 (2026-09-03) -- device-freeze fix, Settings cleanup, loop playlist, Browser directory picker, unified MainScreen OK menu

Two attachments (a device log and a full list of new Settings help
texts) plus a large, multi-part request: MainScreen's OK menu unified
across both states; a real device freeze in Settings when typing on
the Startup directory field; the Music Library/Startup directory
fields should only ever be set via the file Browser, never typed;
Theme removed entirely (every screen now has its own background
image); several other settings removed; "Resume playback (future)"
replaced with an actual "return to start of playlist" feature; all
Settings help texts updated from a user-supplied list; Browser's own
directory menu needs "Set as startup directory"/"Set as Music Library
directory".

### Device freeze: root-caused from a full stack-trace dump at hang time, not guessed

The attached log's own crash dump showed the frozen main thread's
exact call stack: SettingsScreen.keyOK() -> _openDirectoryPicker() ->
LocationBox.__init__() -> FileList.changeDir() -> getMountpoint() ->
posixpath.realpath(), hung. The underlying config (cfg.general.
startup_directory) is a plain ConfigText, which Enigma2's own
ConfigListScreen lets a user edit directly -- number keys route
straight to the focused ConfigText's own inline text-edit, bypassing
keyOK() entirely, letting an arbitrary invalid value get typed in
(matches the user's own report: pressing "1" on that row). Fixed at
both ends: _openDirectoryPicker() now validates the stored value
(os.path.isabs() + os.path.isdir()) before ever handing it to a
directory browser, falling back to default_media_directory()
otherwise; and a new keyNumberGlobal() override ignores number keys
entirely on these two fields (_directoryPickerTarget() already existed
to identify them), so a value can no longer get corrupted that way in
the first place.

### Settings screen: opens Browser as a plain directory picker instead of Enigma2's LocationBox

Per direct follow-up request, replaced LocationBox with this project's
own BrowserScreen, opened via a new keyword: playback_controller is
now Optional (default None) on BrowserScreen.__init__() -- Settings
has no PlaybackController of its own to pass, and there's nothing to
play while picking a directory anyway. Play/"Add to playlist" are
left out of both the directory and file OK menus whenever self.
_playback is None (checked once at menu-build time, not guarded at
each of the 5 existing self._playback call sites individually) rather
than passing a dummy/throwaway PlaybackController, which risked
unwanted side effects from instantiating real playback machinery just
to browse. SettingsScreen's own _directoryPicked()/LocationBox-specific
code is gone; BrowserScreen's own new "Set as startup directory"/"Set
as Music Library directory" actions (see below) write straight to the
same config_manager both screens already share, so no callback value
needs to flow back -- Settings just refreshes its own list on return.

### Browser: "Set as startup directory"/"Set as Music Library directory" added to the directory OK menu

Available on any directory, not just when opened as a picker from
Settings -- per direct request, this also works during normal
browsing, letting the user configure either directory setting from
wherever they already are in the file tree, without a detour through
Settings at all. Writes directly to cfg.general.startup_directory/
cfg.library.scan_directory and saves, with a brief confirmation
message.

### Settings list: Theme, and five other settings, removed

Theme and its own conditional "Custom background color" entry
removed entirely -- every screen now has its own background image,
making the old theme system moot. Also removed (per the user's own
annotated list): Radio navigation mode, Show progress bar, Show
elapsed time, Show remaining time, Show playback state. Scoped to the
Settings UI list only -- the underlying config attributes and any
downstream reads are untouched, avoiding a much larger, riskier
change than what was actually asked for.

### "Resume playback (future)" replaced with a real "Return to start of playlist" feature

cfg.playback.resume_playback (a ConfigYesNo that was never actually
read anywhere -- confirmed by grep before touching it) renamed to
cfg.playback.loop_playlist and wired up for real in PlaybackController.
_handleTrackFinished(): when Automatic Next Track is on but there's no
next track (the playlist just finished) and this new setting is also
on, loops back to track 1 (self._playIndex(0)) instead of stopping.
Directly simulated the branching logic against four scenarios (last
track with looping on, last track with looping off, a track that
isn't last, and looping on but Auto Next itself off) before trusting
it -- confirmed looping only ever triggers when both settings are on
and the playlist has actually ended, never interferes with ordinary
next-track advancement.

### All Settings help texts rewritten from the user's own supplied list

Updated the entire _SETTING_DESCRIPTIONS dict (the mechanism already
existed, feeding the bottom info panel) to match the user's own
wording exactly, translated into new English msgids with the user's
own Finnish text as the .po translation. Caught and corrected one own
mistake before it shipped: an early draft swapped the "Radio default
country" and "Radio default language" descriptions relative to each
other -- re-checked against the user's own original pairing (by
tracing which Finnish label the description text was actually written
under) and fixed the mapping before finalizing.

### MainScreen: unified OK menu across Player and Information panels

_openPlayerActionMenu()/_openInformationMenu() (and their own separate
_playerActionMenuChosen()/_informationMenuChosen() dispatchers) merged
into one _openUnifiedActionMenu()/_unifiedActionMenuChosen() pair, per
direct request. Back/Stop-Resume (and the radio-only Clear history/
Favorites items, unchanged from before) are only included when self.
_playback.hasMedia() -- meaningless otherwise, and now reachable from
the Information panel even before anything has ever played, which the
previous, separate _openInformationMenu() never had to consider. Show
lyrics/Show cover art fullscreen are always offered regardless, since

scenarios (local music, radio with favorites-eligible station,

### Testing

## Round 101 (2026-09-03) -- real crash fix: MemoryError loading a large station database

Device log's own full traceback pinpointed the exact crash: entering
Internet Radio called _performInitialLoad() -> getStationDatabaseInfo()
-> _loadStationDatabase() -> _loadJSON() -> json.load(), which raised
a bare MemoryError -- not caught by _loadJSON()'s own existing
except clause (OSError, ValueError only), so it propagated all the
way up and crashed the whole screen. Most likely cause: the local
station database has grown very large under an unlimited (radio.
search_limit = 0, round 68) station-count setting -- round 69's own
log showed a prior test downloading 58374 stations, and a JSON file
that size can plausibly exceed available memory on a constrained
receiver during parsing (json.load() builds the entire structure in
memory at once).

### Fix

Added a dedicated `except MemoryError` branch to _loadJSON(), logged
distinctly from the existing OSError/ValueError case (so a future
device log makes this specific cause obvious at a glance, unlike a
plain "unable to read" message that wouldn't distinguish "file
missing" from "file too large to parse"), returning the same
`default` value already used for other read failures. All three of
_loadJSON()'s own call sites (favorites, history, and the station
database) share this one method, so all three are protected by the
same fix. _loadStationDatabase()'s own caller already treats a missing/
empty database gracefully (RadioBrowserScreen's _performInitialLoad()
offers a fresh download via _offerDatabaseDownload() when count == 0)
-- no changes needed there, confirmed by reading it end to end rather
than assumed.

Deliberately did not impose a new cap on the "unlimited" radio.search_
limit setting itself, since that was round 68's own explicit,
direct request -- reintroducing a silent cap would contradict it
without being asked. This fix only prevents the CRASH; if the same
oversized database gets downloaded again, the app will now show "no
stations, download again?" instead of freezing/crashing, but won't
actually succeed at loading a database that's genuinely too large for
the device's memory. Flagged for the user: if this keeps recurring,
lowering "Radioasemien raja" to a smaller number (rather than 0)
avoids hitting the device's own memory limit at all.

### Testing

Confirmed the file's own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean (the usual pre-existing
except-clause false positive). Directly simulated the fix against
three scenarios via a mocked json.load(): raising MemoryError
(confirmed the default value comes back instead of the exception
propagating), a normal valid file (confirmed unaffected), and a
missing file (confirmed the existing OSError path still works
unchanged). Not yet confirmed on a real device against the user's own
actual (evidently very large) station database file.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

## Round 102 (2026-09-03) -- new translations: Swedish, German, Spanish

User confirmed round 101's diagnosis was correct in practice: it
crashed once more, but deleting the stored settings/database directory
and letting the app re-fetch stations from scratch resolved it (a
fresh, smaller database loaded fine). No new log attached, and no
further action requested on that front -- moved on to the next
feature: full Swedish, German and Spanish translations, alongside the
existing Finnish/English catalogs.

### Translated all 348 user-facing strings into three new languages

Extracted the exact, ordered list of every msgid from the existing
English catalog programmatically (not retyped by hand, to avoid
transcription drift) and translated all 348 into Swedish, German and
Spanish, written first as three standalone Python dictionaries so
each could be verified complete (every one of the 348 required keys
present, none missing) before ever being turned into a .po file.
Generated all three .po files from those dictionaries plus the
existing English file's own structure, rather than writing .po syntax
by hand for over a thousand lines.

### Two issues caught before finalizing, one an editing mistake

The German dictionary's own translation for "No music library
available...\\n\\nUse Library Update from the menu..." used literal,
unescaped double quotes around "Bibliothek aktualisieren" -- broke
.po syntax (msgfmt's own compiler caught it immediately with a clear
line/column). Fixed by escaping them properly, matching how the
English original itself already had none needing escaping (a case
this project's own transcription check hadn't been exercised against
before, since Finnish and English happened not to need mid-string
quotes anywhere in the existing catalog).

Also wrote a dedicated placeholder-consistency check across all 1044
translations (348 strings x 3 languages) rather than trusting the
translations by inspection alone: extracted every %s/%d/{0}/{1}
occurrence from each English msgid and its own translation, comparing
the two sets -- zero mismatches found, meaning no translation
accidentally dropped, duplicated, or reordered a format placeholder
(any of which would have caused a real runtime error the very first
time that string was formatted, not just a translation quality issue).

### Wiring: two separate, previously-undiscovered hardcoded language lists, both needed updating

config.py's own `_AVAILABLE_LANGUAGE_CODES` (round 65) gates Settings'
own Language choices; a second, entirely separate copy --
localization.py's own `AVAILABLE_LANGUAGES` -- is what
LocalizationManager itself actually checks before trying to load a
catalog at all. Both needed the three new codes added, or the new
catalogs would sit on disk but never actually load (Settings would
offer to select Swedish, but LocalizationManager would silently
reject it and fall back to English). Found by grep rather than
assuming config.py's own list was the only one -- confirmed correct
by reading LocalizationManager.setLanguage()'s own logic end to end.
Corrected localization.py's own stale comment while there too (claimed
"no source changes required" to add a language, which was never
actually true given the hardcoded tuple check right below it) and
cross-referenced the two lists in each other's own comments so a
future round updating one doesn't forget the other.

### Testing

Confirmed both touched Python files' own syntax with ast.parse() and
py_compile; case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on both
(each file's own single pre-existing except-clause false positive).
Compiled all three new .po files with msgfmt (one real syntax error
caught and fixed, see above); counts matching across all five
languages now shipped (fi/en/sv/de/es, 349 msgid lines each); mojibake
sweep clean on all three new files (covering the wider set of
accented-character corruption patterns relevant to German/Spanish,
not just the Finnish ä/ö this project's sweep has checked for until
now). Spot-checked a sample of strings resolve correctly via gettext
in all three new languages, including one with a format placeholder.
Confirmed LOCALE_PATH points to the exact directory the three new
sv/de/es subdirectories were created under. Not yet confirmed on a
real device -- the next test should select each new language from
Settings and confirm the interface actually switches (this exercises
the full pipeline: config storage, LocalizationManager's own catalog
loading, and the translations themselves).
