## Round 103 (2026-09-04) -- fixed the VK bug at its actual root, localized Yes/No, dark-theme colour mismatch, Settings info-text lifecycle timing

Two screenshots confirmed Swedish translations work well, but raised
four further issues: the virtual keyboard still appears on Settings'
top two rows (despite rounds 99 and 100's own fixes) and reportedly on
Radio's own default-language/country rows too; ConfigYesNo's own
"Kyllä"/"Ei" labels don't follow the app's own language setting; the
Settings info text still isn't visible; and lyrics fullscreen's dark
theme has a text/background colour mismatch.

### The virtual keyboard bug, fixed at its actual root this time

Rounds 99 and 100 guarded keyOK()/keyNumberGlobal()/keyLeft()/
keyRight() one at a time, each time chasing whichever entry point the
previous fix hadn't covered -- and a real device screenshot proved
neither fix actually worked. Recognised this as chasing symptoms
rather than the cause: a plain ConfigText's own handleKey() is what
Enigma2's ConfigList calls for ANY key pressed on the selected item,
regardless of which screen-level method happened to route there
first (including, evidently, paths this project's own guards hadn't
found yet). Added ConfigBrowseOnlyText(ConfigText), overriding
handleKey() itself as a no-op -- removes the capability at its
source, for every key and every screen, rather than continuing to
guard individual entry points. Applied to all four fields that are
only ever meant to be set through a picker flow: general.
startup_directory, library.scan_directory, radio.default_country,
radio.default_language -- the last two being the "radio's own
language-selection rows" from the report, confirmed to be plain
ConfigText too (round 100's own OK-based set-default flow never
changed their underlying type). Direct .value assignment (how every
picker flow already sets these) is untouched -- only handleKey()
itself, invoked purely by remote-key input, is now a no-op.

### ConfigYesNo's labels didn't follow the app's own language

Plain ConfigYesNo resolves its own "yes"/"no" labels via Enigma2's
global language setting, fixed once at object-construction time
(module import) -- entirely independent of, and earlier than,
MediaPlayer3's own Settings -> Language, and never live-updates when
that changes. Added ConfigYesNoLocalized(ConfigYesNo), overriding
getText() to use this project's own _() (always live, never cached)
instead; self.value itself untouched, so every existing `if cfg.xxx.
value:` check elsewhere keeps working unchanged. All 15 ConfigYesNo(
call sites in config.py switched to the new class via a single
targeted find-and-replace (constructor calls only, matched by the
"= ConfigYesNo(" prefix so the class definition line itself wasn't
touched) rather than one at a time. Added "Yes"/"No" to all five
language catalogs (fi/en/sv/de/es).

### Settings info text: added a second trigger point rather than guessing a fix blind

Investigated again rather than assuming last round's "no bug found"
conclusion was the end of it. Found MainScreen already has an
established, working precedent for exactly this class of problem:
self.onShown.append(self._onShown), used because some widgets' own
methods (here, the "config" list's getCurrent()) aren't reliable
until Enigma2 has actually attached the screen's widgets, which
hasn't happened yet at __init__() time. Added the same pattern:
self.onShown.append(self._updateSettingInfo), re-running it once the
screen is actually shown, on top of the existing __init__-time call
(kept, since it's harmless and covers screens/receivers where the
timing isn't an issue).

### Lyrics fullscreen: dark theme colour mismatch, found and fixed

Confirmed from PANEL_BACKGROUND_COLOR/PANEL_TEXT_COLOR's own values in
skin.py: they're fixed light-theme constants with no dark variant at
all. Round 97 already made the "title" widget theme-aware
(title_foreground_color, computed from self._skin_variant) but never
touched "content"/lyrics_line_N/"hint", which still used these fixed
light values regardless of theme -- while round 95's own background
image generation DID correctly sample separate light/dark colours,
creating exactly the mismatch reported (a correctly dark background
behind still-light text boxes). Fixed by computing panel_background_
color/panel_text_color from self._skin_variant too, using the exact
same dark values (#1C202B/#F0F0F0) MainScreen's own MAINSCREEN_SKIN_
PALETTES["dark"] already uses, so every window agrees on what "dark
theme" looks like.

### Testing

Confirmed all three touched files' own syntax with ast.parse() and
py_compile; case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on all
(each file's own single pre-existing except-clause false positive);
precise "--"-inside-XML-comment sweep clean on all three. Directly
simulated ConfigBrowseOnlyText's own handleKey() override against
three scenarios via a mock base class (a plain ConfigText's handleKey
mutating its value as the original bug would, the override leaving
the value completely untouched across several different key presses,
and direct .value assignment still working normally) -- confirmed the
override achieves exactly what's needed without needing real
Enigma2 Components.config available in this sandbox. Re-ran the
mandatory skin-XML validation for lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py at both
Light and Dark x HD/SD (all four passed) and additionally confirmed
the generated XML actually contains the new dark-theme hex values
when dark is selected, and the original light-theme values when it
isn't. Added "Yes"/"No" to all five language catalogs, recompiled
with msgfmt, counts matching across all five (351 msgid lines each),
mojibake sweep clean, spot-checked gettext resolution in fi/sv/de/es.
Not yet confirmed on a real device -- particularly the ConfigBrowseOnlyText
fix, since this is the third attempt at the same underlying bug and
the previous two both looked correct on paper but didn't hold up on
real hardware.

## Round 104 (2026-09-04) -- corrected misdiagnosis (key hint bar, not VK), fixed the real "unlimited for own language" bug, radio defaults navigate to Internet Radio

User corrected their own earlier report: what looked like a virtual
keyboard is actually a persistent key-hint overlay tied to the
selected row's own declared element TYPE, not something that requires
a key press to appear -- round 103's ConfigBrowseOnlyText fix (which
only touches handleKey()) was never going to remove it, since the
hint doesn't depend on what handleKey() does. Also reported: Settings'
own Yes/No values still needed checking (already fixed in round 103,
before this report), Internet Radio's own "unlimited for own
language" doesn't actually find all stations in practice (limit=20000
+ unlimited-for-own-language still missed real Finnish stations), and
proposed a specific two-pass fix.

### Root-caused and fixed the real "unlimited for own language" bug

Grepped every reference to radio.unlimited_for_own_language and found
it was only ever read in radiobrowserscreen.py's own interactive
search -- never in internetradio_manager.py's own updateStationDatabase(),
which is what actually builds the local database everything else
reads from. That method's own main pass orders strictly by global
clickcount, so once target_limit is smaller than RadioBrowser's own
worldwide count, plenty of real, lower-popularity stations in the
user's own language never make the cut at all -- exactly matching the
report. Implemented the user's own proposed fix directly: a second,
exhaustive (no cap) pass fetching every station for radio.default_
language and/or radio.default_country if either is set, falling back
to the app's own UI language if neither is -- merged into the main
pass's own results with stationuuid-based deduplication. Moved the
app-language-to-RadioBrowser-name mapping (previously a class
attribute duplicated nowhere else) to internetradio_manager.py's own
module level so both this new pass and RadioBrowserScreen's own
_promoteAppLanguage() share one definition -- and added the sv/de/es
entries that mapping had never gotten after round 102 introduced those
languages (found while moving it, not otherwise reported).

### Radio's own default country/language rows now open Internet Radio

Per direct request: OK on these two rows in Settings now opens
RadioBrowserScreen directly (session.open(RadioBrowserScreen, None))
instead of falling through to ConfigListScreen's own default
handling -- the actual setting only ever happens there anyway (round
100's own "Set as Radio default language/country"), so Settings just
navigates to where that's actually done, the same way it already
navigates to a directory browser for the two directory fields rather
than replicating a picker inline.

### Attempted fix for the persistent key-hint bar: ConfigSelection instead of ConfigText

Per direct suggestion ("Käynnistyshakemiston kohdan voisi muuttaa
tavalliseksi tekstiksi"): new ConfigBrowsePath(ConfigSelection),
posing as a single-choice selector instead of a ConfigText -- a
categorically different type to Enigma2, which round 103's own
ConfigBrowseOnlyText couldn't be, since it still inherited ConfigText's
own type identity regardless of the handleKey() override. Applied to
all four picker-only fields (general.startup_directory, library.
scan_directory, radio.default_country, radio.default_language).
Explicitly documented as a best-effort attempt based on general
Enigma2 knowledge rather than something this project could verify
directly -- the exact mechanism behind the hint bar isn't something
a device log or static reading can confirm. setValue() re-runs
ConfigSelection.__init__() with the new single choice rather than
poking at internal attributes directly, favouring a more robust
(if slightly unusual) update path over guessing exactly which
attributes back the real class's own choices/default state.

### Testing

## Round 105 (2026-09-04) -- real crash fix: ConfigBrowsePath's own construction pattern broke config_manager.set()'s validation

User confirmed round 104's ConfigBrowsePath fix actually worked for
the key-hint bar (Startup directory now works correctly, no hint
shown) -- but setting Radio's own default language via OK now crashed
the app when confirming "Aseta radion oletuskieleksi: finnish?" with
Yes (No worked fine, since nothing gets set then).

### Root-caused from a full stack-trace dump, not guessed

The device log's own traceback pinpointed the exact line:
config.py's own validateValue(), `return value in [choice[0] for
choice in entry.choices]`, raised IndexError: string index out of
range. Root cause: Enigma2's own real ConfigSelection collapses a
(value, description) choice tuple into a bare string internally
whenever value == description -- exactly what ConfigBrowsePath
(round 104) always passes (e.g. choices=[("", "")] before any real
value has ever been set, or choices=[("finnish", "finnish")] once
set). entry.choices then yields plain strings instead of tuples for
those entries; validateValue()'s own old code indexed into that
STRING's own first CHARACTER (choice[0]) instead of a tuple's first
ELEMENT -- "" has no first character at all, hence the crash on the
very first attempt (before any value existed yet).

Confirmed why only the Radio rows crashed and not the directory
fields, which round 104 itself reported working correctly:
BrowserScreen's own _setConfigDirectory() sets `.value` directly
(ConfigBrowsePath's own setValue() property, no validation involved
at all), while RadioBrowserScreen's own _setAsRadioDefaultConfirmed()
goes through config_manager.set()'s own higher-level wrapper, which
calls validateValue() before ever assigning anything -- the only path
that could actually reach the broken code.

### Fix

validateValue()'s own ConfigSelection branch now handles both shapes:
`choice[0] if isinstance(choice, (tuple, list)) else choice`, safe for
any ConfigSelection regardless of whether its own choices happen to
collapse this way or not -- not just a workaround specific to
ConfigBrowsePath's own construction pattern. Grepped the whole
codebase for other `.choices` usages that might share the same
tuple-only assumption -- found none outside this one method.

### Testing

Confirmed config.py's own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean (the usual pre-existing
except-clause false positive); precise "--"-inside-XML-comment sweep
clean. Directly simulated the fixed logic against four scenarios: the
normal tuple-shaped case every other ConfigSelection in this project
already uses (confirmed unaffected); the exact collapsed-empty-string
case that crashed on a real device (confirmed no IndexError, correct
True/False results); a collapsed real-value case (a language already
set once); and a defensively mixed list of both shapes in one
(confirmed no crash either way). Not yet confirmed on a real device
against the exact original repro (setting a Radio default language/
country via OK and answering Yes) -- the next test should also verify
the value actually gets stored and reflected back in Settings and in
Internet Radio's own promoted list-position-2 behaviour (round 100).

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23.

---

## Round 106 (2026-09-04) -- second, deeper crash-adjacent bug found while investigating a repeat report; "Kaikki" (clear) option added

Two log files uploaded, both identical, reporting the same crash as
round 105's own fix was meant to resolve. Compared the traceback's
own line 968 code against the current codebase directly: the log
showed the exact single-line pre-fix code (`return value in
[choice[0] for choice in entry.choices]`), not round 105's own
multi-line isinstance-guarded version -- confirmed the fix is
genuinely still present in the current source, strongly suggesting
the device had not yet been updated to the round-105 build at the
time of this specific test, rather than the fix itself failing.
Flagged this to the user directly rather than assuming the fix was
wrong.

### A second, more fundamental problem found while re-verifying round 105's own fix, not by inspection alone

While tracing through whether round 105's fix would actually let
config_manager.set() succeed for a genuinely NEW value (not just avoid
crashing), realised it wouldn't: ConfigBrowsePath's own .choices
always contains exactly the CURRENT value and nothing else (setValue()
replaces the whole list every time) -- so validateValue()'s general
ConfigSelection check ("is the new value already among the current
choices") can never pass for an actual change. Round 105 stopped the
crash, but never actually let a new value through either --
config_manager.set() would have silently rejected any different value
and kept the old one, logging "Invalid configuration value" -- meaning
Radio's own default language/country could plausibly never have
actually been settable via config_manager.set() at all since round
104 introduced ConfigBrowsePath, only round 100's own original
ConfigText-based version (before round 104) ever really worked this
way. Confirmed the two directory fields were never affected, since
BrowserScreen's own _setConfigDirectory() sets .value directly,
bypassing config_manager.set()/validateValue() entirely.

Fixed by special-casing ConfigBrowsePath in validateValue() BEFORE the
general ConfigSelection branch: validated the same way ConfigText
already is (any string acceptable), since ConfigBrowsePath is really a
disguised free-text field -- ConfigSelection is only used for its
side effect of avoiding Enigma2's own text-entry hint bar (confirmed
actually working per the user's own round-105 report), not for
genuine fixed-choice semantics.

### New feature: "Kaikki" (Any) now offers clearing the default

Per direct request ("Molemmissa olisi hyvä jos voisi valita myös
kohdan kaikki, jos haluaa esim. selata vain toisella rajoituksella"):
_offerSetAsRadioDefault() no longer excludes index 0 ("Any") --
selecting it now offers "Clear Radio default language/country"
(setting the value back to ""), letting the user restrict a search by
only one of language/region while leaving the other unset, without a
detour through Settings to blank it out by hand. Two new translated
strings added to all five language catalogs.

### Testing

Confirmed both touched files' own syntax with ast.parse() and
py_compile; case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on both
(each file's own single pre-existing except-clause false positive);
precise "--"-inside-XML-comment sweep clean on both. Directly

returned False; clearing to an empty string -- confirmed True; a real

## Round 108 (2026-09-04) -- the real reason Settings' own info text never showed, across three earlier failed attempts

User confirmed 1.0.0 is out and everything works well, but the
Settings info text (reported and investigated twice before, rounds
100 and 103, neither actually fixing it) still doesn't show. Also
supplied a corrected version of the help-texts list: the two Radio
default descriptions now say "2. sijalla" (2nd position) instead of
"1. sijalla", matching round 100/104's own actual promoted-to-
position-2 behaviour, and the four settings marked "<--poistetaan"
are simply gone from the list now (already removed, round 98).

### Root-caused for real this time, with a working reproduction

Reconsidered the problem from scratch rather than re-checking the
same call-timing theory a third time (round 103's own onShown fix
addressed a real, but apparently not the actual, timing concern).
Found that `_SETTING_DESCRIPTIONS` was a CLASS-level dict --
`_SETTING_DESCRIPTIONS = {_("Startup directory"): _(...), ...}`
sitting directly in the class body, not inside any method. Every
`_("...")` call in a class-level attribute like this runs exactly
ONCE, at module-import time (when settingsscreen.py is first
imported, early in plugin startup) -- before LocalizationManager has
necessarily settled on the user's own saved language, and never again
afterwards even when the language is changed live later (this
project's own established, no-restart-needed design). `_buildList()`'s
own labels, by contrast, call `_("...")` fresh every time it runs
(inside `__init__`, by which point the correct language is already
loaded) -- so the label actually shown in the list and this dict's
own keys could end up frozen in two different languages, and
`.get(label)` could never find a match. This is structurally
different from (and the real explanation neither of) round 100's own
brief look or round 103's own onShown investigation actually
addressed -- both were reasoning about WHEN the lookup runs, not
WHETHER the dictionary itself was ever going to contain matching keys
in the first place.

### Fix

Converted `_SETTING_DESCRIPTIONS` from a class-level dict into
`_buildSettingDescriptions(self)`, a method returning a freshly-built
dict on every call -- exactly like `_buildList()` itself already does
for the labels, so both are always evaluated in whatever language is
currently active. `_updateSettingInfo()` now calls this method once
per invocation instead of referencing the (removed) class attribute.
Grepped the whole file afterward to confirm no other reference to the
old attribute name remained.

### Testing

Confirmed the file's own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile after
the refactor (a dict literal spanning multiple lines uses bracket-
continuation rules, not block-indentation rules, so the visual
indentation mismatch between the old class-attribute style and the
new method-body style compiles fine regardless -- verified directly
rather than assumed); case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean
(the usual pre-existing except-clause false positive). Wrote a
targeted simulation reproducing the exact bug mechanism with a mock
translation function that can change languages live, matching how the
real localization_manager behaves: confirmed the OLD (class-level
dict) pattern genuinely fails to find a match once the active
language differs from whatever was active at class-definition time,
and confirmed the NEW (per-call method) pattern correctly matches
regardless of how many times the simulated language changes
afterward -- this is the first round on this specific bug that
actually reproduced the failure mode directly rather than reasoning
about the code by inspection alone.

Also corrected the two Radio default country/language help
descriptions from "shows first" to "shows second... right after
'Any'", matching the user's own corrected reference text and the
actual round 100/104 behaviour (promoted to position 2, since "Any"
always occupies position 1). Two new strings added to all five
language catalogs, recompiled with msgfmt, counts matching (355
msgid lines each), mojibake sweep clean, spot-checked the corrected
Finnish string resolves exactly matching the user's own supplied
wording.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23 -- this one is 1.0.001, the first package under
the new versioning convention established in round 107.

---

## Round 109 (2026-09-04) -- info text stuck on the first row, never updating on UP/DOWN

A screenshot showed round 108's own fix partially working: the info
text now shows real content (no longer blank), but it's stuck showing
the FIRST row's own description ("Käynnistyshakemisto"'s text) even
though "Language" (a different row) is the one actually highlighted
in the list.

### Diagnosis

selectionChanged() (calling _updateSettingInfo() correctly, confirmed
again by re-reading it) is the method meant to fire whenever the
highlighted row changes, via ConfigListScreen's own internal
onSelectionChanged wiring on the "config" widget. The screenshot's own
evidence -- correct text for the very first row (set once via
__init__/onShown, round 103's own fix), never updating afterward even
though the visual highlight itself clearly does move (confirmed by
the highlighted "Language" row in the screenshot) -- points at
onSelectionChanged simply not firing on plain UP/DOWN navigation on
this receiver, despite being the standard, expected Enigma2 mechanism.
No existing precedent elsewhere in this codebase uses ConfigListScreen
to cross-check against (settingsscreen.py is the only file that does),
so this couldn't be verified against another already-working example
in this project.

### Fix

Added explicit keyUp()/keyDown() overrides, matching this class's own
existing keyLeft()/keyRight() pattern exactly: call ConfigListScreen's
own keyUp()/keyDown() first (preserving the actual row-navigation
behaviour), then explicitly call self._updateSettingInfo() afterward
-- guarantees the info text refreshes on every row change regardless
of whether onSelectionChanged's own wiring does on this particular
receiver. Confirmed neither method was already defined elsewhere in
the file (would have been silently shadowed otherwise) and that all
of keyUp/keyDown/keyLeft/keyRight/keyOK/selectionChanged now exist
exactly once each.

### Testing

Confirmed the file's own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean (the usual
pre-existing except-clause false positive). Verified no CH+/CH-
page-jump handling exists on this screen that would need the same
treatment (grepped for pageUp/pageDown/CH+/CH- -- none found, this
screen doesn't page). Not yet confirmed on a real device -- keyUp/
keyDown as ConfigListScreen's own override method names is standard,
well-established Enigma2 platform convention, but (like round 99's
own keyNumberGlobal guess) this project has no other ConfigListScreen-
based screen to cross-check the exact names against internally, so
this specific assumption should be verified on the next test.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23 -- this one is 1.0.002.

## Round 110 (2026-09-04) -- Test Skin: a safe, writable way to try a new skin

Per direct request: a new "Test Skin" system, letting someone try a
new background-image skin without touching the plugin's own installed
files -- files live in a writable .mediaplayer3/test_skin/ directory,
copied there automatically from a bundled template the first time the
program runs. Deleting/renaming that directory recovers Light
automatically. A follow-up question confirmed a specific piece of
this wasn't actually built yet (directory-just-created -> also reset
the Skin setting itself, not just the rendered fallback) -- implemented
directly once flagged, with a five-scenario simulation before trusting
it.

### Architecture

storage.py: added "test_skin" to StorageManager's own SUBDIRECTORIES
tuple (auto-created/verified the same way every other data directory
already is) and getTestSkinPath(). Added wasTestSkinJustCreated() --
a flag captured once, during _initialize(), before either subdirectory
state (existed already vs. just created) is possible to observe from
outside -- exposing this WITHOUT storage.py itself needing to know
anything about cfg.appearance.skin (deliberately kept out of this
module, matching its own stated "storage infrastructure only, no
application-specific data" boundary).

skin.py: new module-level resolve_skin_asset_path(variant, tier,
filename) -- the single place "test_skin" is special-cased (reads
from storage_manager.getTestSkinPath() instead of the plugin's own
bundled resources/skins/ tree, falling back to Light per file when a
specific file isn't there) instead of duplicating that logic across
every screen. All nine background-image screens (MainScreen, MainMenu,
Browser, Music Library, Playlist, Podcast, Internet Radio, Settings,
lyrics fullscreen) were each carrying their own inline `os.path.join
(SKIN_PATH, self._skin_variant, tier, filename)` -- all nine switched
to call this one shared function instead, and each of their own
per-screen skin-variant whitelist tuples (e.g. MAINSCREEN_SKIN_VARIANTS)
got "test_skin" added alongside "light"/"dark" so it's actually
accepted as a valid choice rather than silently falling back to the
tuple's own default.

plugin.py: new _copyTestSkinTemplate(), called from the startup
sequence whenever storage_manager.wasTestSkinJustCreated() is True --
copies the bundled resources/skins/test_skin_template/ (see below)
into the writable directory, then (per the follow-up question) also
resets appearance.skin away from "test_skin" if that was still the
saved choice, so Settings itself reflects reality rather than only
the rendered background silently falling back per file while Settings
still claims "Test Skin" is selected. Never raises -- a copy failure
just leaves test_skin empty, already handled safely by resolve_skin_
asset_path()'s own per-file fallback.

config.py: cfg.appearance.skin gained a fourth choice, ("test_skin",
"Test Skin"). Settings' own help description for "Skin" mentions it
and points at SKIN_ELEMENTS.md.

### Bundled template and reference document

resources/skins/test_skin_template/ -- an exact copy of Dark's own 36
PNGs (confirmed identical file listing to light/dark via direct
comparison, not assumed) plus its own skin.json ("Test Skin", font_
family "Bold" matching Light/Dark), as a working starting point rather
than an empty folder. Also ships SKIN_ELEMENTS.md, a reference
document covering every one of the nine screens: design canvas size,
exact hd/sd pixel dimensions (extracted directly from the real PNG
files with PIL, not guessed), which filenames are needed per active-
panel state, and a plain-language description of each screen's own
panel layout -- written for someone who wants to design new background
art without reading the plugin's own source code.

### A real side-discovery, noted but not acted on this round

While tracing skin_manager.getFont()'s own resolution path, found that
light/skin.json and dark/skin.json BOTH set "font_family": "Bold" --
meaning every "Regular" font lookup across the WHOLE app resolves to
Bold whenever either theme is active (confirmed plugin.py's own
startup calls skin_manager.loadSkin() with the user's actual saved
choice, not just the "default" skin loaded at SkinManager's own
__init__). This is likely a much larger contributor to the existing
OpenPLi Bold-font known issue than the handful of explicitly-hardcoded
"Bold" usages already documented -- flagged for a future round rather
than acted on now, since it wasn't what was asked and touches
something broader than this round's own scope.

### Testing

Confirmed all 13 touched Python files' own syntax with py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on all (the usual
pre-existing except-clause false positive, one file with none at
all); precise "--"-inside-XML-comment sweep clean on all 13. Directly
simulated the directory-just-created reset logic against five
scenarios (freshly created + was test_skin -> reset; freshly created
+ was already light/dark -> untouched; already existed + was test_skin
-> untouched) before trusting it, matching exactly what was asked.
Simulated _copyTestSkinTemplate()'s own copy logic against real
temporary directories (a fresh copy, and a second run to confirm
dirs_exist_ok=True doesn't crash on already-populated files).
Directly exercised resolve_skin_asset_path()'s own logic against the
real files on disk: confirmed light/dark resolve to real existing
files unchanged, confirmed test_skin's own per-file fallback to Light
actually finds a real file when the writable directory is empty, and
confirmed the bundled template's own file listing is byte-for-byte
identical (by set comparison) to light's/dark's for both hd/ and sd/.
Confirmed _buildSkin()'s own XML-generating structure is untouched by
this round (only the decode-time image-path call changed in each
screen, not skin XML generation itself) rather than re-deriving the
mandatory skin-XML validation from scratch for all nine screens. New
Skin-setting description string added to all five language catalogs,
recompiled with msgfmt, counts matching (356 msgid lines each),
mojibake sweep clean. Not yet confirmed on a real device -- this is a
substantial new subsystem touching 13 files; the next test should
cover a genuinely fresh install (confirming the template copy and
directory-creation timing), selecting Test Skin with the template
as-is, deleting the directory to confirm the Light recovery (both the
rendered fallback and the Settings value itself), and replacing one
PNG to confirm the per-file fallback still covers the rest.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23 -- this one is 1.0.003.

## Round 111 (2026-09-05) -- real bug: test_skin reset itself back to Light on every single launch

A device log showed the exact problem: every single MediaPlayer3
launch (not just the first) logged "Test Skin template copied" and
"test_skin directory was just (re)created; resetting appearance.skin
to light" -- confirmed by four separate "Starting MediaPlayer3" log
entries within about two minutes, each one repeating the same reset,
even though the directory genuinely only got created ONCE (a single
"[Storage] Directory (test_skin) created" line, well before any of
the repeated resets).

### Root cause

Round 110's own wasTestSkinJustCreated() cached a single boolean,
computed once during StorageManager.__init__() (a module-level
singleton, assumed constructed exactly once per Enigma2 session).
That assumption doesn't hold on the test receiver: its own plugin
loader apparently reconstructs this singleton fresh on every single
plugin launch, not just once per Enigma2 session -- so the cached
flag was stale (and wrongly True) before it was ever read, every
single time. This wasn't a timing bug within one run; it was a wrong
assumption about how many times __init__() itself would run over the
plugin's actual lifetime on this specific receiver.

Tracing this through surfaced a second, more serious consequence:
_copyTestSkinTemplate() was guarded by this same always-True flag,
meaning it would have overwritten a user's own already-customised
test_skin files (PNGs they'd replaced, an edited skin.json) with the
bundled template on every single launch -- not just skipped-vs-run
correctly once. The reported symptom (Skin setting reset) was the
easily-visible half of this; silently destroying custom work on every
launch was the more serious, not-yet-reported half.

### Fix

wasTestSkinJustCreated() no longer caches anything at construction
time. It now checks the real filesystem fresh, on every call:
`not os.listdir(path)` (an OSError -- directory missing entirely --
also counts as "just created"). This reflects real, persistent
on-disk state instead of in-memory construction-time history, so it
gives the correct answer regardless of how many times the module or
singleton gets reconstructed. Removed the now-unnecessary
_test_skin_was_just_created attribute and its assignment in
_initialize()'s own subdirectory loop entirely, rather than leaving
dead state behind.

plugin.py's own call site needed no changes -- wasTestSkinJustCreated()'s
own return-value semantics (True = treat as fresh, copy the template
and maybe reset the Skin setting; False = already populated, touch
neither) are unchanged, only how the answer is computed internally.

### Testing

Confirmed storage.py's own syntax with ast.parse() and py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on both touched files
(the usual pre-existing except-clause false positive). Directly
simulated the fixed logic against five scenarios using real temporary
directories on disk (not mocked): directory doesn't exist yet; exists
but is bare/empty (freshly created, nothing copied into it yet); ALREADY
POPULATED -- explicitly simulating "a reloaded module reconstructing
the singleton on a second launch", the exact real-device scenario that
exposed the bug -- confirmed this now correctly returns False instead
of the old implementation's wrong True; the user's own documented
recovery step (deleting the directory) correctly reads as "just
created" again afterward; and a partially-customised directory (only
one user file present, nothing else) correctly reads as NOT just
created, so a user's in-progress customisation is never mistaken for
an empty template-ready directory. Not yet confirmed on the actual
device that reported this, though the fix directly addresses the
exact mechanism the log evidence pointed to (persistent, on-disk
truth instead of a construction-time snapshot that doesn't survive
repeated module reloads).

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23 -- this one is 1.0.004.

---

## Round 112 (2026-09-05) -- second real crash: KeyError 'test_skin' in every screen's own colour palette dict

A device log's own full traceback pinpointed the exact crash:
`MAINSCREEN_SKIN_PALETTES[self._skin_variant]` raised `KeyError:
'test_skin'` inside MainScreen's own _buildSkin(), crashing startup
outright once "test_skin" actually became the active variant (round
110 added it to every screen's own SKIN_VARIANTS whitelist, letting
it PASS that check and become self._skin_variant -- but never added a
matching entry to the SEPARATE dict, in every screen, that actually
supplies each variant's own colour palette).

### Scope: found and fixed everywhere, not just where it crashed first

Grepped for every `[self._skin_variant]` dict lookup across the whole
codebase rather than patching only MainScreen (where the log happened
to show the crash first) -- found the identical gap in seven more
screens (Browser, MainMenu, Playlist, Podcast, Internet Radio,
Settings, Music Library -- the last one using a differently-named
`SKIN_PALETTES` rather than the XSCREEN_SKIN_PALETTES pattern every
other screen uses, easy to have missed with a narrower search). Fixed
all eight with the same one-line alias -- `XSCREEN_SKIN_PALETTES
["test_skin"] = XSCREEN_SKIN_PALETTES["dark"]` -- right after each
dict's own definition, so test_skin starts out visually identical to
Dark and stays in sync with any future change to Dark's own palette
automatically (a reference to the same dict, not a copy).

Also found a second, non-crashing but still wrong instance of the
same underlying gap: lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py's own dark-theme
check (round 103) used a direct string comparison, `if self.
_skin_variant == "dark":`, which "test_skin" would silently fail to
match, falling through to the light-coloured branch instead of
crashing outright. Fixed to `in ("dark", "test_skin")`. A second,
adjacent comparison (title_foreground_color) was checked and confirmed
already safe -- it's inverted on purpose (only "light" gets its own
value, everything else already gets the dark one), so it needed no
change; left a comment explaining why, so a future round doesn't
"fix" it unnecessarily.

Searched the whole codebase once more afterward for any other light/
dark pairing that might need the same treatment (any string literal
pairing "light" and "dark" together, outside of comments) -- found
nothing else.

### Testing

Confirmed all nine touched files' own syntax with py_compile;
case-insensitive AST undefined-name sweep clean on all (the usual
pre-existing except-clause false positive, one file with none at
all); precise "--"-inside-XML-comment sweep clean on all nine.
Programmatically executed each of the eight dict-based palette
definitions (extracted and exec'd in isolation, not just read by eye)
to directly confirm: a "test_skin" key now exists in every one, and
its value is identical to that same dict's own "dark" entry -- for
all eight, not assumed from the one already checked. Directly
re-executed the EXACT statement that crashed on the real device
(MAINSCREEN_SKIN_PALETTES[self._skin_variant] with self._skin_variant
set to "test_skin") to confirm it now resolves successfully and
returns the same palette as "dark", rather than trusting the fix from
its own structure alone. Not yet confirmed on a real device.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, per the ongoing
request from Round 23 -- this one is 1.0.005.

## Round 113 (2026-09-05) -- vintage-radio Test Skin: transparency, amber text, header offset

Two device screenshots of the first vintage-radio background attempt
showed three concrete problems, all fixed here.

### 1. Opaque widget backgrounds hid the artwork (the main issue)

Every text widget sat on a solid dark-blue block, because round 112's
own test_skin palette was a plain alias to Dark's -- whose
panel_background_color is a real opaque colour. Gave test_skin its
own palette instead of the alias, with panel_background_color set to
"#FF000000" -- Enigma2's own FULLY-TRANSPARENT value, not black (this
engine inverts the usual ARGB convention: 00 = opaque, FF =
transparent, per to_opaque_skin_color()'s own docstring). That same
helper would have forced the alpha back to "00" though, so
_buildSkin() now passes test_skin's value straight through, bypassing
the conversion; every other variant keeps the existing behaviour
untouched.

### 2. Text colours

Replaced Dark's near-white values with amber/brass tones read off the
user's own reference image: panel_text_color #E8A24C,
header_inactive_fg #C08A45, header_active_fg #FFC978.

### 3. Header row sat too high

Per direct request, the header row (Player title / branding / clock)
moved from y=18 to y=40 -- but ONLY for test_skin. Replaced the
hardcoded 18 in all six header rect() calls with a `header_y`
variable resolved per variant, so Light/Dark (both already confirmed
correct on real devices) are provably unchanged.

### Background artwork

Regenerated procedurally with PIL, this time driven by the REAL
widget geometry read out of _buildSkin() (cover, meta, media, status,
elapsed/progress/remaining, queueposition, both panel title/list
pairs) rather than guessed proportions -- every bezel, recess and
chip is positioned from those actual rects. A first attempt also drew
vacuum tubes, knobs and a speaker grille to match the reference more
literally; those were removed after rendering showed them clipped, because
the real layout leaves only ~2% margin at the sides and ~3.5% at the
bottom (the reference image's own panels are narrower). Said so
directly rather than shipping clipped decoration. Note this is a
procedurally-drawn stylisation, not a photorealistic match -- no image
generator is available in this environment.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile. Ran
the mandatory skin-XML validation across ALL THREE variants x BOTH
tiers (6 combinations, every one parsed by ElementTree), and
additionally asserted per-combination that: test_skin's XML really
does contain the transparent "#FF000000" value, light/dark's really
do NOT (so the transparency change can't leak into a confirmed-good
variant), and the header y offset resolves to the expected scaled
value in each tier for each variant (HD 18 vs 40, SD 10 vs 23), with
test_skin strictly lower than light. One failure the first run
surfaced was my own test's bug (an x-coordinate assertion that
doesn't hold once SD scaling is applied), corrected rather than
worked around. Not yet confirmed on a real device.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, plus a separate
images-only zip so the artwork can be dropped straight into
.mediaplayer3/test_skin/ without reinstalling. This is 1.0.006.

## Round 114 (2026-09-05) -- real architecture fix: transparency was punching through to live video

A device screenshot (round 113's own delivery) looked correct, but an
actual PHOTO of the TV showed the live channel/OpenViX boot screen
behind every text widget instead of test_skin's own vintage-radio
background. The distinction mattered: whatever tool produces this
project's own screenshots apparently captures the OSD/GUI layer only,
which is why round 113's own verification never caught this.

### Root cause

Enigma2's own backgroundColor alpha channel, on this receiver, punches
straight through the WHOLE OSD compositing stack to the video plane
underneath -- it is not a same-window alpha blend against sibling GUI
elements the way transparency normally works in most UI toolkits.
Round 113's own "#FF000000" (Enigma2's real fully-transparent value)
was therefore correct in VALUE but wrong in MECHANISM: it revealed
live video, not this screen's own background image sitting one
z-layer behind it. `transparent="1"` is a different, WIDGET-level
attribute already used elsewhere in this exact skin (player_title,
app_branding, clock, playlist_title, info_title) and confirmed
correct on real devices across many earlier rounds -- it leaves the
screen's own GUI background showing through instead.

### Fix

Every widget that read panel_background_color (meta, media, status,
elapsed, remaining, queueposition, playlist_list, info_content, and
all 13 lyrics_line_N rows -- 9 distinct widget definitions, one inside
a loop) now resolves a single conditional attribute string,
panel_background_attr, computed once: `transparent="1"` for
test_skin, or the existing `backgroundColor="{panel_background_color}"`
for every other variant, completely unchanged. Replaced the literal
backgroundColor line at all 9 definition sites with this one variable
rather than duplicating the conditional at each site.

One residual uncertainty flagged rather than glossed over:
playlist_list is a MenuList (scrollbarMode="showOnDemand"), not a
Label like the other eight -- whether transparent="1" behaves
identically for a MenuList's own row-selection highlighting isn't
something this project could verify without a device. Left it
converted the same way as the others (consistent with the goal, and
with no evidence either way that it needs different treatment) but
worth specific attention on the next device test.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile. Ran
the mandatory skin-XML validation across all three variants x both
tiers again (6 combinations); additionally confirmed programmatically
that test_skin's own generated XML contains zero literal
"backgroundColor=\"#FF000000\"" occurrences (the punch-through value
is gone entirely, not just supplemented) and at least the expected
count of transparent="1" widgets (31: 10 pre-existing + 21 newly
converted, including all 13 lyrics rows); confirmed Light's and
Dark's own meta widget still carries a real, non-empty backgroundColor
exactly as before (both untouched); and directly inspected test_skin's
own generated "meta" widget XML to confirm it now has NO
backgroundColor attribute at all, only transparent="1". Not yet
confirmed on a real device -- specifically needs a PHOTO of the actual
screen again, not just a screenshot, given that's exactly what
distinguished correct from broken last time.

Also caught and fixed a process gap while working on this: round 113's
own version-history comment in project.py had never actually been
written (a version bump via a quick sed left the surrounding dated
change-history block silently out of sync) -- added it retroactively
alongside this round's own entry rather than leaving the gap.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together, plus the same
images-only zip (unchanged artwork -- this round was a widget-XML fix,
not an image content change). This is 1.0.007.

## Round 119 (2026-09-06) -- real structural bug: scrolling lyrics rows never followed the box at all

Two more photos requested cover/header/progress-bar nudges, plus
flagged something more serious: "Sanoitusten laatikko on alussa jo
vähän liian pieni, mutta kun testin vieritys alkaa, niin tekstit
hyppää liian alas" -- the lyrics box already looks slightly too small
at the start, but once actual lyrics scrolling begins, the text jumps
too far down.

### Root cause

The 13 lyrics_line_N widgets (round 93's own scrolling-lyrics
mechanism, replacing the static info_content placeholder the moment
real synced lyrics start) were built with HARDCODED x=931/width=841/
y-start=560 -- completely ignoring in_content_rect the whole time.
Every earlier round's own adjustment to that box (113 through 118,
narrowing it, raising its bottom edge repeatedly down to 220) only
ever changed the STATIC placeholder shown before scrolling starts;
the instant real lyrics began scrolling, the rows snapped to the
ORIGINAL Light/Dark position and width regardless of variant -- this
was never actually testable by looking at the static screen alone,
only by starting real playback with synced lyrics, exactly as
reported.

Calculated the row set's own real total height directly from
MainScreen.LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS (10 rows x 28 + 2 rows x 34 + 1 row x 44
= 392) rather than guessing -- confirming round 118's own 220px box
could never have contained the real scrolling window even before this
fix, by 172px.

### Fix

lyrics_row_y now starts from in_content_rect[1] instead of a literal
560; each row's own rect() now uses in_content_rect[0]/in_content_rect[2]
for x/width instead of literals 931/841. test_skin's own in_content_rect
height raised to 400 (comfortably fits the real 392px total, 8px to
spare) -- still lower than the original 428, honouring the repeated
"raise the bottom edge" requests from rounds 117-118, but for the
first time actually informed by what the real content needs rather
than an arbitrary number.

### Also this round (test_skin-only, all against the same two photos)

- Cover art: +24 x further (140 from 116)
- Progress bar: +5 y -- sampled the reference image's own pixels
  directly (a sharp drop to near-black starting at y=377) to find
  its own trough's real top edge, rather than guessing
- Clock: -32 x further left (1500 from 1532)
- Player title ("Soitin"): given its own conditional x for the first
  time (was always the shared literal 88) -- +52 x toward centre (140)

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
and comment sweeps both clean. Ran the mandatory skin-XML validation
across all three variants x both tiers. Parsed the generated XML
directly for five repositioned widgets across all three variants (15
assertions, all matched, Light/Dark unchanged). Critically, also
parsed all 13 lyrics_line_N widgets' own actual generated positions
for test_skin specifically: confirmed every one now uses x=931/
width=677 (matching in_content_rect, not the old hardcoded 841),
confirmed the first row starts at y=560 as expected, and explicitly
computed the real total window height (392) against the box's own
height (400) to confirm it now fits with margin to spare, rather than
just trusting the fix from its own logic -- this is the check that
would have caught round 118's own 220px box being impossibly small
had it been run then. Not yet confirmed with real scrolling lyrics on

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.012.

## Round 120 (2026-09-06) -- track title higher, lyrics box genuinely shorter (with a second runtime bug caught along the way)

Two more photos: track title could be a bit higher still; the lyrics
box's own bottom edge is now too low both while playing and while
stopped (distinct from round 119's own bug -- this is about the box's
actual height/position, not rows ignoring it).

### Track title

media_rect moved up further (185 from 210), checked against meta's
own box (92 to 232) to stay clear of its own two lines of text rather
than guessing.

### Lyrics box: made genuinely shorter without reopening round 119's own bug

Round 119 confirmed the real 13-row window needs 392px at Light/Dark's
own row heights, and sized test_skin's own box (400px) to just fit
that. Simply shrinking the box further, without changing the row
heights themselves, would have reopened exactly the overflow bug round
119 just fixed. Instead, added test_skin_lyrics_rows -- a scaled-down
row-height table (24/29/37 instead of 28/34/44, keeping the same 13-row

genuinely shorter 340px box. MainScreen.LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS (the shared

### A second runtime bug caught while implementing this, before it shipped

_showLyricsWindow() (round 93's own runtime font-sizing method, used
every time actual lyrics text updates during playback) referenced
self.LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS -- the same shared class constant -- directly,
for its own font-size lookups. Left as-is, this would have kept
setting Light/Dark's own bigger font sizes onto test_skin's own now-
smaller row widgets at runtime, even though the skin-build-time XML
correctly used the smaller sizes -- overflowing each row the moment
real lyrics started scrolling, a close cousin of round 119's own bug
in a different part of the same mechanism. Fixed by stashing the
active row set actually used (self._lyrics_window_rows, alongside the
already-existing self._lyrics_font_family/self._lyrics_font_scale
this same method already relies on) during _buildSkin(), and having
_showLyricsWindow()/_hideLyricsWindow() read that instead of the raw
class constant. The two remaining LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS references
elsewhere (both purely `len(...)` count lookups) were checked and left
alone -- both row sets have exactly 13 elements, so counting either
one is equivalent and doesn't need the same fix.

## Round 121 (2026-09-06) -- real bug: silent playback failure masked as normal progress

User report: over the last couple of days, tracks occasionally fail
to actually play (progress bar still advances) but start working
after a manual Stop then Play. Asked what could be causing it.

### Root-caused from a device log's own full evidence, not guessed

grepped the log for GStreamer/eServiceMP3 activity around the exact
moment position tracking became unreliable, and found the smoking
gun directly: right at a track's own PAUSED->PLAYING transition,
`Gstreamer error: Could not determine type of stream. (4) from
dvbaudiosink1` and `Gstreamer error: Internal data stream error. (1)
from flacparse1`/`aqueue` -- a genuine GStreamer pipeline decode
failure for that specific FLAC file. From that exact moment,
`gst_element_query_position failed in getPlayPosition` repeated every
tick, continuously, for the rest of the track (confirmed: the log's
own "Estimated position: Xs | GStreamer-reported: Unknown" line never
recovered a real value again during this track, all the way through
at least 31 seconds).

This is a real, lower-level GStreamer/decoder failure this project
can't prevent outright (a corrupted file, a transient decoder
resource issue, or similar) -- but it exposed a real gap in this
project's own code: tick()'s own "estimated position" fallback (Build
0008, rounds 3-6, specifically built to smooth over BRIEF, transient
GStreamer position-query hiccups) had no upper bound on how long it
would keep doing that. A track whose pipeline never actually produces
audio at all still gets a smoothly-advancing progress bar via the
wall-clock estimate, indefinitely, with nothing to ever notice the
underlying failure -- exactly matching the user's own report of
silent, playing-but-silent tracks that only get noticed and fixed
manually.

### Fix: detect the sustained failure and recover automatically

Added _consecutive_unknown_elapsed_ticks, incremented whenever
elapsed is None (not just implausible -- genuinely unreadable) while
nominally playing, reset to 0 the moment a real reading returns, and
also reset at every point a track genuinely starts fresh or is
explicitly seeked (three existing _track_start_wall_time assignment
sites), so a legitimate startup or seek settling window is never
mistaken for a stuck pipeline. Once this counter reaches
STUCK_PLAYBACK_RECOVERY_TICKS (8, deliberately generous -- roughly 8
seconds at tick()'s own ~1/second cadence, well past normal startup
settling but well short of a whole song) tick() logs the situation
and automatically replays the current track (self._playIndex(self.
_queue_index)) -- the same effective action the user was already
doing manually, just automatic and immediate rather than requiring
the user to notice silence first.

### Testing

Confirmed playback_controller.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/
py_compile; AST undefined-name sweep clean. Directly simulated the
counter/recovery logic against five scenarios: normal playback with
always-valid elapsed (never recovers, as expected); a brief 3-tick
startup glitch that then recovers on its own (never triggers,
confirming no false positive during ordinary settling); a sustained
30-tick failure (recovers repeatedly every 8 ticks, matching the
threshold exactly, rather than either never recovering or recovering
too eagerly); exactly 7 consecutive unknown ticks then a real reading
(must NOT trigger -- confirmed it doesn't, the off-by-one boundary
case that would be easy to get wrong); and exactly 8 consecutive
ticks (must trigger exactly once, immediately, confirmed). Not yet
confirmed against the actual originally-reported scenario on a real
device, since that specific GStreamer decode failure wasn't
reproducible on demand -- the fix is grounded in the log's own
evidence of what happens once the failure occurs, not a live repro of
the underlying GStreamer bug itself.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.014.

---

## Round 122 (2026-09-06) -- round 121's own recovery never actually recovered, looped forever instead

A device log confirmed round 121's own fix was firing exactly as
designed -- "No GStreamer position for 8 consecutive ticks" logged
repeatedly, every 8 ticks, from 15:11:06 through at least 15:13:38 and
presumably longer -- but never actually restored playback. Matches
the user's own direct report: it keeps retrying the track start over
and over, only a manual Stop then Play actually works.

### Root cause

Round 121's own recovery called self._playIndex(self._queue_index)
directly, which goes through play() -- and play() only ever calls
self._service.play(filename), never self._service.stop() first.
stop() itself DOES call self._service.stop(). If the underlying
service/GStreamer pipeline object itself is what's left broken (not
just its own reported position), asking it to "play" again without
ever stopping it first can hand the same broken state straight back --
explaining exactly why the automatic retry kept failing in a loop
while the user's own manual Stop-then-Play, which does call
self._service.stop() first, worked every time.

### Fix

The recovery now calls self.stop() before self._playIndex(self.
_queue_index) -- the exact same two calls, in the exact same order,
that pressing Stop and then Play would make (confirmed by reading
play()'s own tail: it correctly sets STATE_PLAYING at the end, the
same as any other successful play() call, so the sequence is coherent
with the existing external Stop/Play contract, not a new one).

### Testing

Confirmed playback_controller.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/
py_compile; AST undefined-name sweep clean. Simulated the call
sequence with a mock controller and asserted stop() is actually
invoked, and strictly before _playIndex() (not just present
somewhere) -- confirming the fix matches the user's own successful
manual sequence structurally, not just in intent. Not yet confirmed
against a real recurrence of the original GStreamer decode failure on
device, for the same reason as round 121: the underlying failure
isn't reproducible on demand.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.015.

---

## Round 123 (2026-09-06) -- attempted prevention, not just recovery: stop the outgoing track before every transition

Round 122's own recovery confirmed working. Per direct follow-up
request ("onko edellisen kappaleen lopetuksessa jotain, jolla voidaan
huolehtia... ettei korjausta edes tarvitsisi" -- is there something in
how the previous track ends that could prevent needing the fix at
all), investigated whether NORMAL track transitions share the exact
same gap that made round 121's own recovery fail before round 122
fixed it.

### Confirmed: the same gap exists in every normal transition

nextTrack() (auto-advance and manual skip) calls _playIndex() ->
play() -> self._service.play() directly, exactly like round 121's own
broken recovery attempt did -- never calling stop() on the outgoing
track first. Traced one level deeper into ServiceController.play()
itself: _startService() calls navigation.playService() directly,
Enigma2's own built-in "replace whatever's currently playing"
mechanism (the same one ordinary channel-to-channel zapping already
relies on, normally sufficient on its own). But this project's own
existing code already documents "a known category of race condition
on GStreamer-based Enigma2 audio playback" (Build 0008's own tick()
docstring) -- and round 122's own confirmed fix (an explicit stop()
before replaying is what actually recovers a stuck pipeline; asking
playService() to just replace it directly does not) is exactly an
instance of that category. Different files failed across different
test sessions (U2, Ace Of Base) in the reporting logs, ruling out one
specific corrupted file and supporting a general transition-timing
race rather than a per-file issue.

### Fix: apply the same proven sequence to every transition, not just recovery

ServiceController.play() now stops its own outgoing service first
(self._stopService()) whenever self._current_service is already set
-- i.e. every transition from one MediaPlayer3-owned track to another,
but never on the very first track of a session (nothing to stop yet)
and never touching a service MediaPlayer3 doesn't own itself
(_stopService()'s own existing self._current_service guard, already
there specifically to avoid tearing down unrelated live TV running in
the background). This is an attempt at PREVENTING the underlying
GStreamer race rather than only recovering from it after the fact,
grounded directly in what round 122 already proved actually works for
recovery.

### An honest, unresolved trade-off

Stopping the previous service before starting the next one is a
different sequence than a direct service replace, and could introduce
a brief (likely sub-second, unconfirmed) gap between tracks where
none existed before -- not something this project could measure
without a real device. Flagged directly rather than presented as a
free fix: the next test should confirm both that this actually
reduces/eliminates the original silent-track failures AND that track-
to-track transitions still sound acceptable, not just the first of
the two.

### Testing

Confirmed service_controller.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/
py_compile; AST undefined-name sweep clean. Simulated play()'s own
call sequence with a mock across three scenarios: the very first
track of a session (confirmed _stopService() is correctly skipped --
nothing to stop yet, avoiding an unnecessary no-op call every single
first play), and two consecutive normal transitions (confirmed
_stopService() is called, and strictly before _createServiceReference()/
_startService(), for both -- not just the first transition after a
fresh start). Not yet confirmed against a real recurrence of the
original intermittent failure, or for any audible transition-gap
regression, on a real device.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.016.

---

## Round 124 (2026-09-06) -- confirmed the user's own hypothesis: a real timing race, not just missing the stop call

User asked directly whether this could be a GStreamer timing
difference -- the previous track's own stop not having actually gone
through yet. A further device log confirmed this precisely.

### The evidence

Round 123's own stop-before-play fix IS running (confirmed:
"Stopping playback service" now appears before every normal track
change in the log) -- but a failure still occurred, and critically,
the GStreamer error burst that followed named the EXACT SAME element
instance numbers ("dvbaudiosink1", "flacparse1") as had just been
involved in the stop moments earlier. navigation.stopService()
returns control to Python immediately; it does not wait for
GStreamer's own underlying pipeline teardown (releasing the actual
hardware audio decoder/sink resources) to actually finish. Calling
_createServiceReference()/_startService() for the NEXT track
immediately afterward can request a new pipeline before the previous
one has actually released those same resources -- a genuine race,
exactly matching the user's own hypothesis, not a missing call this
time (round 123 already added the call; the call alone wasn't
sufficient).

### Fix

Added a brief, deliberate blocking pause (time.sleep(0.2), 200ms)
between _stopService() and the following _createServiceReference()/
_startService() within play() itself -- but only on this specific
internal sequence (round 123's own new stop-before-play code path),
not on every stop()/play() call generally. Deliberately NOT applied
to round 122's own recovery path (self.stop() followed by a separate
_playIndex() call) or the user's own manual Stop-then-Play button
presses, both of which involve two separate, externally-driven calls
with their own natural gap already, and have not shown this symptom;
adding a blocking delay there wasn't asked for and isn't supported by
any evidence of the same race. The 200ms figure is a reasonable
starting point given the evidence, not a value taken from any
authoritative Enigma2 timing documentation -- flagged as such, not
presented as precisely calibrated. Explicitly noted this runs on
Enigma2's own main thread and does briefly block the UI on every
track transition now, a deliberate trade-off against an entire track
silently failing.

### Testing

Confirmed service_controller.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/
py_compile; AST undefined-name sweep clean. Simulated play()'s own
timing directly with a mock, measuring real elapsed wall-clock time
(not just call order) between _stopService() and the following
_createServiceReference(): confirmed a real ~200ms gap on a
transition, and confirmed no delay at all on the very first play of a
session (nothing to stop, the sleep is skipped entirely, matching
round 123's own existing guard). Not yet confirmed whether this
actually eliminates the original intermittent failure on a real
device, or how noticeable the 200ms pause is between tracks in
practice.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.017.

---

## Round 125 (2026-09-06) -- programmer feedback review begins: EPG/BLUE swap; research into Enigma2's native Help/HelpableActionMap system

Received substantial architecture feedback from an experienced
Enigma2 programmer (feedback.txt) plus a reference screenshot of
Enigma2's own native Help screen (a per-screen remote-control button
guide with an image of the remote, matching Screens.HelpMenu.HelpMenu).
Summary of the full feedback, for future rounds:

1. HELP should open Enigma2's own integrated HelpMenu (button info per
   screen), not this project's own custom rambling-text HelpScreen.
   Requires every screen's ActionMap -> HelpableActionMap, plus mixing
   in Screens.HelpMenu.HelpableScreen and calling its __init__().
2. There is no button/hint bar, and colour buttons currently do
   nothing at all in this app -- confirmed true (grepped: MainScreen
   has zero hint_text widgets, unlike Settings/Browser/etc.).
3. This project's own existing custom help CONTENT (the detailed,
   free-form text) should move to a separate "info" screen once HELP
   itself becomes the native per-screen guide.
4. Translation setup is over-complicated versus Enigma2 convention
   (typically a few lines in __init__.py, not a dedicated
   LocalizationManager module).
5. .mo files should not be committed to the repo (the bitbake recipe
   generates them); .po files should live in a "po/" folder at the
   repo root, not be shipped in the plugin package itself.

### Researched via web search before touching anything

Confirmed the real, standard Enigma2 pattern across openatv/OpenViX/
opendreambox source: `class MyScreen(Screen, HelpableScreen):`,
calling `HelpableScreen.__init__(self)`, with
`HelpableActionMap(self, "ContextName", {"action": (self.method,
_("help text")), ...})` -- a dict of (function, help-text) TUPLES
rather than bare functions. HelpableScreen provides callHelpAction(),
bound to the "displayHelp" action, which opens
`Screens.HelpMenu.HelpMenu(session, self.helpList)` -- exactly what
the reference screenshot shows. Confirmed via a real-world example
this exact wiring is easy to get wrong: a linked bug report showed a
screen with HelpableActionMaps but a missing HelpableScreen.__init__()
call, leaving its own HELP button completely inactive.

This is genuinely a large, multi-screen undertaking (every screen's
own ActionMap needs conversion, help text needs writing for every
action, the whole custom Help/HelpScreen/help_manager system needs
replacing or repurposing) -- not something to rush through in one
round. Planned as a staged project across upcoming rounds rather than
attempted wholesale here.

### Implemented this round: the specific, concrete swap requested

MainScreen's EPG/INFO key (compatibility.getInfoKeyActionNames()) and
BLUE now swap roles: BLUE takes over the Player<->Information panel
cycle (previously EPG/INFO, unchanged behaviour, just a different
physical key), and EPG/INFO now opens this project's own existing
custom help content (helpPressed(), the same content, no rewrite) --
laying the groundwork for HELP itself to later become Enigma2's own
native per-screen guide once the larger HelpableActionMap conversion
happens, without waiting for that whole project to relocate this
project's own existing detailed help text off the HELP button first.
HELP itself is deliberately left bound to the same helpPressed() for
now (not yet converted) -- changing what HELP does before the native
mechanism actually exists would leave it doing nothing useful in the
interim.

### Still open, deliberately not attempted this round

- The bottom hint-bar / button-bar space in MainScreen's own normal
  (Light/Dark) background, matching every other screen's own
  convention -- confirmed genuinely missing (zero hint_text widgets),
  but needs new background artwork (reserved space) plus new widget
  rows with text for every bound key, including this round's own
  blue/EPG swap. Sizeable enough to warrant its own dedicated round.
- The full HelpableActionMap conversion and native HelpMenu wiring,
  project-wide.
- Repurposing the existing custom Help content into a distinct "info"
  screen once HELP is freed up by the above.
- Translation system simplification to Enigma2's own __init__.py
  convention.
- Moving .po files to a repo-root "po/" folder and excluding .mo files
  from the repo/package (bitbake recipe's own job to generate them).
- A fuller audit of which OTHER functions across the app could
  reasonably move behind colour buttons, beyond this round's own
  single EPG/blue example.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
undefined-name sweep clean. Confirmed via grep that both the "blue"
literal and the getInfoKeyActionNames()/getHelpKeyActionNames() loops
now bind to the intended methods. Not yet confirmed on a real device.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.018.

## Round 126 (2026-09-06) -- MainScreen's own hint bar, Light/Dark only

Continuation of round 125's programmer-feedback review. Item #3:
MainScreen had zero hint_text widgets, unlike every other screen.

### Panel height reduction

pl_list_rect/in_content_rect (Light/Dark's own literal values) reduced
from height 428 to 330 -- 98px freed at the bottom, matching the same
per-variant-block pattern established since round 113. test_skin's own
values are untouched (it already carved out its own reserved space,
for a different reason, since round 115).

### A real bug caught before it shipped

First attempt placed the new hint_bar_xml construction inside the
same early per-variant block as pl_list_rect etc. -- which runs
BEFORE rect()/font() are defined further down in _buildSkin() (grepped
to confirm: `def rect(x, y, w, h):` is genuinely later in the method).
Referencing rect() from hint_bar_xml's own f-string at that point
raises UnboundLocalError in real execution too, not just in this
project's own mock validation harness. Moved the whole hint_bar_xml
construction to right after rect()/font() are actually defined,
keeping the same self._skin_variant condition.

A second, structural mistake was caught and fixed the same way: the
first edit landed the new code inside the WRONG branch of the
existing if/self._skin_variant/else block (test_skin's own branch
instead of Light/Dark's), plus an accidental duplicate `else:` with a
nonsensical self-assignment left over from a bad edit. Found via
grepping for every `if self._skin_variant == "test_skin":` / `else:`
pair in the file and counting them, not by re-reading the diff alone.

### Five new widgets

hint_text_ok / hint_text_blue / hint_text_menu / hint_text_info /
hint_text_exit, transparent="1", using a new "hint_fg" palette entry
added to MAINSCREEN_SKIN_PALETTES -- copied verbatim from
SettingsScreen's own existing "light"/"dark" hint_fg values (#036DFA /
#F0F0F0) for visual consistency across the app, not invented fresh.
Content: "OK: Menu" / "BLUE: Player/Info" / "MENU: Main Menu" /
"EPG/INFO: Help" / "EXIT: Back" -- five slots, matching Settings' own
established convention of showing the most important/non-obvious keys
rather than an exhaustive list of every bound key (MainScreen has far
more active keys than Settings does).

### Background artwork

Regenerated all 8 Light/Dark images (HD/SD x player/info), preserving
the existing top section (cover art, header, folder/info icons)
pixel-for-pixel rather than redrawing it: sampled the actual source
image's own pixels to find the lower panels' real top edge (a colour
transition at y=348 in the 1024-tall canvas, not guessed), wiped
everything from there down, and redrew both panels as clean rounded
rectangles at the new shorter height plus their own header strip and
icon, followed by a new footer card with five icon-glyph placeholders
in the freed space -- matching Settings/Browser's own established
"third card at the bottom" visual convention. An earlier attempt that
patched only the bottom few pixels left a visible seam artifact
(confirmed by direct visual inspection, not assumed fine); fixed by
redrawing each full panel from its real top edge instead of patching
a sliver.

### Also this round

Fixed a log-message inconsistency: activePanelPressed() (now bound to
BLUE, per round 125) still logged "[MainScreen] EPG/INFO pressed."
even when triggered by the physical BLUE key -- confirmed from a
device log showing this exact stale message alongside a "Keymap
'ColorActions' -> Action = 'blue'" line. Corrected to "[MainScreen]
BLUE pressed (panel switch)."

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
undefined-name sweep and "--"-comment sweep both clean. Ran the
mandatory skin-XML validation across all three variants x both tiers:
confirmed the hint bar is present for light/dark and absent for
test_skin in every combination, and that hint_text_ok's own generated
position/size matches exactly for both light and dark. Directly
viewed all 8 regenerated background images (not just generated and
assumed correct) -- confirmed no seam artifacts, existing artwork
intact, footer bar renders cleanly at both HD and SD, both panel
modes.

### Still open (unchanged from round 125's own list)

Native HelpableActionMap/HelpMenu conversion project-wide; repurposing
the existing custom help content into a distinct "info" screen;
translation system simplification; po/mo file placement fix; a fuller
colour-button audit beyond round 125's own EPG/blue swap.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.019.

## Round 127 (2026-09-06) -- round 126 had a real regression: the middle status card was accidentally erased

A device photo showed the progress bar's own slider handle sitting on
a visible seam. Investigation found round 126's own "find the real
panel top by sampling pixels" logic had picked up the wrong boundary:
what it treated as "the lower panels' own top edge" (y=348) was
actually the ORIGINAL middle status card's own top edge (the card
that holds elapsed/progress/remaining) -- confirmed by sampling
further down and finding no second gap before y=414, meaning round
126's own erase-and-redraw wiped that middle card out entirely and
had the lower panels start immediately where it used to be, leaving
the elapsed/progress/remaining widgets floating with no dedicated
card background of their own, exposed to the gap between the two
lower panels at the screen's horizontal centre -- exactly explaining
the visible seam under the slider handle.

### Fix matches the direct request's own wording

"progressbar mahtuu ylimpaan laatikkoon" (the progress bar should fit
INSIDE the top box) reads as: widen the top card to include the whole
status row, rather than restore a separate middle card. Implemented
by sampling the still-untouched top card's own real bottom edge and
extending it down to y=410 (covering elapsed/progress/remaining
entirely), then redrawing the two lower panels starting further down
(y=455, informed by this round's own second change below) rather than
where round 126 mistakenly had them start.

### Also this round

- Playlist/Information titles moved up (490 -> 475) per direct
  request, giving the lower panels a touch more headroom below the
  now-taller top card.
- Hint bar text shifted right, away from its own icon glyphs, which
  it was directly overlapping in the photo (icons stayed at their
  existing x; text x moved from 54/370/790/1110/1490 to
  104/420/840/1160/1540, widths trimmed to match).

### A second real mistake caught and fixed before finalizing

The first regeneration attempt started the "wipe old content" step
exactly at the old card's own bottom edge (y=335) -- but a rounded
rectangle's own corner curve pulls inward well before its nominal
bottom (starting around bottom-minus-radius, here y=310), so a sliver
of the OLD corner curve survived at the left/right edges, creating a
visible pinch where the new, taller card began. Confirmed by sampling
pixels at the card's own edge columns before and after the fix, not
just re-eyeballing the render. Fixed by starting the wipe well before
any possible corner curve (40px of margin) so nothing of the old
curve remains once the new patch is drawn.

A third mistake, caught the same way: the Dark variant's own card_bg
colour was initially sampled from a coordinate that fell inside the
cover-art placeholder box rather than the actual card background,
producing a colour mismatch between the untouched top portion and the
new extension. Fixed by sampling from a column clear of the cover box.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
and comment sweeps both clean. Ran the mandatory skin-XML validation
across all three variants x both tiers. Confirmed playlist_title's own
y for all three variants (475 for light/dark, 470 for test_skin,
unchanged) and hint_text_ok's own new x (104) directly from the
generated XML. Directly viewed all 8 regenerated images at both a
zoomed level and by sampling raw pixels at the seam locations (not
just glancing at a render) before and after each of the three fixes
above -- each confirmed clean only after the corresponding pixel-level
check passed, not from visual inspection alone.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.020.
