## Round 130 (2026-09-06) -- native HelpMenu pilot: MainScreen first

Per direct request, started the programmer-feedback review's own
item #1: Enigma2's own native HelpMenu (a remote-control button guide
per screen), tried on MainScreen first before any wider rollout.

### Implementation

MainScreen now mixes in Screens.HelpMenu.HelpableScreen alongside
Screen, with HelpableScreen.__init__(self) called explicitly (a real
bug report found during round 125's own research showed a screen with
HelpableActionMaps but a missing call to this exact method left its
own HELP button completely inactive -- deliberately not repeated
here). The existing single combined "actions" dict (11 contexts, one
ActionMap, built up across many earlier rounds) is preserved
structurally rather than split apart -- help text is attached by
wrapping each handler FUNCTION (not each literal action name, since
several action names already resolve to the same handler across the
various compatibility.getXxxKeyActionNames() loops) into a (function,
help_text) tuple, then passed to HelpableActionMap with the exact same
context list as before.

### A real version-compatibility risk, handled without touching functionality

HelpableActionMap's own multi-context support (accepting a LIST of
contexts, not just one) is a real but comparatively recent Enigma2
addition (confirmed via web search against an actual merged PR:
OpenViX#253, "HelpableActionMaps multiple contexts" -- explicitly
added because the ORIGINAL implementation's own docstring says "you
can only use ONE context here"). This project targets several
distributions (OpenATV/OpenPLi/OpenViX/OpenBH) whose own support for
this can't be assumed. Wrapped construction in a try/except: if
HelpableActionMap raises for any reason, the exact same actions fall
back to a plain ActionMap with the original, unwrapped functions --
so a platform lacking this support loses only the native help text
for this one screen, never any actual key binding.

### A second real bug caught before it could ship

compatibility.HELP_KEY_ACTIONS includes "displayHelp" -- the EXACT
action name Enigma2's own native help mechanism (HelpableScreen's own
internal handler, callHelpAction) listens for. The existing
getHelpKeyActionNames() loop bound this same name to this project's
own helpPressed() in the SAME "HelpActions" context -- a local
screen-level ActionMap always wins over a mixed-in base class's own,
so this would have silently defeated the whole point of the round: the
physical HELP button would keep opening this project's own custom
help content, and HelpableScreen's own native guide would never
actually be reachable. Fixed by excluding "displayHelp" specifically
from what this loop binds, while every other spelling
HELP_KEY_ACTIONS covers (a handful of older/alternate keymap
conventions) still falls back to helpPressed() as a compatibility net.
Found by re-reading the exact tuple's own contents rather than
assuming the existing HELP-key handling was compatible with adding
HelpableScreen.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
undefined-name sweep clean. Verified all 18 handler methods referenced
in the new help_text_by_handler dict genuinely exist (grepped each
one's own `def` individually, rather than trusting the literal
attribute references would resolve). Simulated the actual wrapping and
fallback logic directly with fake HelpableActionMap/ActionMap classes:
confirmed the success path produces correct (handler, help_text)
tuples for every action including ones sharing a handler, and
confirmed the fallback path reverts to plain, unwrapped functions
identical to the pre-round-130 behaviour -- so a platform without
multi-context support genuinely loses nothing but the help text. Ran
the mandatory skin-XML validation across all three variants x both
tiers to confirm _buildSkin() itself is completely unaffected by this
round's own ActionMap-only changes. Not yet confirmed on a real device
whether HELP actually opens Enigma2's own native guide, or whether
this specific Enigma2 build's own HelpableActionMap supports the
multi-context list at all -- this pilot needs a real device test
before deciding whether to roll the same pattern out to other screens.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.023.

---

## Round 131 (2026-09-06) -- native HelpMenu rolled out to every remaining screen

Round 130's own MainScreen pilot confirmed working on a real device
(a photo showed Enigma2's own native HelpMenu opening correctly, with
the remote-control image and every action's own help text). Per
direct request, applied the exact same pattern to every other screen
in the project: BrowserScreen, CoverArtFullscreenScreen,
DeveloperScreen, HelpScreen, LyricsFullscreenScreen, MainMenu,
MusicLibraryScreen, PlaybackInfoScreen, PlaylistScreen, PodcastScreen,
RadioBrowserScreen, SettingsScreen -- 12 screens total this round, 13
including MainScreen.

### Consistent pattern applied everywhere

Each screen: mixes in Screens.HelpMenu.HelpableScreen (SettingsScreen
already had ConfigListScreen as a second base -- HelpableScreen added
as a third, no known conflict between the two, matching stock Enigma2
Setup screens that already combine both); calls HelpableScreen.
__init__(self) right after Screen.__init__(self, session); wraps
each handler FUNCTION (not literal action name, several already
share handlers) with its own short help text into a
HelpableActionMap, inside the same try/except-with-plain-ActionMap-
fallback pattern round 130 established, so a platform lacking
HelpableActionMap's own multi-context support loses nothing but that
one screen's own native help text. Every loop that binds
compatibility.getHelpKeyActionNames() to a local handler now
specifically excludes "displayHelp" (round 130's own found conflict
with HelpableScreen's own internal handler for that exact action
name), confirmed present in all 9 screens that have such a loop.

### HelpScreen's own behaviour changes slightly

Converting HelpScreen itself means HELP no longer closes it directly
when it's the active screen (that action name is now reserved for
Enigma2's own native mechanism, consistent with every other screen);
Cancel/Exit still closes it. A reasonable, deliberate side effect of
applying the policy uniformly rather than a special exception.

### A real bug caught by re-checking every file, not assuming success

developer_screen.py had never previously used this project's own
_() translation wrapper (from .localization import _) -- confirmed by
checking that every "_(\"...\")" match in the file, before this fix,
was one of the NEW lines just added for help text, not a pre-existing
call. Adding help_text_by_handler entries with _(...) would have
raised NameError the moment this screen's own __init__ ran, on every
box, since the name was never imported. Fixed by adding _ to the
existing "from .localization import localization_manager" line.
Checked all other 11 modified files for the same mistake directly
(grepped for the correct import in each) -- all already had it from
before, since they'd used translated Label text previously; only
developer_screen.py was new to needing it.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for all 13 modified
files. Verified every handler method referenced in each file's own
help_text_by_handler dict genuinely exists via individual grep checks
(not assumed from the surrounding code). Programmatically confirmed,
across all 13 files: every getHelpKeyActionNames() loop that should
exclude "displayHelp" does so exactly once; every screen's own
HelpableActionMap/fallback ActionMap pair is present exactly once.
Re-ran the full AST undefined-name sweep on every file after the
developer_screen.py fix to confirm it actually resolved the issue
(the "_" name no longer flagged) rather than just applying the fix
and assuming it worked. Not yet confirmed on a real device for any
of these 12 newly-converted screens -- MainScreen alone has real
device confirmation so far.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.024.

unrelated rather than assumed safe. Verified no existing yellow/green
binding was overwritten in any of the four screens gaining one
(grepped each file's own colour-key usage before making the change,
all four came back empty). Not yet confirmed on a real device that

## Round 133 (2026-09-07) -- colour-button playlist add/remove across five screens

Per direct request, added GREEN/RED (add/remove to/from a playlist)
to MainScreen, BrowserScreen (Directories/Files columns),
MusicLibraryScreen (Artists/Albums/Tracks columns), plus a separate
RED/GREEN/YELLOW set on PlaylistScreen itself (clear/create/rename).

### New playlist_manager capabilities

Three new methods, each simulated with real path data before trusting
them:
- clearPlaylist(name) -- empties a playlist's own tracks without
  deleting the playlist entry itself (deliberately distinct from the
  existing deletePlaylist()).
- removeTracksInDirectory(name, directory) -- the true inverse of
  addFolder()/addFilesInDirectory(), recursive, with an os.sep-based
  boundary check confirmed (by simulation) not to false-positive-match
  "/music/rock" against "/music/rockother".
- removeTrackByPath(name, filepath) -- the path-based equivalent of
  the existing index-based removeTrack(), needed wherever the caller
  doesn't already know the track's own index in the target playlist.
A key-name bug was caught and fixed while writing these: a track's
own file path is stored under "path" (confirmed from
_trackFromPath()'s own dict literal), not "file_path" as first
written.

### PlaylistScreen (fully done)

RED = clearPlaylistPressed() (confirms via MessageBox first, matching
the existing Delete confirmation's own pattern; scoped to local
playlists only, matching Export's own existing local-only
restriction), GREEN = createNewPlaylistPressed() (skips the existing
"Create playlist" ChoiceBox's own single real option, straight to the
name prompt), YELLOW = renamePlaylistPressed() (works for both local
and radio-favourite entries, matching the existing Rename menu
option's own availability). Added "ColorActions" to the context list.

### BrowserScreen (fully done)

GREEN is context-dependent: in the Playlist column, keeps round 132's
own selectPlaylistPressed() (choosing which playlist this column
shows) since "add to playlist" has no meaning there; in Directories/
Files, adds the focused item instead (reusing the existing
_requireCurrentPlaylist()/_addDirectoryToPlaylist() machinery for
directories, playlist_manager.addTrack() directly for files). RED
removes the focused item's own tracks (removeTracksInDirectory()/
removeTrackByPath()) the same way, no column-dependent special case
since it has no meaning in the Playlist column either (nothing bound
there). New _afterRemove() mirrors the existing _afterAdd()'s own
messaging style. A mistake was made and caught mid-edit: a str_replace
call accidentally deleted a for-loop's own header line, confirmed and
fixed by reading the file back with Python's own repr() rather than
trusting a possibly-misleading terminal display of escaped quotes.
Simulated the full green/red dispatch across all three focus states
(playlist/directories/files) with a mock before trusting it.

### MainScreen and MusicLibraryScreen (fully done, new infrastructure)

Neither screen had any playlist-target-picking mechanism before this
round. Each gained its own self-contained
_requireCurrentPlaylist()/_playlistPickerChosen()/
_newPlaylistNameEntered() trio (structurally identical to
BrowserScreen's own established version, not extracted into a shared
module this round -- a reasonable follow-up, not attempted here given
the added scope/risk) plus its own _current_playlist_name state.

MainScreen: GREEN/RED target self._playback.getCurrentFile() (the
currently loaded track) directly.

MusicLibraryScreen: added _tracksForCurrentFocus(), resolving what
GREEN/RED should act on per column -- a single path (Tracks), every
track under the selected album via library_manager.getTracks(artist=,

musiclibraryscreen.py had never imported playlist_manager or

have raised NameError on the very first GREEN/RED press on every box.
Fixed by adding both imports; confirmed via the same "check every
handler actually exists" discipline used throughout this whole
HelpableActionMap project.

### Explicitly deferred this round

Visual hint-bar text for the new colour keys was NOT added on any of

## Round 134 (2026-09-07) -- MainScreen's radio/files playlist mix-up fixed; correct StaticText/key_red convention adopted

Positive confirmation that round 133's own GREEN/RED works correctly
on BrowserScreen, MusicLibraryScreen, and PlaylistScreen. Two further
items: a real functional gap in MainScreen, and a direct programmer
tip about the correct Enigma2 convention for colour-button hints.

### MainScreen: radio and files sharing one playlist target

"Mainscreen toimii oikein, mutta kayttaa samaa soittolistaa
tiedostoille ja radiolle" -- round 133's own _current_playlist_name
was a single value regardless of what's actually playing, so a radio
stream and a local file could end up added to the very same playlist
by default. Per the direct request, MainScreen's own
_requireCurrentPlaylist() now computes a smart suggested name first
(self._playback.isPlayingStream() -> "General (radio)", otherwise
_defaultPlayPlaylistName()'s own existing "Files" string, imported
from browserscreen.py rather than duplicating that string) and
surfaces it as the picker's own top entry, de-duplicated against the
real playlist list if a playlist by that exact name already exists.
Simulated the choice-building logic across three scenarios (radio
with no existing playlists, files with "Files" already existing,
radio with "General (radio)" already existing) before trusting it --
the first assertion attempt was itself wrong (checked for an exact
tuple match rather than the underlying value), caught and corrected
before treating the logic as verified.

### StaticText()/key_red: the correct Enigma2 convention

Direct feedback: "For colour buttons use StaticText() and use the
standard names, i.e. 'key_red', 'key_green', 'key_yellow', 'key_blue'."
Confirmed via web search against real Enigma2/enigma2-plugins source
(HdmiCEC's own setup screen, Emission's own EmissionOverview.py,
several others) -- self["key_red"] = StaticText(_("...")), imported
from Components.Sources.StaticText, is genuinely the standard
component/naming Enigma2 screens and skins expect for colour-button
hints, not this project's own earlier Label()+made-up-widget-name
pattern (e.g. hint_text_yellow elsewhere in this codebase).

Added the correct StaticText key_red/key_green/key_yellow components
to the four screens round 133 gave new colour-button behaviour
(PlaylistScreen: red/green/yellow; BrowserScreen/MainScreen/
MusicLibraryScreen: green/red) -- but deliberately did NOT add a
matching skin <widget source="key_green" render="Label".../> entry
for any of them this round. Checked each screen's own hint bar first:
all have only ~150-200px of unused width in their own already-full
row, not enough for three more full hint texts without the same kind
of background-artwork/widget-geometry redesign MainScreen's own hint
bar needed across rounds 113-129. Rushing that now risked repeating
the exact seam/mismatch mistakes those rounds had to fix one at a
time. An Enigma2 Source component created without a matching skin
entry is simply invisible, never an error -- the same tolerant
behaviour this project's own existing hint_text_* Labels already rely
on -- so this is a safe, real step (the correct component now exists
in code) without a rushed visual change.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for all four modified
files (mainscreen.py, browserscreen.py, playlistscreen.py,
musiclibraryscreen.py). Ran the AST undefined-name sweep on all four
-- clean except the known "error" false positive. Checked for an
import cycle before importing _defaultPlayPlaylistName from
browserscreen.py into mainscreen.py (traced browserscreen.py's own
full import list and its own transitive imports; none import back
from mainscreen.py). Ran the mandatory skin-XML validation for
mainscreen.py across all three variants x both tiers to confirm the
new import/logic didn't affect skin generation. Confirmed
addTrack()'s own docstring already handles creating a playlist that
doesn't exist yet, so selecting the suggested name directly (without
going through "Create new...") works correctly without extra code.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.027.

## Round 135 (2026-09-08) -- .po/.mo file placement corrected

Per the programmer feedback review's own item #5 ("*.mo files should
not be in the repo. These are added by the bitbake recipe. *.po files
should not be shipped. They should be in 'po' folder in the root of
the repo."), restructured translation file handling to match this
exactly.

### What moved

- po/<lang>.po (fi/en/sv/de/es) now live at the repo root, outside
  src/ entirely -- so mediaplayer3.bb's own existing
  "cp -r ${S}/src/* ..." in do_install() can never accidentally ship
  them, without needing an explicit exclude rule.
- The previously-committed src/resources/locale/<lang>/LC_MESSAGES/
  MediaPlayer3.mo files are deleted from the repo entirely. They are
  build output, not source -- generated fresh every build instead.

### What the .bb recipe now does

Added do_compile(), which runs msgfmt against each po/<lang>.po,
writing the compiled .mo directly into ${S}/src/resources/locale/
<lang>/LC_MESSAGES/MediaPlayer3.mo -- the exact path localization.py's
own LOCALE_PATH (RESOURCE_PATH/locale) expects Python's own gettext
module to find at runtime, confirmed by reading localization.py/
paths.py directly rather than assuming. This runs before do_install(),
so the compiled .mo files already exist inside ${S}/src by the time
that step's own copy runs. Added gettext-native to DEPENDS.

### Verified the new source is equivalent to what was already shipping

Before deleting anything, compiled po/fi.po with msgfmt and compared
the result against the currently-committed .mo it's meant to replace:
the entry count matched exactly (356, both), and a direct gettext
lookup of five spot-checked strings (Play/Stop/Menu/Help/Exit)
produced byte-identical translated output in both. Also validated the
exact do_compile() shell fragment standalone (not just reasoned about
it) -- ran it against a temp copy of the real po/ files for all five
languages and confirmed every one produces a loadable, correct .mo.

### A real process gap this also surfaces

This project's own delivery pipeline in this conversation builds the
.ipk manually (there's no real bitbake/Yocto build system running
here) by copying the working src/ directory into a staging area.
Since src/ itself no longer carries compiled .mo files, that manual
build process needs its own equivalent compile step from now on --
msgfmt run into the STAGING copy specifically, never into the actual
working src/ itself (which should stay exactly as clean as the real
repo now is, matching what a real bitbake build would see, and
matching what the .zip deliverable -- a full repo snapshot -- should
contain). Applied starting with this round's own packaging step
below.

### Also added

.gitignore, excluding src/resources/locale/*/LC_MESSAGES/*.mo
specifically (plus the standard __pycache__/*.pyc/*.pyo) -- so a
future round can't accidentally re-commit a compiled .mo the same way
this round found committed already.

### Delivery

This round has no runtime Python changes (translation CONTENT and
lookup logic are both completely unchanged -- only where the SOURCE
files live and how the SHIPPED .mo gets built). The .ipk is built
using the corrected process (msgfmt into the staging copy only); the
.zip is the repo as it now stands (po/ present, no committed .mo).
This is 1.0.028.

## Round 137 (2026-09-08) -- adaptive stop-state polling instead of a blind fixed delay

Positive confirmation: the Main Menu correctly shows only 7 entries
now (About/Playback Info/Developer Tools all gone), and the new
verbose-log diagnostics dump is confirmed working. Per direct request,
attempted to replace round 124's own blind, fixed 200ms sleep between
stopping the outgoing track and starting the next with an actual state
check: "kysy Gstreamer tila, jos stop niin aloita, jos ei niin
pysaytetaan ja kysytaan uudestaan."

### Researched before implementing, not assumed

Searched for how Enigma2's own eServiceMP3 implementation actually
handles this internally. A real, linked commit's own source
(athoik/enigma2, "ServiceMP3: Optimize GStreamer start sequence")
showed eServiceMP3::stop() sets its own internal m_state to stStopped
SYNCHRONOUSLY, the instant stop() is called -- before
gst_element_set_state(..., GST_STATE_NULL) has necessarily completed
at the GStreamer level -- and the same commit's own comment says
plainly: "The get state on stop might block forever, so use 5 seconds
timeout." This means the state Python can actually observe
(navigation.getCurrentService() -- there is no direct Python binding
for GStreamer's own internal pipeline state) reflects that same
synchronous flag, not confirmation that the underlying hardware
decoder resources have actually been released. Even Enigma2's own C++
layer doesn't have a clean, fast answer to this -- it resorts to a
5-SECOND timeout as its own safety net.

### What was implemented, and what wasn't claimed

Given that honest limit, this round doesn't claim to have found a
genuine "ask GStreamer if it's really done" signal (none is available
from Python), but does implement the polling loop as requested,
using the best available proxy: navigation.getCurrentService(). After
the existing stop() call, polls up to 10 times (30ms apart) for
getCurrentService() to become None, re-issuing stopService() itself
on each attempt where it hasn't yet (per the exact requested
sequence) -- exiting the loop as soon as it does, rather than always
waiting the full amount. The existing 200ms residual safety pause
(round 124's own fix) still runs afterward regardless of how the
polling resolved, since confirming the high-level flag doesn't rule
out the underlying race round 124 found -- this is a genuine, stated
trade-off, not silently dropped. In the common case (state confirmed
on the very first check, as the C++ source suggests is likely) this
changes nothing about total wait time; the loop's own real value is
in the less common case where the state genuinely takes a few checks
to settle, where it now retries the stop call itself rather than
assuming a single call was enough.

### Testing

Confirmed service_controller.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/
py_compile; AST sweep clean (only the known "error" false positive).
Simulated the polling loop directly with a mock navigation object
across three scenarios: already stopped on the very first check (exits
immediately, 1 attempt); takes 3 checks to settle (exits as soon as it
does, not before); and never settles within the 10-attempt cap (falls
through to the existing residual pause regardless, matching round
124's own established fallback behaviour rather than hanging or
skipping the safety margin). Not yet confirmed on a real device
whether this measurably reduces the original intermittent failure's
own frequency, given the honest uncertainty above about what the
available state query actually proves.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.030.

---

## Round 138 (2026-09-09) -- stuck-playback detection window shortened from 8 to 5 ticks

A device log confirmed round 137's own polling change didn't help,
because it targeted the wrong part of the sequence: "Näyttää sama
virhe tulevan edelleen. Korjauksen viivetta voisi ainakin pienentaa n.
5 sekuntiin."

### Read the log's own timestamps rather than assuming what changed

Round 137 touched the STOP-TO-PLAY transition (ServiceController.
play()'s own brief pause once a new track is already being started).
The device log's own timestamps showed the actual multi-second delay
happens BEFORE that -- in playback_controller.py's own tick()-based
DETECTION itself: every occurrence in the log showed the "No GStreamer
position for 8 consecutive ticks" recovery message firing 6.6-8.0
seconds after the corresponding GStreamer error burst, and this
pattern repeated roughly every 17-37 seconds throughout the whole
session (confirmed by grepping every occurrence of both the error and
the recovery message and comparing their own timestamps directly,
rather than assuming). Confirmed the tick interval itself is exactly
1000ms (mainscreen.py's own self._refresh_timer.start(1000, False)),
matching STUCK_PLAYBACK_RECOVERY_TICKS = 8's own arithmetic (8 ticks x
~1s = the observed ~8s) exactly.

### The fix

Lowered STUCK_PLAYBACK_RECOVERY_TICKS from 8 to 5 -- still 2.5x-5x
above the "a second or two" normal startup/seek settling window this
constant's own round 121 comment describes, so this shouldn't
introduce false positives from ordinary brief position-reporting
gaps. The recovery path itself (stop() then _playIndex(), per round
122's own fix) already runs through ServiceController.play(), so it
automatically benefits from round 137's own polling improvement too --
no additional wiring needed there.

### What this round deliberately does not attempt

The underlying GStreamer failures themselves (repeating every 17-37
seconds across the whole session, on different tracks each time, not
one specific corrupt file) are not something this round investigates
or claims to fix -- the direct request was specifically to shorten the
recovery delay ("Korjauksen viivetta... niin sita ei juurikaan edes
huomaisi", make the delay barely noticeable), not to eliminate the
underlying cause, which would need a genuinely separate investigation
(possibly hardware/GStreamer-version specific to this receiver) this
round doesn't have the evidence to start.

### Testing

Confirmed playback_controller.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/
py_compile; AST sweep clean (only the known "error" false positive).
Confirmed via grep that STUCK_PLAYBACK_RECOVERY_TICKS has exactly one
other reference in the whole codebase (its own comparison in tick()),
so this is a fully self-contained, single-constant change. Not yet
confirmed on a real device whether recovery now measurably takes
closer to 5 seconds, or whether the underlying GStreamer failures
still recur at a similar frequency (expected, since this round doesn't
address their cause).

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.031.

---

## Round 139 (2026-09-09) -- help content renamed to "Information"/"Tiedot" (help_manager/HelpScreen -> guide_manager/GuideScreen)

Per direct request, moved this project's own existing detailed
per-screen documentation from "help" framing to "information" framing
throughout -- item #2 of the programmer feedback review, now that
every screen's own HELP button opens Enigma2's native guide instead
(rounds 130-132) and EPG/INFO already opens this content (round 132).

### A real naming collision caught before starting

Flagged directly to the user before touching anything: information_
panel.py already implements MainScreen's own separate "Information"
concept (lyrics/metadata/codec display within the player UI) --
naming the renamed classes/modules directly after "Information" would
create two unrelated things called "Information" in the same
codebase. Per the user's own chosen resolution, used a different
internal identifier (GuideManager/guide_manager.py, GuideScreen/
guide_screen.py) while still presenting as "Information"/"Tiedot" to
the user in every user-facing string.

### What changed

- help_manager.py -> guide_manager.py: HelpManager -> GuideManager,
  getHelp() -> getGuide(), _fallbackHelp() -> _fallbackGuide(),
  default title/fallback text "Help"/"No help available." ->
  "Information"/"No information available.".
- help_screen.py -> guide_screen.py: HelpScreen -> GuideScreen, same
  string updates, default window title "MediaPlayer3 - Help" ->
  "MediaPlayer3 - Information".
- All 8 screens with their own helpPressed() (BrowserScreen, MainMenu,
  MainScreen, MusicLibraryScreen, PlaylistScreen, PodcastScreen,
  RadioBrowserScreen, SettingsScreen): renamed to infoPressed(),
  imports updated, both the getInfoKeyActionNames() and
  getHelpKeyActionNames() (fallback) loop bindings updated, help_text_
  by_handler entries updated to "show information about this screen".
- Two real pieces of dead code removed as part of this: PlaylistScreen
  and SettingsScreen each still had round 132's own orphaned
  infoPressed() (showing track/setting info directly -- unreachable
  since round 132 moved EPG/INFO to open help content instead, but
  left in place at the time). Removed before the rename to avoid a
  duplicate method definition; confirmed exactly one infoPressed()
  survives per file afterward.
- Removed the orphaned resources/help/developerscreen.md (leftover
  content from round 136's own DeveloperScreen deletion).
- Deliberately left Enigma2's OWN native symbols completely untouched:
  HelpableScreen, HelpableActionMap, getHelpKeyActionNames(),
  HELP_KEY_ACTIONS, "HelpActions" -- these are Enigma2's own API
  names, not this project's, and renaming them would be both wrong
  and pointless.
- Corrected several live (non-dated) comments that named the old
  classes directly, in compatibility.py, information_panel.py,
  lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py, and three spots in mainscreen.py --
  left every genuinely DATED changelog-style entry (e.g. "2026-07-28
  Build 0008 -- Added HELP key handling: opens HelpScreen...")
  untouched, since those are accurate historical records of what was
  true at their own point in time, not current claims.

### Translations

Updated po/<lang>.po for all 5 languages (fi/en/sv/de/es). Found a
real duplicate-msgid bug in my own first attempt: "Information" and
"No information available." already existed as msgids (from
information_panel.py's own unrelated strings) with the exact
translations I would have added -- my first edit created invalid
duplicate msgid blocks, caught by running msgfmt and reading its own
"duplicate message definition" errors directly rather than assuming
success. Fixed by removing my own duplicate entries and keeping the
pre-existing ones (identical content either way). Verified all 5
compile cleanly and spot-checked the actual translated values via
Python's own gettext module afterward.

**Known, pre-existing gap, not touched by this round**: round 130's
own HelpableActionMap help text (e.g. "show information about this
screen", "close this information screen") was never added to any .po
file in the first place -- confirmed via grep before and after this
round's own edits. This predates round 139 and isn't something this
rename fixes; a genuinely separate, larger task (auditing every
help_text_by_handler string across all 13 screens for translation
coverage) would be needed to close that gap.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for every file touched
(13 Python files) and the whole src/ directory as a final sweep. Ran
the AST undefined-name sweep on all 13 -- clean except the known
"error" false positive. Confirmed via grep, twice (once mid-round,
once as a final sweep), that no file still imports from the deleted
help_manager/help_screen modules and no def helpPressed remains
anywhere. Confirmed exactly one infoPressed() definition survives in
each of the two files that had a dead duplicate (playlistscreen.py,
settingsscreen.py). Ran the mandatory skin-XML validation for
mainscreen.py across all three variants x both tiers. Compiled and
spot-checked all 5 updated translation files' actual runtime values,
not just their own syntax. Not yet confirmed on a real device that
EPG/INFO's own native-help-guide entry now correctly reads
"Information" rather than "Help" for each screen.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.032.

## Round 140 (2026-09-09) -- translation system simplified to Enigma2 convention (independent language choice removed)

Per direct request (programmer feedback item #4: "Translation setup
is over-complicated versus Enigma2 convention -- typically a handful
of lines directly in __init__.py, not a dedicated LocalizationManager
module/class"), researched real Enigma2 plugin source (IMDb,
ShareMyBox, FileBrowser __init__.py files) before touching anything,
confirming the actual standard: a module-level _() using gettext
directly against the receiver's own current system language
(Components.Language/config.osd.language), not a dedicated manager
class.

### A real design conflict found before starting

Flagged directly to the user: this project's own Settings screen
already offered an INDEPENDENT language choice (cfg.general.language,
default "system" but overridable) -- something the standard Enigma2
convention doesn't support at all (every plugin surveyed just follows
the box's own OSD language unconditionally). Per the user's own
explicit choice, dropped the independent choice entirely rather than
trying to preserve it inside a simplified implementation -- MediaPlayer3
now always follows the receiver's own system language, exactly like
every other Enigma2 plugin.

### What changed

- localization.py: LocalizationManager class removed entirely.
  Replaced with a module-level _() that calls compatibility.
  getSystemLanguage() fresh on every call (matching this project's
  own established "always live, never baked in once" precedent from
  config.py's own ConfigYesNoLocalized) and caches the loaded gettext
  translation object per language code to avoid re-reading the .mo
  file from disk every single call. New getCurrentLanguage() helper
  for the few callers that need the resolved code itself, not a
  translated string.
- config.py: removed cfg.general.language (the ConfigSelection
  itself), _AVAILABLE_LANGUAGE_CODES, and resolveLanguageCode().
- plugin.py: removed the startup language-application call entirely
  -- no longer needed, since _() now always reads the current system
  language itself.
- settingsscreen.py: removed the "Language" entry from the Settings
  list (and its own deliberately-untranslated-label comment from
  round 31), and the apply-on-change block that used to call
  localization_manager.setLanguage().
- internetradio_manager.py and radiobrowserscreen.py: both had a real,
  separate dependency on the old general.language setting (falling
  back to "the app's own UI language" for radio station language
  filtering when no explicit radio.default_language is set) -- updated
  both to call localization.getCurrentLanguage() directly instead.
- diagnostics.py: LocalizationManager's own getTranslationStats()
  (lookup/missing-key counters) no longer exists -- simplified to
  report just the language actually in use and the available
  languages list.
- resources/help/settingsscreen.md: its own intro sentence still said
  "appearance, language, and playback..." -- a genuinely user-facing
  reference to a setting that no longer exists. Fixed. Checked every
  other help document for the same problem; radiobrowserscreen.md's
  own several "language" mentions are all about RadioBrowser's own
  unrelated station-search-by-broadcast-language feature, confirmed
  unaffected.

### Two real bugs caught before shipping, by grep and manual review rather than assuming success

1. config.py's own _ENTRIES dict (built at MODULE IMPORT TIME, not
   inside any function) still had "general.language": cfg.general.
   language as one of its own key-value pairs after cfg.general.
   language itself was deleted -- this would have raised AttributeError
   on the very first import of config.py, crashing the whole
   application at startup, on every single box. py_compile's own
   syntax check did NOT catch this (it only validates syntax, never
   runtime attribute access) -- caught only by a targeted grep sweep
   across the whole codebase for every remaining reference to the
   removed symbols, run specifically because a syntax-only check was
   recognized as insufficient for a removal of this kind. Manually
   reviewed the ENTIRE _ENTRIES dict afterward, line by line, to
   confirm no sibling issues remained.
2. The first sweep for resolveLanguageCode() missed two genuine, still-
   active call sites in radiobrowserscreen.py entirely (an import and
   two calls) -- found only on a SECOND, more thorough sweep. A
   settingsscreen.py "info text" dict also still had a "Language" key
   describing the now-removed setting (harmless -- an unused extra
   dict key raises nothing -- but dead, confusing content); removed.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for every one of the 8
touched files, and the whole src/ directory as a final sweep. Ran the
AST undefined-name sweep on all 8 -- clean except the known "error"
false positive. Simulated the new _()'s own caching, fallback, and
language-resolution logic standalone with mocks across four scenarios
(normal language, cache hit confirmed via a load-counter, unsupported
language code, missing catalog file) before trusting it. Ran a
targeted grep for every remaining reference to LocalizationManager/
localization_manager/resolveLanguageCode/_AVAILABLE_LANGUAGE_CODES/
cfg.general.language TWICE -- once mid-round (which caught the
_ENTRIES bug and the radiobrowserscreen.py gaps), once as a final
sweep (clean, only dated changelog entries and this round's own
comments remain). Manually reviewed config.py's own complete _ENTRIES
dict end to end. Checked every resources/help/*.md document for a
stale mention of the removed Language setting. Not yet confirmed on a
real device that the app still launches correctly and picks up the
receiver's own system language without the removed Settings entry.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.033.

## Round 141 (2026-09-09) -- test_skin's own footer hint bar added

Per direct request, added MainScreen's own hint bar to test_skin --
item #3 of the programmer feedback review, done for Light/Dark since

lower panels being part of the photographed background rather than a
programmatically-drawn card meant round 126's own "shrink the card,
carve out the footer" approach didn't directly apply).

### Measured the real image geometry directly before designing anything

followed by a decorative speaker-grille strip from y~858 to y~995

tall, starting at y=560) already extend well past that real visual
panel bottom (~988/900 vs ~838-840) -- meaning list/lyrics content has
likely been rendering below the photographed panel's own edge, over
the wood texture, for as long as those numbers have been in place.
Reported to the user rather than silently fixed alongside the
requested hint bar work, since it's a separate, pre-existing issue
outside this round's own scope and fixing it properly would need its

the emblem still peeks out below the new bar, reading as an

entry (brighter than panel_text_color, for contrast against the new
bar's own dark fill) -- the Python-side Label() creation for these
widgets was already unconditional (created for every variant since
round 126), so only the skin XML side needed adding.

### A second real bug found and fixed along the way

hint_text_info's own Label text still read "EPG/INFO: Help" --
missed when round 139 renamed the underlying content from "Help" to
"Information" throughout. Fixed to "EPG/INFO: Information"; confirmed
via grep this was the only remaining occurrence of the stale wording
anywhere in the codebase. Left untranslated in po/, consistent with
round 139's own already-documented gap (this whole hint-bar text
family was never added to any .po file in the first place).

### An important caveat for the user

resources/skins/test_skin_template/ is only ever copied into a user's
own real Test Skin folder when that folder is freshly created (round
110's own logic) -- an already-populated Test Skin directory (which
this project's own user has been actively iterating on since round
113) is never touched by a template update on its own. Reinstalling
this round's own .ipk will not, by itself, show the new hint bar on a
device that already has its own Test Skin folder; that folder would
need to be cleared (or the new images copied into it manually) for
the change to actually appear there. Stated directly to the user
rather than left to be discovered as "it doesn't seem to work."

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
sweep clean (only the known "error" false positive). Ran the mandatory
skin-XML validation across all three variants x both tiers, confirming
test_skin's own new hint_bar_xml is well-formed. Visually reviewed
every stage of the image generation (bare overlay only, then with
text, at both HD and SD scale) before finalizing, rather than trusting
the coordinate math alone. Grepped for every other occurrence of the
stale "INFO: Help" wording across the whole codebase to confirm the
one instance found was the only one. Not yet confirmed on a real
device -- and, per the caveat above, confirming this specifically
needs either a fresh Test Skin folder or the four regenerated images
copied in manually, not just a normal reinstall.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.034.

## Round 142 (2026-09-09) -- test_skin hint bar fixed (badge overlap + missing HELP entry); lyrics overflow fixed

Two real device photos showed round 141's own hint bar text sitting
directly on top of the reference image's own decorative centre badge,
garbled and hard to read. Per direct request, fixed the overlap, added
a genuinely missing HELP entry to the same bar, and used the same
session to fix the lyrics-scrolling overflow the user had separately
confirmed after round 141's own report of it.

### Root cause of the overlap: two compounding mistakes

1. Round 141's own hint bar used a semi-transparent fill (235/255
   alpha) for the new footer plate -- letting the reference photo's
   own badge and grille texture show faintly through even in areas the
   text didn't numerically overlap the badge's own rect, muddying the
   result on a real device photo in a way the tool's own PIL-rendered
   preview never fully revealed. Fixed: fill is now fully opaque
   (255/255).
2. Round 141 never actually measured the badge's own real position
   before placing "MENU"/"EPG/INFO" text -- both landed directly on
   top of it. Measured it properly this round with a fresh pixel
   sample against the pre-overlay artwork (not the already-modified
   working file): the badge's own real horizontal extent is
   approximately x 845-965 design units.

### HELP entry: a real, separate gap, not just a side effect of fixing the overlap

Enigma2's own native per-screen guide (rounds 130-132) never had a
hint bar entry of its own on ANY variant, on any screen -- confirmed
by checking Light/Dark's own existing 5-slot layout, which also never
listed HELP. Rather than just shrinking the existing 5 slots to dodge
the badge, added HELP as a genuine sixth slot, splitting all six into
three on each side of the measured badge zone (OK/BLUE/MENU on the
left, EPG-INFO/HELP/EXIT on the right) -- confirmed programmatically
via the generated skin XML that MENU's own right edge (840) and
EPG/INFO's own left edge (970) both clear the measured badge zone
(845-965) with margin on both sides. This round only touches
test_skin's own hint_bar_xml and background images; Light/Dark's
existing five-slot layout is untouched, a reasonable separate
follow-up if a HELP entry is wanted there too later.

### Lyrics overflow: the fix round 141 itself had already scoped out

Per the user's own device confirmation that lyrics scroll below the
real panel boundary round 141 measured: moved in_content_rect/
pl_list_rect's own start up from y=560 to y=545 (a smaller, 10px gap
after the title's own underline decoration at y=535, reclaiming space
round 120 had left more generous), and reduced their own height from
340/428 down to 293 (545 to the real 838 boundary round 141 measured).
Scaled test_skin_lyrics_rows down further to match: the new 13-row
total is 282px, computed and printed directly rather than assumed,
leaving an 11px safety margin inside the genuinely available 293px --
matching this project's own established safety-margin convention
(round 119/120's own 8px) rather than filling the box exactly.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
sweep clean (only the known "error" false positive). Ran the mandatory
skin-XML validation across all three variants x both tiers. Directly
queried the generated test_skin XML for every one of the six new
hint_text_* widgets' own position/size, confirming MENU's own right
edge and EPG/INFO's own left edge both clear the measured badge zone
with margin, rather than trusting the coordinate arithmetic alone.
Computed the new test_skin_lyrics_rows total (282) and confirmed it
against the new 293px budget programmatically before trusting the
numbers. Visually reviewed the regenerated HD and SD background
images at every stage (opaque bar only implied by re-rendering over
the already-modified working file, confirmed the old garbled text was
fully covered by the new same-rect, now-opaque plate) before
finalizing, rather than assuming success from the code alone. Not yet
confirmed on a real device -- and, per round 141's own still-standing
caveat, an already-populated Test Skin folder needs the four
regenerated images copied in manually to actually show this change.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.035.

## Round 143 (2026-09-09) -- test_skin hint bar reverted to plain background; TXT-format lyrics font/line-count fixed

Per direct request (with the user's own preserved "before" copies of
the background images attached), reverted round 141/142's own drawn
overlay plate entirely -- this skin reads better with hint text sitting
directly on the plain photograph (transparent, matching Light/Dark's
own approach) than with a solid card drawn over the grille/badge.
Also fixed TXT-format lyrics legibility on test_skin, confirmed
separately by the user as still hard to read even after round 142's
own height fix.

### Background images: reverted using the user's own files directly

resources/skins/test_skin_template/hd/mainscreen_player_active.png
and mainscreen_info_active.png replaced with the user's own uploaded
copies (1808x1024, confirmed byte-identical to each other); the SD
tier derived by resizing those same two files rather than hand-edited
separately, keeping both tiers genuinely in sync. Removed the whole
overlay-drawing step from this round's own process entirely -- there
is no bar/plate/badge-hiding logic left for test_skin at all now,
only the six hint_text_* XML widgets sitting transparently on the
plain image, the same mechanism Light/Dark already uses.

### Hint text repositioned

OK/MENU/HELP keep their own round 142 positions (already clear of the
badge, unaffected by removing the plate since the badge itself was
never actually hidden by anything other than round 141/142's own now-
removed bar). BLUE/EPG-INFO/EXIT shifted right per the direct request
("blue, epg ja exit vähän oikealle"). Rendered a full mockup of the
plain background with all six labels at their final positions and
viewed it directly before finalizing -- matches the user's own
reference photo closely.

### TXT-format lyrics: a real, separate gap from LRC's own already-fixed sizing

Round 142 only touched the shared in_content_rect/pl_list_rect height
and LRC's own tiered test_skin_lyrics_rows -- LRC format's own live-
windowed rendering (InformationPanel.getCurrentLyricsWindowData())
already used those correctly. Plain TXT-format lyrics go through a
completely different path (the single-Label getCurrentContent()/
_visible_lines mechanism, confirmed by reading that method's own
docstring directly) that was never touched: DEFAULT_VISIBLE_LINES (14,
information_panel.py's own module constant) applied unconditionally
regardless of skin variant, and info_content's own font size (28) was
a single hardcoded literal in _buildSkin() -- neither was ever adapted
for test_skin's own smaller 293px box, meaning 14 lines' worth of text
at 28pt was being requested for a box roughly a third too small,
explaining the reported illegibility (round 142's own fix, aimed only
at LRC, never touched this separate path at all).

Fixed by computing both numbers together rather than independently:
test_skin's own visible_lines set to 6 (InformationPanel(visible_
lines=...), computed at MainScreen.__init__ time based on self.
_skin_variant, which _buildSkin() already sets before InformationPanel
is constructed -- confirmed the ordering directly rather than assumed)
and info_content's own font size raised to 32 for test_skin
specifically (still 28, unchanged, for Light/Dark) -- an estimate
(6 lines * ~44px/line ≈ 266px, comfortably inside the real 293px
budget) rather than an empirically-verified Enigma2 line-height
figure for this exact font, stated as such rather than presented as
exact.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile;
AST sweep clean (only the known "error" false positive). Ran the
mandatory skin-XML validation across all three variants x both tiers.
Directly queried the generated XML for all six repositioned hint_text_*
widgets and for info_content's own new font size, confirming test_skin
reads 32 while Light/Dark both still read 28 unchanged. Rendered and
visually reviewed a full mockup of the six hint labels on the restored
plain background before finalizing. Not yet confirmed on a real
device whether visible_lines=6/font=32 actually fits cleanly within
293px -- the line-height ratio behind that number is an estimate, not
verified against Enigma2's own real font metrics, flagged as such to
the user.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.036.

## Round 144 (2026-09-10) -- TXT lyrics root cause found (fix deferred); per-language help documents

Two separate pieces of work: correctly diagnosing (and NOT
half-fixing) the TXT lyrics font issue, and adding per-language
support for the Information/guide documents.

### TXT lyrics: root cause confirmed, a third wrong-path fix reverted before shipping

The user reported round 143's own two changes (visible_lines=6,
info_content font 32) had ZERO effect -- still 13 small lines. Rather
than guessing again, traced the actual code path and found the real
cause in information_panel.py's own existing comment (~lines 355-367):
an earlier round deliberately changed the condition from
`lyrics.get("synchronized") and elapsed is not None` to just
`elapsed is not None`, so UNSYNCHRONIZED (TXT) lyrics go through the
exact same windowed rendering path as LRC -- the 13 lyrics_line_N
widgets via _showLyricsWindow(). info_content is .hide()-den entirely
in that state, so neither visible_lines nor info_content's own font
size can affect TXT lyrics at all. Round 143's own changes were
therefore on a dead path for this symptom from the start.

Critically: this round had ALREADY started a third fix on that same
wrong path (a word-wrap/chars_per_line hypothesis in
InformationPanel.getCurrentContent()) before the real cause was
found. That change was fully REVERTED rather than shipped -- confirmed
by grep that zero traces of _chars_per_line/_visualLineCount remain.
Shipping it would have been the third consecutive failed attempt at
the same symptom.

The correct fix (a test_skin-specific ROW COUNT, e.g. 7 larger rows
instead of 13 small ones) is documented in detail in HANDOFF's own
"KNOWN ISSUE" section, including all three call sites that must
change together, and deliberately NOT started -- it's a coordinated
multi-site change where rushing on a dwindling budget would likely
produce a fourth failure.

### Per-language help/Information documents

Per direct request ("epg/info napilla aukeamaan omankielinen ohje jos
saatavilla, mutta muuten näytetään englanninkielinen"): GuideManager.
getGuide() now tries resources/help/<lang>/<screen_id>.md first,
falling back to the existing untranslated resources/help/<screen_id>.md
whenever no translation exists. English is the source language these
documents are written in, so its own files stay directly in help/ --
no help/en/ subfolder is looked for at all.

Deliberately does NOT fall back to a different non-English translation
-- only the current language or English, so a Spanish user with no
Spanish documents sees English, never Finnish. Verified by simulation
specifically (scenario 4 below) rather than assumed from the code
structure.

Per the direct request to hold off on wider translation while further
changes are still likely, translated ONLY MainScreen's own three
panel documents into Finnish (help/fi/player.md, playlist.md,
information.md -- MainScreen's own help is panel-dependent, so all
three together are "MainScreen's help"). Every other screen keeps
showing English, which the fallback handles with no code change
needed. Adding a language later needs no code change either, just new
files in the right folder.

Also extended GuideManager.getDiagnostics() to report current_language
and which translated document sets exist -- confirmed first that the
existing available_documents listing's own ".md" filter already
excludes the new subfolder, so that list is genuinely unchanged.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for both touched files
(guide_manager.py, information_panel.py after its own revert); AST
sweep on guide_manager.py completely clean (not even the usual
"error" false positive). Simulated the document-resolution logic
standalone with real files on disk across five scenarios before
trusting it: (1) Finnish user with a Finnish document -> Finnish;
(2) Finnish user, no Finnish document for that screen -> English;
(3) English user -> English directly, never looking for help/en/;
(4) Spanish user with no Spanish documents at all -> English, NOT
Finnish; (5) missing screen entirely -> the existing fallback message.
Confirmed by grep that the reverted round-144 wrap logic left zero
traces. Not yet confirmed on a real device that EPG/INFO now opens
Finnish on MainScreen and English elsewhere.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.037.

## Round 146 (2026-09-10) -- systematic colour-button audit across seven screens

Per direct request, went through every screen's own context/action
menus systematically to find real, currently-menu-only actions common
enough to deserve a direct colour-key shortcut, rather than only
reacting to individual requests as in rounds 132-133. Presented six
concrete candidates plus grouped options to the user; all six were
accepted, plus a genuine follow-up gap the user caught while
reviewing them.

### MainScreen: YELLOW = show lyrics fullscreen

Direct shortcut to the OK menu's own existing "Show lyrics
fullscreen" choice (_showLyricsFullscreen()) -- that method already
handles "no lyrics yet" gracefully on its own (shows a message
instead of an empty screen), so no extra guard was needed.

### BrowserScreen: YELLOW/BLUE = move playlist track up/down

New yellowPressed()/bluePressed(), both delegating to a new shared
_moveSelectedPlaylistTrack(direction) helper -- reuses
playlist_manager.moveTrack() exactly as the Playlist column's own
existing "Move up"/"Move down" menu choices already do. No-op outside
the Playlist column or when nothing is selected there.

### MusicLibraryScreen: BLUE = update library

Direct shortcut to the MENU screen's own existing "Update Library"
choice -- previously two steps (MENU, then Update Library) for a
common action.

### RadioBrowserScreen: GREEN = add to favorites

Direct shortcut to the station menu's own existing "Add to
Favorites" choice for whichever station is currently selected in the
Stations column.

### PodcastScreen: GREEN = subscribe/add episode, RED = unsubscribe

GREEN is column-dependent: subscribes in the Available column
(guarded against re-subscribing to an already-subscribed podcast,
matching the existing menu's own "Already subscribed" no-op choice),
adds to a playlist in the Episodes column, and is a no-op in the
Subscribed column (re-subscribing has no meaning there). RED
unsubscribes in the Subscribed column specifically, no-op elsewhere.

### PlaylistScreen: BLUE = remove selected single track

Deliberately distinct from RED's own existing clearPlaylistPressed()
(clears the WHOLE playlist) -- BLUE removes just the currently
selected track/station, reusing the exact same is_radio-aware branch
_trackMenuChosen()'s own "remove" action already uses. No-op in the
Playlists column, which has no single "track" of its own to remove.

### Follow-up gap found and fixed: Move Up/Down never worked for radio favorites at all

While reviewing the six candidates, the user caught a real, separate
issue: PlaylistScreen's own "Move Up"/"Move Down" choices only ever
appeared for LOCAL entries (_openTrackMenu()'s own radio branch never
offered them, and _trackMenuChosen()'s own handling explicitly
excluded radio with "and not is_radio"). The reason: internetradio_
manager had NO reorder capability of its own at all before this
round. Added moveFavorite(list_name, index, direction) -- mirrors
playlist_manager.moveTrack()'s own signature and swap-based approach
exactly, operating on self._favorites's own per-list entry arrays.
Updated both _openTrackMenu() (radio branch now offers Move Up/Move
Down too) and _trackMenuChosen() (both branches now call the

### Testing

## Round 148 (2026-09-11) -- test_skin's TXT-format lyrics fix: implemented per the round 143/144 plan

Per direct request, implemented the fix HANDOFF's own "KNOWN ISSUE"
section had already scoped out in detail after rounds 143/144's own
two failed attempts and confirmed root cause. Per the direct request,
scoped to test_skin specifically (fullscreen lyrics -- a separate
screen entirely, LyricsFullscreenScreen -- was never touched and
confirmed by grep to reference none of the changed constants).

### Two of the three planned call sites needed no change at all

Re-reading _showLyricsWindow()/_hideLyricsWindow() directly (rather
than trusting the round 143/144 plan's own summary from memory) found
both already iterate self._lyrics_window_rows -- the per-instance,
variant-aware stash _buildSkin() already sets -- not the shared
13-row MainScreen.LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS class constant. Widget creation
in __init__ also needed no change: it already creates the full 13
lyrics_line_N widgets unconditionally regardless of variant (cheap,
and skin XML simply doesn't anchor the unused ones for a variant with
fewer rows), matching the plan's own "creating 13 widgets is
harmless" note.

### The one real remaining gap

_updateInformationPanel()'s own call to getCurrentLyricsWindowData()
still passed len(self.LYRICS_WINDOW_ROWS) (the fixed class constant,
13) instead of len(self._lyrics_window_rows) (the active variant's
own count) -- meaning the manager was always asked to window 13
lines' worth of data regardless of how many rows the active skin
could actually display. Fixed to read the same per-instance stash the
display code already used.

test_skin_lyrics_rows shrunk from 13 rows (round 142's own smaller-
font fix) to 7, in the same 3-normal/1-current/3-normal shape as the
original 13-row design, each row genuinely bigger: 36px/26pt normal
rows, 52px/37pt for the current/bold one. Total 268px, a 25px margin
inside the real 293px budget round 141 measured (more generous than
round 142's own 11px margin, since there was much more room to work
with at 7 rows). Confirmed via a standalone simulation of
lyrics_manager.py's own _windowAroundPadded() that a 7-line window's
own "current" index (window_size // 2 = 3) lines up exactly with
test_skin_lyrics_rows' own bigger/bold row at that same index --
window_size is a fully generic parameter there, nothing 13-specific
needed changing on that side either.

### Accepted side effect

This same row configuration is shared between LRC and TXT (both go
through _showLyricsWindow(); only the per-row font-size tiers are
suppressed for unsynchronized/.txt lyrics specifically, per round
93's own existing "fonttia ei suurenneta kun ajoitus ei ole
tiedossa" behaviour) -- LRC now also shows fewer, bigger lines on
test_skin. Treated as a likely welcome side effect rather than a
regression, since the request was about legibility generally and
bigger text benefits LRC too; not separately requested but not
contrary to anything asked either.

### Testing

Confirmed mainscreen.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile;
AST sweep clean (only the known "error" false positive); full src/
directory syntax sweep also clean. Ran the mandatory skin-XML
validation across all three variants x both tiers, directly counting
generated lyrics_line_N widget definitions per variant: confirmed
Light/Dark both still generate exactly 13 (unchanged), test_skin now

(a stand-in for lyrics_manager.py's own _windowAroundPadded(), a real
20-line lyrics sample, center_index=10, window_size=7) and confirmed

## Round 149 (2026-09-11) -- test_skin's colour palette extended to every other screen

Per direct request ("test_skinin muiden ikkunoiden väriteema
mainscreenin mukaiseksi"), scoped down to colours/text only after
asking (not background images/icons, per the user's own explicit
choice between the two options offered). A real device screenshot
showed exactly why: LyricsFullscreenScreen's own LRC lyrics were
still plain white-on-slate-blue, nothing like MainScreen's own warm
amber/brass look.

### Root cause: a crash-fix from round 112 that was never updated

Six screens (BrowserScreen, MusicLibraryScreen, PlaylistScreen,
PodcastScreen, RadioBrowserScreen, SettingsScreen) plus MainMenu all
had the exact same line: `SKIN_PALETTES["test_skin"] = SKIN_PALETTES
["dark"]` -- a straight alias, added in round 112 purely to fix a
KeyError crash (test_skin had been added to each screen's own variant
whitelist in round 110 without a matching palette entry). At the
time this was a reasonable stopgap, since test_skin's own background
images for every screen still started as literal copies of Dark's
own PNGs. But MainScreen's own test_skin evolved into a completely
different amber/brass vintage-radio look from round 113 onward, and
this alias was never revisited anywhere else -- every other screen
kept silently reading Dark's own plain blue/white values.
LyricsFullscreenScreen had the same gap through a slightly different
mechanism (a direct `if self._skin_variant in ("dark", "test_skin")`
string comparison rather than a dict alias, added in the same round
112 crash-fix pass).

### Implementation

Each of the six dict-based screens' own alias line replaced with a
real, standalone test_skin dict -- amber/brass values copied directly
from MainScreen's own MAINSCREEN_SKIN_PALETTES["test_skin"]
(panel_text_color #E8A24C, header_inactive_fg #C08A45, header_active_
fg #FFC978, hint_fg #FFC978) so every screen reads the exact same
amber, not a slightly different shade per screen. panel_background_
color/list_background_color kept close to Dark's own tone but warmed
toward brown (#1C1610/#161108 vs Dark's own blue-grey #1C202B/
#161922), since the request was colours/text only and these screens'
own background images (still Dark-style layouts) were left untouched.
selected_row_bg/selected_row_fg redesigned as a solid mid-amber band
with dark brown text (#C08A45/#1A1206), matching the amber-on-dark
selection style already visible in MainScreen's own playlist column.
Each screen's own exact key set was read individually first (settings
screen and mainmenu.py both use a different, smaller key set than the
other four -- e.g. a single header_fg instead of header_inactive_fg/
header_active_fg, radiobrowserscreen.py has no info_label_fg at all)
rather than copy-pasting one universal dict everywhere.

LyricsFullscreenScreen's own inline comparison was split into a
proper three-way branch (light / dark / test_skin) instead of the
former two-way (light / everything-else) one, giving test_skin its
own panel_background_color, panel_text_color, and title_foreground_
color values instead of continuing to fall into the dark branch.

### An open question flagged to the user, not guessed at

The screenshot that prompted this round showed a solid blue bar
behind LyricsFullscreenScreen's own title text ("Tiedot: Sanoitukset
(LRC)") -- but that title widget's own skin XML is `transparent="1"`
with no background fill of its own anywhere in this file, and no
other widget or rectangle in the file's own generated XML sits behind
it either. Rather than guess at an explanation (a receiver's own
global skin default for bold Label widgets seemed the most likely
candidate, but this could not be confirmed by reading the code alone)
this was left for the user to identify on the actual device, since
they can see the running app and grep alone couldn't resolve it.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for all seven touched
files (six screens plus lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py) and the whole
src/ directory as a final sweep. AST sweep clean on lyrics_fullscreen_
screen.py (only the known "error" false positive). Wrote a
standalone script that programmatically extracted every "dark" and
new "test_skin" dict from each of the seven files' own source (real
brace-matching on the actual file text, not hand-copied values) and
compared their key sets directly -- confirmed all seven now have an
EXACT match between test_skin's own keys and dark's own keys, with
nothing missing and nothing extra, ruling out the specific class of
KeyError crash round 112 was originally responding to. Also
cross-checked, before writing each screen's own dict, every
palette['key'] reference actually appearing in that screen's own
_buildSkin()-equivalent function, confirming each screen's own
particular key set (some lack info_label_fg, list_background_color,
or use header_fg instead of header_inactive_fg/header_active_fg)
rather than assuming one shared shape for all seven. Not yet
confirmed on a real device.

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.042.

## Round 150 (2026-09-12) -- LyricsFullscreenScreen's own background/row seam fixed; open question resolved

Two real device photos answered round 149's own open question and

directly. No change made to the title bar.

### The real bug: round 149 recoloured the row fill without touching the image it sits on

A second photo showed thin horizontal seams between each lyrics row
on test_skin. Root cause: round 149 changed panel_background_color
for test_skin from the old #1C202B (matching this screen's own
background image body colour exactly) to #1C1610 (brown, matching
MainScreen) -- but never touched lyrics_fullscreen_background.png
itself, which still had its own #1C202B body. Since lyrics_line_N
widgets don't tile with zero gap between rows, the small spaces
between them let the still-blue-grey image show through against the
now-brown row fills, reading as horizontal stripes.

### Fix: transparent rows instead of a matched fill colour, plus a recoloured image

Per the direct request, made this robust two ways rather than one:
1. lyrics_fullscreen_background.png's own body and bottom hint-bar
   area recoloured from #1C202B to #1C1610 (a precise, sampled colour

   title bar itself deliberately untouched, per the user's own
   explicit approval above.

   replaced with a new panel_background_attr value that resolves to
   transparent="1" for test_skin specifically (Light/Dark keep their
   own existing opaque fill, unchanged) -- so even if a future image
   change drifts from the row fill colour again, there's no fill
   colour left to drift out of sync with in the first place.

full src/ directory sweep also clean. Built a proper mock harness for

DESIGN_WIDTH/HEIGHT and _resolveLyricsFullscreenSkinVariant())	and
ran the mandatory skin-XML validation across all three variants,
directly counting transparent="1" vs opaque backgroundColor fills per
variant: confirmed Light/Dark both still show 16 opaque fills/1
pre-existing transparent (the title, unchanged), while test_skin now
shows 16 transparent/1 remaining opaque fill (the screen's own base
backgroundColor, a different, intentionally-untouched variable).
Sampled the recoloured image's own dominant colours after generation
(only three: the new brown body, the untouched blue title bar, and
the dark border) to confirm no leftover blue-grey pixels anywhere,

## Round 152 (2026-09-12) -- MusicLibraryScreen scrollbar fix; storage-mount race condition fixed (radio database/playlists/favorites lost on some reboots)

Two direct requests: extend round 151's own scrollbar fix to
MusicLibraryScreen after all (the user had asked to leave it out
initially, then asked for it once confirmed it has the identical
issue), and investigate why the radio station database "wants to
re-download every time the device restarts."

### MusicLibraryScreen scrollbar

Same fix as round 151's own five screens: scrollbar_bg resolves to
"#3A2E1A" for test_skin, "#E0E0E0" (unchanged) otherwise. No "info"-
style widget exists in this screen at all (confirmed by grep), so
only the scrollbar half of round 151's own fix applies here.

### The real cause of the radio "always re-downloads" symptom

Traced through the actual device log rather than guessing: the "No
stations available yet" download prompt fired, and the user's own OK
keypress confirmed it, on a boot whose /media/hdd was independently
confirmed writable moments earlier in that same log (Diagnostics'
own cache path check). This pointed away from "genuinely no drive"
and toward a startup race: storage.py's own StorageManager is a
module-level singleton, so _pickWorkingDirectoryParent() runs once,
synchronously, the moment storage.py is first imported during plugin
startup -- often only moments after boot, potentially before a
slower-to-mount external USB HDD has actually finished mounting yet.
The function's own single, immediate check had no way to distinguish
"genuinely unavailable" from "not ready yet", and picked the
non-persistent /tmp fallback for the latter case too -- silently
losing that entire boot's own playlists, favorites, AND radio station
database the moment anything got written there, with no message to
the user beyond a log line.

### Fix: retry before falling back, bounded to avoid a needless cost for drive-less receivers

_pickWorkingDirectoryParent() now retries for up to 3 seconds (12
attempts, 250ms apart) before giving up and choosing /tmp -- long
enough to cover a typical slow-mounting HDD finishing shortly after
plugin import, short enough that a receiver with no external drive at
all (for which /tmp is a legitimate, permanent choice, not a failure)
doesn't pay a large fixed startup delay it could never benefit from.
Deliberately chose 3 seconds over a longer window (5 seconds was the
first draft) specifically to keep that no-drive-at-all case's own
worst-case startup cost modest, since a longer timeout only helps the
slow-mount case while actively hurting the no-drive case every single
boot.

### Testing

Confirmed storage.py's own syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile; AST
sweep clean (only the known "error" false positive); full src/
directory sweep also clean. Simulated the retry logic standalone with
a mock "becomes ready after N internal checks" helper across three
scenarios: ready immediately (returns on the very first check, no
wasted attempts), ready after a few retries (returns as soon as it
becomes available, not fixed at some later attempt), and never ready
(exhausts all 12 attempts before correctly falling back to /tmp) --
all three behaved correctly. musiclibraryscreen.py's own fix verified
the same way as round 151's five screens (AST sweep, grep confirming
the pattern replaced everywhere it existed). Not yet confirmed on a
real device that this actually resolves the reboot-timing issue --
the fix is a reasoned response to what the log evidence pointed at,
not something reproduced and re-tested directly, since the failure
mode is inherently timing-dependent and couldn't be forced on demand
here.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.045.

---

## Round 154 (2026-09-12) -- systematic sweep: every screen's own EPG/HELP guide text was stale since round 132/139/146/147

Started from a narrow, direct request (fix RadioBrowserScreen's own
guide text for EPG and BLUE) and found the same underlying bug
repeated across every single screen in the app: rounds 132 (EPG/INFO
repurposed to open this exact guide, search/other functions moved to
colour keys) and 139 (HELP renamed from "Help" to "Information"/
native-guide framing) had both changed real behaviour years' worth of
rounds ago, but the guide text and hint-bar labels describing that
behaviour were never updated to match, anywhere.

### Code-level stale text (two real bugs, not just documentation)

self["hint_text_info"] still read "INFO: Search" in both
radiobrowserscreen.py and podcastscreen.py, despite both screens'
own infoPressed() opening this exact guide since round 132/139 --
search moved to YELLOW in both. Fixed both to "INFO: Information".
musiclibraryscreen.py's own equivalent widget (confusingly still
named hint_text_info internally) already had the correct "YELLOW:
Search" text -- confirmed by reading it directly rather than assuming
the same bug existed there too.

### Documentation-level: seven .md files, all describing pre-round-132 behaviour

- radiobrowserscreen.md, podcastscreen.md, musiclibraryscreen.md: all
  three said "INFO: search ... by name" -- rewritten to "YELLOW:
  search ... by name" plus a new "EPG / INFO: open this guide" line.
  Also added GREEN/RED (favorites, subscribe/unsubscribe, playlist
  add/remove) and, for musiclibraryscreen.md, BLUE (update library) --
  none of which had ever been documented at all despite existing
  since rounds 133/146.
- browserscreen.md: "INFO: choose which playlist..." was describing
  round-132-era behaviour that GREEN took over in round 133 (context-
  dependent: playlist picker in the Playlist column, add-to-playlist
  in Directories/Files) -- rewritten completely, including RED
  (remove from playlist) and YELLOW/BLUE (move up/down, round 146),
  none of which appeared in this document at all before.
- playlistscreen.md: "INFO: show information for whichever panel..."
  was ALSO round-132-era. Also contained a claim round 147 quietly
  made false -- "actions like ... reordering only make sense for
  local playlists" -- when round 147's own direct request specifically
  added reordering to radio favourite lists too. Rewritten with GREEN
  (create playlist), YELLOW/BLUE (move up/down), and RED's own
  current context-dependent delete/remove behaviour, and the Notes
  section corrected to no longer claim radio lists lack reordering.
- settingsscreen.md: "INFO: show the current setting's name and
  value" described something that, on inspection, was never actually
  a KEY action at all -- self["info"] updates automatically as the
  selection moves (confirmed by reading _buildList()'s own
  description-dict mechanism directly), not on any keypress. Removed
  from Keys entirely and reframed as a Notes-section fact about
  always-visible behaviour, rather than continuing to misdescribe it
  as a button function.
- mainmenu.md: never mentioned EPG/INFO opening this same guide at
  all (a gap since round 132, not a wrong description) -- added the
  line; "HELP: show this help" also corrected to describe the native
  Enigma2 guide HELP actually opens since round 130.

Grepped every remaining .md file afterward for "show this help" and
"INFO: search/choose/show" as a final sweep -- zero matches anywhere,
confirming no eighth instance was missed.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for both touched Python
files (radiobrowserscreen.py, podcastscreen.py) and the whole src/
directory as a final sweep. AST sweep clean on both (only the known
"error" false positive). Read each screen's own actual current
key-binding dict directly before rewriting its own document, rather
than trusting the OLD document's own claims about what any key did --
this is what caught playlistscreen.md's own false "radio lists can't
reorder" claim, which a text-only pass would have missed since it
wasn't the specific EPG/BLUE complaint that started this round. Not
yet confirmed on a real device.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.047.
