## Round 155 (2026-09-12) -- native help translated (Finnish); Vintage Radio graduated into its own independent skin choice

Two direct requests before the planned 1.1.000 version bump: fill the
last known documentation gap (round 130's own native per-button help
text, never translated into any language), and turn Test Skin's own
current vintage-radio look into a permanent, independent skin choice
sitting alongside Test Skin rather than replacing it.

### Native help translation

Extracted every help_text_by_handler dict across all 11 screens that
have one (browserscreen.py, coverart_fullscreen_screen.py,
guide_screen.py, lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py, mainmenu.py,
mainscreen.py, musiclibraryscreen.py, playlistscreen.py,
podcastscreen.py, radiobrowserscreen.py, settingsscreen.py) --
61 unique strings total, confirmed by grep that zero already existed
anywhere in po/fi.po despite already being wrapped in _() calls in
the source (the gettext plumbing was correct from round 130 onward;
only the translation entries themselves were missing). Wrote Finnish
translations for all 61 and appended them to po/fi.po. Confirmed the
file still compiles cleanly with msgfmt (no duplicate msgid errors)
and spot-checked four translations by reading them back out of the
compiled .mo directly, rather than trusting the append alone.

### Vintage Radio: a genuinely independent copy, not an alias

Per the user's own explicit choice (asked directly, since the three
possible interpretations differed by an order of magnitude in scope):
a fully separate, independent skin option sitting in the Settings
list alongside Test Skin -- both continue to work independently from
here on, editing one has no effect on the other.

Investigated resolve_skin_asset_path() (skin.py) first, before
touching anything: confirmed its own special-case only triggers for
the literal string "test_skin" (redirecting to StorageManager's own
writable, user-editable directory); any other variant name -- Light,
Dark, or now Vintage Radio -- falls through to the plugin's own
bundled resources/skins/<variant>/ tree, unchanged, no code needed to
add this behaviour for the new variant. Also checked plugin.py's own
_ensureTestSkinTemplate() (round 110's own template-copy-on-first-use
logic) -- confirmed it only ever matches the literal string
"test_skin" too, so it can never mistakenly apply to Vintage Radio.

Implementation, in order:
1. Copied resources/skins/test_skin_template/ (all 38 PNGs, both
   tiers) to a new resources/skins/vintage_radio/ -- a real, separate
   folder read directly like Light/Dark's own bundled trees, not tied
   to test_skin's own writable-directory mechanism at all. Removed
   the template-only SKIN_ELEMENTS.md/skin.json, which don't apply to
   a permanent, non-editable skin. Confirmed byte-identical to the
   source template with diff -rq before moving on.
2. config.py: added ("vintage_radio", "Vintage Radio") to cfg.
   appearance.skin's own ConfigSelection choices.
3. Nine files (mainscreen.py, browserscreen.py, musiclibraryscreen.py,
   playlistscreen.py, podcastscreen.py, radiobrowserscreen.py,
   settingsscreen.py, mainmenu.py, lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py): added
   "vintage_radio" to each file's own SKIN_VARIANTS tuple; added a
   genuinely independent copy (not a reference) of that file's own
   test_skin palette dict under "vintage_radio" -- editing one from
   here on has no effect on the other, per the user's own explicit
   requirement; and widened every self._skin_variant == "test_skin"
   check to self._skin_variant in ("test_skin", "vintage_radio")
   (mainscreen.py had 8 such checks, lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py had
   3, the six dict-palette screens had 1 each for round 151's own
   scrollbar/info fix, mainmenu.py had none at all). coverart_
   fullscreen_screen.py and guide_screen.py were confirmed (by grep)
   to have no test_skin awareness at all, so nothing to extend there.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for all ten touched

## Round 156 (2026-09-13) -- OpenATV's own HELP button root cause found and fixed; a small native-help content merge

Cross-referenced against three device logs (OpenViX, OpenBH, OpenATV):
the Finnish translation and Vintage Radio work from round 155 both
confirmed working on OpenViX and OpenBH. OpenATV showed a real,
different bug: pressing HELP opened this project's own guide (the
same content EPG/INFO opens) instead of Enigma2's own native
per-screen button guide.

### Root cause found with real log evidence, not guessed at

compatibility.py's own HELP_KEY_ACTIONS lists six candidate action
names for the physical HELP key: ("displayHelp", "displayHelpLong",
"HELP", "help", "showHelp", "keyHelp"). Round 130's own design
excluded only "displayHelp" from each screen's own local ActionMap
binding (letting HelpableScreen's own native mixin handle it
exclusively), routing every OTHER name in that list to infoPressed()
as a fallback for images using a different naming convention for
their OWN primary HELP binding.

The OpenATV log's own raw ActionMap trace showed the actual problem
with this assumption: "Context 'HelpActions' keyName 'KEY_HELP' (138)
mapped to 'displayHelp'" AND "...mapped to 'displayHelpLong'" both
appear for the SAME physical key on this same image -- "displayHelpLong"
isn't a different image's own alternate PRIMARY binding the way the
other HELP_KEY_ACTIONS entries are; it's a SECOND action HelpableScreen's
own native mechanism also listens for, registered alongside
"displayHelp" itself on at least this image. Since only "displayHelp"
was excluded, "displayHelpLong": infoPressed() stayed live in every
screen's own local ActionMap -- and a local ActionMap binding always
wins over a mixed-in base class's own, per round 130's own established
reasoning for why "displayHelp" itself needed excluding in the first
place. On any image where the same HELP keypress fires both action
names (OpenATV, per this log), the local "displayHelpLong" binding
wins the key over the native mechanism -- explaining exactly the
observed symptom (HELP behaving identically to EPG/INFO).

### Fix

All nine screens with this exact exclusion pattern (mainscreen.py,
browserscreen.py, guide_screen.py, mainmenu.py, musiclibraryscreen.py,
playlistscreen.py, podcastscreen.py, radiobrowserscreen.py,
settingsscreen.py -- confirmed by grep this was the complete set)
updated to exclude "displayHelpLong" the same way as "displayHelp".
coverart_fullscreen_screen.py and lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py were
confirmed (by grep) to have no HELP key handling of their own at all,
so nothing to change there.

### Small content fix: native help text merged, per direct request

mainscreen.py's own help_text_by_handler had separate "previous radio
station (internet radio only)" / "next radio station (internet radio
only)" entries for upPressed/downPressed -- per direct request,
merged into "previous track/station" / "next track/station" (UP/DOWN
already do double duty for local tracks and radio stations depending
on context; the longer parenthetical was specific to the radio case
only). Updated the matching Finnish translations in po/fi.po to match
-- the longer full guide document's own more detailed phrasing
(resources/help/mainscreen.md, which already correctly distinguished
the two cases) was deliberately left alone, since the request was
specifically about the native tooltip text, a different, more
compact context.

### Testing

Confirmed syntax with ast.parse()/py_compile for all nine touched
screen files plus mainscreen.py's own separate content edit, and the
whole src/ directory as a final sweep. AST sweep clean on all nine
(browserscreen.py had zero matches at all; the rest only the known
"error" false positive). Validated po/fi.po still compiles cleanly
with msgfmt after the msgid/msgstr updates. Confirmed by grep that
coverart_fullscreen_screen.py/lyrics_fullscreen_screen.py have no
HELP key handling to extend. This fix is a reasoned response to
concrete log evidence (the exact action-name collision is visible
directly in the OpenATV trace), not a guess -- but genuinely unverified
on the device itself yet, since the tool used to inspect the log
cannot press a physical remote button. This is the user's own next
test before finalizing 1.1.000.

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.0.049,
still a test build -- 1.1.000 follows once this is confirmed.

---

## Round 156 follow-up (2026-09-13) -- OpenATV HELP issue: root cause found for real; confirmed a platform limitation, not fixable here

Round 156's own "displayHelpLong" exclusion fix, once actually tested
on the device, had zero effect -- a second OpenATV log confirmed the
symptom was completely unchanged. Traced that second log properly
rather than accepting the first theory as correct just because it was
plausible:

```
[eRCDeviceInputDev] 1 8a 1                              <- physical HELP key, raw scancode
[InfoBarGenerics] Key 'KEY_INFO' (0x166 - 358) Make.     <- OpenATV's own input layer relabels it KEY_INFO
[ActionMap] Map screen 'MainScreen' context 'InfoActions' -> Action 'info'.
[MainScreen] INFO pressed.
```

OpenATV's own input layer translates the physical HELP button's raw
scancode directly into KEY_INFO *before* the keypress ever reaches
any Enigma2 ActionMap context at all -- confirmed directly from the
log's own sequence, not inferred. The keypress never touches
"HelpActions"/"displayHelp"/"displayHelpLong" on this platform, so no
exclusion at the ActionMap level (round 156's own fix, or any
variation of it) can ever affect this -- separating "this one
physical key" from "InfoActions" would also break EPG/INFO's own
correct behaviour, since both share that exact same context here.

The user independently confirmed this is a genuine OpenATV 8.0.0-beta
platform limitation by testing other apps and plugins on the same
image: HELP doesn't open the native remote-control guide anywhere on
that platform, not just in this project. Documented in HANDOFF as a
known, platform-level limitation rather than pursued further --
round 156's own "displayHelpLong" exclusion is still a correct,
worthwhile fix in its own right (a genuine gap in the original round
130 design, confirmed by the OpenATV keymap dump showing both actions
registered together on other platforms), just not the fix for this
particular symptom on this particular platform.

This is worth remembering as a pattern for the future: a plausible,
well-reasoned first theory from log evidence is still a theory until
the fix built on it is actually tested on the device it was meant to
fix. Reading the SAME log more carefully a second time (following the
actual causal chain a level deeper -- from the ActionMap dispatch
down to the raw input event) found the real answer that the first
pass's own narrower search had missed entirely.

---

## Round 158 (2026-09-13) -- GitHub Actions autotag workflow added

Per direct request (a message from the programmer considering
MediaPlayer3 for the OpenViX repo, referencing a specific commit --
DimitarCC/e2-emby-client@c346ecd -- as the exact pattern to follow):
added a workflow that creates a git tag automatically whenever
src/__init__.py's own version string changes on push to main.

### __init__.py made the single source of truth for the version

The referenced workflow reads the version via a plain shell grep --
`grep -oP '(?<=__version__ = ")[^"]+' src/__init__.py` -- which can
only ever find a literal, hardcoded string in that exact file; it has
no way to resolve a Python import or evaluate any expression. Given
project.py's own VERSION constant was already the project's own
existing source of truth, referenced by several other modules
(coverart_manager.py, internetradio_manager.py, lrclib_manager.py,
skin.py, version.py), duplicating the literal string in __init__.py
as well would have created two places to update on every release,
with a real risk of the two drifting out of sync (a plausible failure
mode given how many rapid version bumps this project has already
been through). Reversed the relationship instead: __init__.py now
defines `__version__ = "1.1.000"` as the actual literal string the
workflow's own regex needs, and project.py's own VERSION imports it
(`from . import __version__ as VERSION`) rather than defining its own
copy -- confirmed with a standalone package-import simulation (a real
package directory in /tmp, not just a syntax check) that this
resolves correctly with no circular import, and that VERSION and
__init__.py's own __version__ match exactly.

### A real mistake caught before shipping

The comment explaining this change originally included the literal
example text `__version__ = "X.Y.Z"` -- which the exact same grep
pattern the workflow uses would ALSO match, alongside the real
`__version__ = "1.1.000"` line, producing two results instead of one
and silently breaking the workflow's own version detection the
moment it ran (a malformed, multi-line "version" string, most likely
failing the tag-creation step outright). Caught by literally running
the same grep command the workflow itself uses against the real file
before finalizing, not just visually reviewing the diff -- rephrased
the comment to avoid the pattern entirely, then re-ran the same grep
to confirm exactly one match remained.

### Workflow file

.github/workflows/autotag.yml added, matching the referenced commit's
own autotag.yml precisely (checkout with full history, read the
version, skip if the corresponding v-prefixed tag already exists,
otherwise create and push it) -- the other four workflow files in
that same commit (buildbot.yml, compile.yml, pylint.yml, ruff.yml)
were not part of this request and were not added. Triggers on push to
"main" specifically, matching the referenced example and GitHub's own
current default branch name -- worth the user's own attention if the
actual repository ends up using a different default branch name.

### Testing

Confirmed __init__.py's and project.py's own syntax with ast.parse();
the whole src/ directory's own final syntax sweep also clean.
Confirmed the exact grep pattern the workflow uses returns exactly
one match against the real, final __init__.py. Validated autotag.yml
itself parses as well-formed YAML (noting, without treating it as a
problem, that a generic YAML 1.1 parser reads the bare "on:" key as
the boolean true rather than the trigger keyword -- a well-known,
harmless quirk GitHub's own workflow parser handles correctly
regardless, and the same structure the referenced original commit
itself uses unmodified).

### Delivery

Both .zip and .ipk built and delivered together. This is 1.1.000
with the autotag workflow added -- no functional/runtime change to
the plugin itself, so the version number is not bumped again for
this round.
